\pi_{\ell}(\sin^{2}\theta f)=\sum_{m=-\ell}^{\ell}\left[\sum_{\ell^{\prime}=|m|}^{\infty}f_{\ell^{\prime},m}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\sin^{2}\theta Y_{\ell^{\prime},m}\overline{Y}_{\ell,m}\,\sin\theta d\theta d\varphi\right]Y_{\ell,m}.

Note that for given
\ell
and
\ell^{\prime}
, the integral

\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\sin^{2}\theta Y_{\ell^{\prime},m}\overline{Y}_{\ell,m}\,\sin\theta d\theta d\varphi=0

if and only if

\int_{-1}^{1}(1-x^{2})P^{m}_{\ell^{\prime}}(x)P^{m}_{\ell}(x)\,dx=0,

(2.14)

where
P^{m}_{\ell}
and
P^{m}_{\ell^{\prime}}
denote associated Legendre polynomials. By the definition of Legendre polynomials, it follows immediately that the above integral vanishes if
\ell^{\prime}-\ell
is odd and is non-vanishing when
\ell=\ell^{\prime}
. It remains to consider the cases when
\ell^{\prime}-\ell
is even and non-zero. Without loss of generality, we can assume that
0\leq m\leq\ell^{\prime}
and
\ell\geq\ell^{\prime}+2
.

We use the following standard recursive relations between associated Legendre polynomials (see for example Chapter 8 of
[
AS72
]
): let
l\neq 0
, then

\displaystyle\sqrt{1-x^{2}}P_{l}^{m}=

\displaystyle-(2l+1)^{-1}[(l-m+1)(l-m+2)P^{m-1}_{l+1}-(l+m-1)(l+m)P^{m-1}_{l-1}]\quad\textnormal{for $m\geq 1$},

\displaystyle\sqrt{1-x^{2}}P_{l}^{m}=

\displaystyle-(2l+1)^{-1}[P^{m+1}_{l+1}-P^{m+1}_{l-1}]\quad\textnormal{for $m\leq l-2$}.

to write

\begin{split}(2\ell+1)(2\ell^{\prime}+1)(1-x^{2})P^{m}_{\ell^{\prime}}P^{m}_{\ell}=&\>\left[(\ell-m+1)(\ell-m+2)P^{m-1}_{\ell+1}-(\ell+m-1)(\ell+m)P^{m-1}_{\ell-1}\right]\\
\cdot&\left[(\ell^{\prime}-m+1)(\ell^{\prime}-m+2)P^{m-1}_{\ell^{\prime}+1}-(\ell^{\prime}+m-1)(\ell^{\prime}+m)P^{m-1}_{\ell^{\prime}-1}\right],\end{split}

when
\ell^{\prime}\neq 0
and
m\geq 1
. For
\ell^{\prime}\neq 0
and
m=0
, we obtain instead

(2\ell+1)(2\ell^{\prime}+1)(1-x^{2})P^{0}_{\ell^{\prime}}P^{0}_{\ell}=[P^{1}_{\ell+1}-P^{1}_{\ell-1}][P^{1}_{\ell^{\prime}+1}-P^{1}_{\ell^{\prime}-1}].

The integral (
2.14
) corresponding to the above cases is non-vanishing if and only if
\ell=\ell^{\prime}
or
|\ell-\ell^{\prime}|=2
.

It only remains to consider the case:
\ell^{\prime}=m=0
and
\ell\geq 2
, for which it follows by integrating by parts that

\begin{split}\int_{-1}^{1}(1-x^{2})P^{0}_{\ell}P^{0}_{0}\,dx=2^{-\ell}(\ell!)^{-1}\int_{-1}^{1}(1-x^{2})\frac{d^{\ell}}{dx^{\ell}}((x^{2}-1)^{\ell})\,dx\end{split}

is non-vanishing if and only if
\ell=2
. The expression (
2.12
) then follows and (
2.13
) can easily be computed explicitly by keeping track of the appropriate normalization constants.
∎

In the lemma below, we state various Poincaré-type inequalities on
\mathbb{S}^{2}
.

Lemma 2.5
.

Let
f\in H^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{2})
. Then:

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}f_{\geq\ell}|^{2}\,d\omega\geq

\displaystyle\>\ell(\ell+1)\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}f_{\geq\ell}^{2}\,d\omega,

(2.15)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}f_{\ell}|^{2}\,d\omega=

\displaystyle\>\ell(\ell+1)\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}f_{\ell}^{2}\,d\omega,

(2.16)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}f_{\ell}^{2}+|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}f_{\ell}|^{2}+|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{2}f_{\ell}|^{2}\,d\omega\leq

\displaystyle\>(1+\ell(\ell+1)+\ell^{2}(\ell+1)^{2})\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}f_{\ell}^{2}\,d\omega.

(2.17)

Furthermore, let
N\in\mathbb{N}
and assume that
f\in H^{\max\{N,2\}}(\mathbb{S}^{2})
. Then there exists constants
0<c<C
, depending on
N
, such that

c\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{s}\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{\frac{N-s}{2}}f|^{2}\,d\omega\leq\sum_{n=0}^{N}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{n}f|^{2}\,d\omega\leq C\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{s}\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{\frac{N-s}{2}}f|^{2}\,d\omega,

(2.18)

where
s=0
if
N
is even and
s=1
if
N
is odd.

Proof.

The inequalities (
2.15
)–(
2.17
) follow by decomposing
f_{\geq\ell}=\sum_{\ell^{\prime}=\ell}^{\infty}f_{\ell^{\prime}}
, using that
\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}f_{\ell}=-\ell(\ell+1)f_{\ell}
and integrating by parts on
\mathbb{S}^{2}
.

We obtain (
2.18
) by integrating by parts with respect to covariant derivation on
\mathbb{S}^{2}
.
∎

2.7
Hardy inequalities along hyperboloidal hypersurfaces

Throughout this paper we will frequently appeal to Hardy inequalities, in the form of the estimates in Lemma
2.6
below, to estimate lower-order weighted derivatives in terms of higher-order weighted derivatives.

Lemma 2.6
.

Let
0\leq a<b
and let
f\in C^{1}([a,b])
, then for
p\in\mathbb{R}\setminus\{-1\}
:

\displaystyle\int_{a}^{b}x^{p}f^{2}(x)\,dx\leq

\displaystyle\>4(p+1)^{-2}\int_{a}^{b}x^{p+2}\left(\frac{df}{dx}\right)^{2}(x)\,dx+2(p+1)^{-1}\left[(x^{p+1}f^{2})(b)-(x^{p+1}f^{2})(a)\right].

(2.19)

Let
r_{+}\leq r_{1}<r_{2}
and let
f\in C^{1}(\Sigma_{\tau})
, then for
p\in\mathbb{R}\setminus\{-1\}
:

\begin{split}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r_{1}\leq\uprho\leq r_{2}\}}r^{p}f^{2}\,d\omega d\uprho\leq&\>4(p+1)^{-2}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r_{1}\leq\uprho\leq r_{2}\}}r^{p+2}(Xf)^{2}\,d\omega d\uprho\\
&+2(p+1)^{-1}\left[\int_{S^{2}_{\tau,r_{2}}}r_{2}^{p+1}f^{2}\,d\omega-\int_{S^{2}_{\tau,r_{1}}}r_{1}^{p+1}f^{2}\,d\omega\right].\end{split}

(2.20)

And for
r_{+}<r_{1}<r_{2}
and
f\in C^{1}(\mathcal{R})
, there exists a constant
C=C(M,a,r_{1})>0
such that

\begin{split}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r_{1}\leq\uprho\leq r_{2}\}}r^{p}f^{2}\,d\omega d\uprho\leq&\>C(p+1)^{-2}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r_{1}\leq\uprho\leq r_{2}\}}r^{p+2}(Lf)^{2}+r^{p-2}(Tf)^{2}+r^{p-2}(\Phi f)^{2}\,d\omega d\uprho\\
&+2(p+1)^{-1}\left[\int_{S^{2}_{\tau,r_{2}}}r_{2}^{p+1}f^{2}\,d\omega-\int_{S^{2}_{\tau,r_{1}}}r_{1}^{p+1}f^{2}\,d\omega\right].\end{split}

(2.21)

Proof.

The estimate (
2.19
) follows from integrating
\frac{d}{dx}\left(x^{p+1}f^{2})\right)
and applying the fundamental theorem of calculus and then immediately implies (
2.20
). We conclude (
2.21
) from (
2.20
) combined with the following relation between
L
and
X
:

L=\frac{\Delta}{2(r^{2}+a^{2})}X+O(r^{-2})T+O(r^{-2})\Phi.

∎

3
Preliminaries: wave equation

We recall and derive in this section some preliminary estimates for the geometric wave equation on Kerr spacetimes that will form important ingredients for the estimates in subsequent sections.

3.1
Main equations

In the lemma below we represent the wave equation
\square_{g_{M,a}}\psi=0
in terms of the vector fields
T,Y,\Phi,X,L
and
\underline{L}
.

Lemma 3.1
.

Let
\psi\in C^{\infty}(\mathcal{R}\to\mathbb{C})
be a solution to

\square_{g_{M,a}}\psi:=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\det g_{M,a}}}\partial_{\alpha}\left(\sqrt{\det g_{M,a}}(g_{M,a}^{-1})^{\alpha\beta}\partial_{\beta}\psi\right)=0.

(3.1)

Then
\psi
satisfies the following equation:

0=\>a^{2}\sin^{2}\theta T^{2}\psi+2(r^{2}+a^{2})TY\psi+Y(\Delta Y\psi)+2aT\Phi\psi+2aY\Phi\psi+2rT\psi+\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\psi.

(3.2)

We can reformulate (
3.2
) as follows:

\begin{split}X(\Delta X\psi)+2aX\Phi\psi+\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\psi=&\>2[h\Delta-(r^{2}+a^{2})]XT\psi+[(\Delta h)^{\prime}-2r]T\psi\\
&+[2h(r^{2}+a^{2})-h^{2}\Delta-a^{2}\sin^{2}\theta]T^{2}\psi+2a(h-1)T\Phi\psi.\end{split}

(3.3)

Furthermore, the rescaled quantity
\phi:=\sqrt{r^{2}+a^{2}}\psi
satisfies the equations:

\displaystyle 0=

\displaystyle\>a^{2}\sin^{2}\theta T^{2}\phi+2aT\Phi\phi+2aY\Phi\phi+2(r^{2}+a^{2})TY\phi+(r^{2}+a^{2})Y\left(\frac{\Delta}{r^{2}+a^{2}}Y\phi\right)+\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\phi

(3.4)

\displaystyle-2a\frac{r}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\Phi\phi-\sqrt{r^{2}+a^{2}}\frac{d}{dr}(r(r^{2}+a^{2})^{-3/2}\Delta)\phi,

\displaystyle 4\underline{L}L\phi=

\displaystyle\>a^{2}\sin^{2}\theta\frac{\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}T^{2}\phi+2a\frac{\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}T\Phi\phi+\frac{\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\phi+2a\frac{r\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{3}}\Phi\phi

(3.5)

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2r}\frac{d}{dr}\left(r^{2}\Delta^{2}(r^{2}+a^{2})^{-3}\right)\phi.

Proof.

The above identities follow from straightforward computations that use the expressions for
g_{M,a}
and
g_{M,a}^{-1}
in (
2.1
) and (
2.2
), respectively, and the definitions of the vector fields
T,\Phi,Y,X,L,\underline{L}
in Section
2.2
.
∎

By rewriting (
3.3
) in terms of the conformal coordinates
(\tau,x,\theta,\varphi_{*})
, we obtain an equation for
\phi
(defined in the statement of Lemma
3.1
) with smooth coefficients with respect to the differentiable structure on
\widehat{\mathcal{R}}
:

Corollary 3.2
.

Let
\psi\in C^{\infty}(\mathcal{R}\to\mathbb{C})
be a solution to (
3.1
). Then
\phi
satisfies the following equation in
(\tau,x,\theta,\varphi_{*})
coordinates:

\begin{split}0=&\>(1+a^{2}x^{2})\partial_{x}\left(\frac{\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})}x^{2}\partial_{x}\phi\right)+2[1+a^{2}x^{2}-x^{2}\hat{h}(x)]\partial_{\tau}\partial_{x}\phi+\left[-\frac{2(r^{2}+a^{2})-\hat{h}}{\Delta}\hat{h}+a^{2}\sin^{2}\theta\right]\partial_{\tau}^{2}\phi+\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\phi\\
&+2a\left[1-\frac{2(r^{2}+a^{2})-\hat{h}}{\Delta}\right]\partial_{\tau}\partial_{\varphi_{*}}\phi-2ax^{2}\partial_{x}\partial_{\varphi_{*}}\phi-(1+a^{2}x^{2})\frac{d}{dx}\left(\frac{\hat{h}x^{2}}{1+a^{2}x^{2}}\right)\partial_{\tau}\phi-\frac{2ax}{1+a^{2}x^{2}}\partial_{\varphi_{*}}\phi\\
&+(1+a^{2}x^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}\frac{d}{dx}\left(\frac{(1-2Mx+a^{2})}{(1+a^{2}x^{2})^{\frac{3}{2}}}\right)\phi.\end{split}

(3.6)

Remark 3.1
.

When viewed as a function on the extended manifold
\widehat{\mathcal{R}}
, the restriction
\phi|_{\mathcal{I}^{+}}
is known as
the (Friedlander) radiation field
.

We introduce the following higher-order quantities in
\{r>r_{+}\}
:

\phi^{(n)}=(2(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}\Delta^{-1}L)^{n}\phi.

Observe that we can alternatively express
\phi^{(n)}=(-1)^{n}(\partial_{x})^{n}\phi
in
(u,x,\theta,\varphi_{*})
coordinates, so
\phi^{(n)}
may be considered natural higher-order analogues of
\phi
from the point of view of the conformal spacetime
(\widehat{\mathcal{R}},\hat{g}_{M,a})
.

Proposition 3.3
.

Let
\psi\in C^{\infty}(\mathcal{R}\to\mathbb{C})
be a solution to (
3.1
). Then
\phi^{(n)}
satisfies the following equation

\begin{split}4\underline{L}L\phi^{(n)}=&\>a^{2}\sin^{2}\theta\frac{\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}T^{2}\phi^{(n)}+2a\frac{\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}T\Phi\phi^{(n)}+2(1+2n)a\frac{r\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{3}}\Phi\phi^{(n)}\\
&+\Delta(r^{2}+a^{2})^{-2}\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\phi^{(n)}+\Delta(r^{2}+a^{2})^{-2}[n(n+1)+O_{\infty}(r^{-1})]\phi^{(n)}-[4nr^{-1}+O_{\infty}(r^{-2})]L\phi^{(n)}\\
&+a\sum_{k=0}^{n-1}O_{\infty}(r^{-2})\Phi\phi^{(k)}+n\sum_{k=0}^{n-1}O_{\infty}(r^{-2})\phi^{(k)}.\end{split}

(3.7)

Proof.

We will prove (
3.7
) by induction. First, note that the
n=0
case follows from (
3.5
). Now suppose (
3.7
) holds for some
n\in\mathbb{N}
. Then

\begin{split}4\underline{L}L\phi^{(n+1)}=&\>4L\left(\underline{L}\left(2\Delta^{-1}(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}L\phi^{(n)}\right)\right)+4[\underline{L},L]\phi^{(n+1)}\\
=&\>8L\left(\Delta^{-1}(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}\underline{L}L\phi^{(n)}\right)-4L\left(\frac{d}{dr}(\Delta^{-1}(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2})\Delta(r^{2}+a^{2})^{-1}L\phi^{(n)}\right)\\
&+4\frac{ar\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{3}}\Phi\phi^{(n+1)}.\end{split}

By applying (
3.7
), we obtain

\begin{split}8L\left(\Delta^{-1}(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}\underline{L}L\phi^{(n)}\right)=&\>a^{2}\sin^{2}\theta\frac{\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}T^{2}\phi^{(n+1)}+2a\frac{\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}T\Phi\phi^{(n+1)}+\Delta(r^{2}+a^{2})^{-2}\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\phi^{(n+1)}\\
&+4(1-2n)a\frac{r\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\Phi\phi^{(n+1)}+\Delta(r^{2}+a^{2})^{-2}[n(n+1)+O_{\infty}(r^{-1})]\phi^{(n+1)}\\
&-[4nr^{-1}+O_{\infty}(r^{-2})]L\phi^{(n+1)}\\
&+a\sum_{k=0}^{n}O_{\infty}(r^{-2})\Phi\phi^{(k)}+(n+1)\sum_{k=0}^{n}O_{\infty}(r^{-2})\phi^{(k)}.\end{split}

Furthermore,

\begin{split}-4L\left(\frac{d}{dr}(\Delta^{-1}(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2})\Delta(r^{2}+a^{2})^{-1}L\phi^{(n)}\right)=&\>[-4r^{-1}+O_{\infty}(r^{-2})]L\phi^{(n+1)}+[2r^{-2}+O_{\infty}(r^{-3})]\phi^{(n)}\end{split}

By combining the above equations, we arrive at (
3.7
) with
n
replaced by
n+1
, which concludes the induction argument.
∎

3.2
Propagation of regularity and limits at null infinity

It will be convenient to appeal to a propagation of regularity result for the wave equation (
3.1
) with respect to the differentiable structure on both
\mathcal{R}
and
\widehat{\mathcal{R}}
.

Proposition 3.4
.

Let
k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
.

(i)

Let
(\Psi,\Psi^{\prime})\in H^{k+1}_{\rm loc}(\Sigma_{0})\times H^{k}_{\rm loc}(\Sigma_{0})
. Then there exists a solution
\psi
to (
3.1
) (in the distributional sense), such that for all
T\geq 0
:

\displaystyle\psi\in

\displaystyle\>C^{0}([0,T],H^{k+1}_{\rm loc}(\Sigma_{0}))\cap C^{1}([0,T],H^{k}_{\rm loc}(\Sigma_{0})),

\displaystyle\psi|_{\Sigma_{0}}=

\displaystyle\>\Psi,

\displaystyle T\psi|_{\Sigma_{0}}=

\displaystyle\>\Psi^{\prime}.

(ii)

Assume moreover that
(\sqrt{r^{2}+a^{2}}\Psi,\sqrt{r^{2}+a^{2}}\Psi^{\prime})\in H^{k+1}(\widehat{\Sigma}_{0})\times H^{k}(\widehat{\Sigma}_{0})
. Then, for all
\tau\geq 0
,

(\phi|_{\Sigma_{\tau}},T\phi|_{\Sigma_{\tau}})\in H^{k+1}(\widehat{\Sigma}_{\tau})\times H^{k}(\widehat{\Sigma}_{\tau}).

(3.8)

(iii)

Let
(\Psi,\Psi^{\prime})\in C^{\infty}(\Sigma_{0})\times C^{\infty}(\Sigma_{0})
. Assume moreover that

(\sqrt{r^{2}+a^{2}}\Psi,\sqrt{r^{2}+a^{2}}\Psi^{\prime})\in H^{N+1}(\widehat{\Sigma}_{0})\times H^{N}(\widehat{\Sigma}_{0})

for some
N\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
. Then for all
\tau\geq 0
and
k,m,n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, such that
k+m+n\leq N
,

\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{k}\partial_{\tau}^{m}\phi^{(n)}|^{2}(\tau,\uprho,\theta,\varphi)\,d\omega

attains a finite limit as
\uprho\to\infty
.

Proof.

Part (i) follows from standard result for linear wave equations, see for example
[
Sog08
]
. In order to obtain part (ii), we consider (
3.6
) and observe that the coefficients in the equation are smooth functions of
(x,\theta)
(note in particular the smoothness in
r
.) Therefore, (
3.8
) follows from standard (finite-in-time) higher-order energy estimates with respect to
(\tau,x,\theta,\varphi_{*})
coordinates.

By the fundamental theorem of calculus it follows that for
0<x_{0}\leq r_{+}^{-1}
and
x\leq x_{0}

\begin{split}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}&|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{k_{1}}\partial_{\tau}^{k_{2}}\partial_{x}^{k_{3}}\phi|^{2}(\tau,x,\theta,\varphi)\,d\omega=\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{k_{1}}\partial_{\tau}^{k_{2}}\partial_{x}^{k_{3}}\phi|^{2}(\tau,x_{0},\theta,\varphi)\,d\omega\\
&-2\int_{x}^{x_{0}}\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{k_{1}}\partial_{\tau}^{k_{2}}\partial_{x}^{k_{3}}\phi\cdot\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{k_{1}}\partial_{\tau}^{k_{2}}\partial_{x}^{k_{3}+1}\phi(\tau,x^{\prime},\theta,\varphi)\,d\omega dx^{\prime}.\end{split}

Observe that the first integral on the right-hand side is finite by smoothness of
\psi
with respect to the differentiable structure on
\mathcal{R}
, which in turn follows from the smoothness of
\Psi
and
\Psi^{\prime}
, together with a standard propagation of regularity argument; see again
[
Sog08
]
.

Then, after applying Cauchy–Schwarz to the second term on the right-hand side of the equation above, together with (
3.8
) and (
3.6
), it follows that the limit of the left-hand side at
x=0
is well-defined provided
k_{1}+k_{2}\leq N
. Since
\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{k}\partial_{\tau}^{m}\phi^{(n)}
, with
k+m+n\leq N
, can be written as a linear combination of
\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{k_{1}}\partial_{\tau}^{k_{2}}\partial_{x}^{k_{3}}\phi
with
k_{1}+k_{2}+k_{3}\leq N
, we obtain part (iii).
∎

Throughout the remainder of the paper we will assume for the sake of convenience that the initial data for (
3.1
) satisfy:

(\sqrt{r^{2}+a^{2}}\Psi,\sqrt{r^{2}+a^{2}}\Psi^{\prime})\in C^{\infty}(\widehat{\Sigma}_{0})\times C^{\infty}(\widehat{\Sigma}_{0}),

though this assumption can be significantly weakened by a standard density argument with respect to the (weighted) energy norms in the relevant estimates.

3.3
Energy boundedness and integrated local energy decay

Two key ingredients towards deriving the results in the present article are
energy boundedness
and
integrated local energy decay
. In the context of subextremal Kerr spacetimes with
|a|<M
, these were both derived in
[
DRSR16
]
. We summarize the relevant results as Theorem
A
below.

Theorem A

(Dafermos–Rodnianski–Shlapentokh-Rothman,
[
DRSR16
]
)
.

Let
\psi
be a solution to (
3.1
) arising from suitably regular initial data with respect to
\widehat{\mathcal{R}}
. Then the following estimates hold:

(i)

There exists a constant
C=C(M,a)>0
, such that for any
\tau_{2}\geq\tau_{1}\geq 0
:

\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{2}}}\mathbf{J}^{N}[\psi]\cdot\mathbf{n}_{\tau_{2}}\,r^{2}d\omega d\uprho\leq C\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{1}}}\mathbf{J}^{N}[\psi]\cdot\mathbf{n}_{\tau_{1}}\,r^{2}d\omega d\uprho.

(3.9)

(ii)

Let
n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
. For
R_{0}>0
suitably large and
R_{1}>0
, there exists a constant
C=C(n,R_{0},R_{1},M,a)>0
, such that for any
\tau_{2}\geq\tau_{1}\geq 0
:

\sum_{0\leq k_{1}+k_{2}+k_{3}\leq n}\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\left[\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{R_{0}\leq r\leq R_{1}\}}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{k_{1}}T^{k_{2}}Y^{k_{3}}\psi|^{2}d\omega d\uprho\right]\,d\tau\leq C\sum_{m\leq n}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{1}}}\mathbf{J}^{N}[T^{m}\psi]\cdot\mathbf{n}_{\tau_{1}}\,r^{2}d\omega d\uprho.

(3.10)

Furthermore, for all
\delta>0
, there exists
C=C(n,M,a)>0
, such that

\int_{D^{\tau_{2}}_{\tau_{1}}}r^{-1-\delta}\left[(\underline{L}\phi)^{2}+(L\phi)^{2}+r^{-2}\phi^{2}+r^{-2}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\phi|^{2}\right]\,d\omega d\rho d\tau\leq C\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{1}}}\mathbf{J}^{N}[\psi]\cdot\mathbf{n}_{\tau_{1}}\,r^{2}d\omega d\uprho.

(3.11)

(iii)

Let
n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
. For
R_{0}>0
arbitrarily large, there exists a constant
C=C(n,R_{0},M,a)>0
, such that for any
\tau_{2}\geq\tau_{1}\geq 0
:

\sum_{0\leq k_{1}+k_{2}+k_{3}\leq n}\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\left[\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\leq R_{0}\}}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{k_{1}}T^{k_{2}}Y^{k_{3}}\psi|^{2}d\omega d\uprho\right]\,d\tau\leq C\sum_{k_{1}+k_{2}\leq n+1}\int_{\Sigma_{0}}\mathbf{J}^{N}[T^{k_{1}}\Phi^{k_{2}}\psi]\cdot\mathbf{n}_{\tau}\,r^{2}d\omega d\uprho.

(3.12)

We note moreover that by definition of
\Sigma_{\tau}
in Section
2.3
, and the choice
h_{0}\neq 0
in particular, we can estimate

\mathbf{J}^{N}[\psi]\cdot\mathbf{n}_{\tau}\sim(X\psi)^{2}+r^{-2}(T\psi)^{2}+r^{-2}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\psi|^{2}.

It follows immediately by combining (
3.9
) and (
3.10
) and applying (
2.20
) with
p=0
that for all
0\leq\tau_{1}\leq\tau_{2}
:

\int_{N_{\tau_{2}}}(L\phi)^{2}+r^{-2}(\underline{L}\phi)^{2}+r^{-2}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\phi|^{2}\,d\omega d\rho+\int_{I_{s}\cap D^{\tau_{2}}_{\tau_{1}}}(\underline{L}\phi)^{2}+r^{-2}(L\phi)^{2}+r^{-2}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\phi|^{2}\,d\omega d\rho\leq C\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{1}}}\mathbf{J}^{N}[\psi]\cdot\mathbf{n}_{\tau_{1}}\,r^{2}d\omega d\uprho.

(3.13)

In order to obtain more refined energy decay estimates for the projections
\psi_{\geq\ell}
, we need additional integrated local energy decay estimates for
\psi_{\geq\ell}
.

Proposition 3.5
.

Let
\psi
be a solution to (
3.1
) arising from suitably regular initial data with respect to
\widehat{\mathcal{R}}
. Then the following estimates hold for
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
:

(i)

For
R_{0}>2M
suitably large, there exists a constant
C=C(M,a,R_{0})>0
, such that for any
\tau_{2}\geq\tau_{1}\geq 0
:

\begin{split}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{2}}}&J^{N}[\psi_{\geq\ell}]\cdot\mathbf{n}_{\tau_{2}}\,r^{2}d\omega d\uprho\\
&+\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\infty}\left[\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq R_{0}\}}M^{\delta}r^{1-\delta}[(T\psi_{\geq\ell})^{2}+(Y\psi_{\geq\ell})^{2}]+r^{-1}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\psi_{\geq\ell}|^{2}+M^{\delta}r^{-1-\delta}\psi_{\geq\ell}^{2}\,d\omega d\uprho\right]d\tau\\
\leq&\>Ca^{2}\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\infty}\left[\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}M^{-\delta}r^{-1+\delta}\left[(T^{2}\psi_{\geq\max\{\ell-2,0\}})^{2}+M^{2}(T^{3}\psi_{\geq\max\{\ell-2,0\}})^{2}+(T^{2}\Phi\psi_{\geq\max\{\ell-2,0\}})^{2}\right]\,d\omega d\uprho\right]d\tau\\
&+C\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{1}}}\left[\mathbf{J}^{N}[\psi_{\geq\ell}]\cdot\mathbf{n}_{\tau_{1}}+a^{2}\mathbf{J}^{N}[T^{2}\psi_{\geq\max\{0,\ell-2\}}]\cdot\mathbf{n}_{\tau_{1}}\right]\,r^{2}d\omega d\uprho.\end{split}

(3.14)

(ii)

There exists a constant
C=C(M,a)>0
, such that for any
\tau_{2}\geq\tau_{1}\geq 0
:

\begin{split}\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\infty}&\left[\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}M^{\delta}r^{1-\delta}[(T\psi_{\geq\ell})^{2}+(Y\psi_{\geq\ell})^{2}]+r^{-1}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\psi_{\geq\ell}|^{2}+M^{\delta}r^{-1-\delta}\psi_{\geq\ell}^{2}\,d\omega d\uprho\right]d\tau\\
\leq&\>Ca^{2}\sum_{k_{1}+k_{2}\leq 2}\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\left[\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}M^{-\delta}r^{-1+\delta}(T^{2+k_{1}}\Phi^{k_{2}}\psi_{\geq\max\{\ell-2,0\}})^{2}\,d\omega d\uprho\right]d\tau\\
&+C\sum_{k_{1}+k_{2}\leq 1}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{1}}}M^{k_{1}}\mathbf{J}^{N}[T^{k_{1}}\Phi^{k_{2}}\psi_{\geq\ell}]\cdot\mathbf{n}_{\tau_{1}}+a^{2}M^{k_{1}}\mathbf{J}^{N}[T^{2+k_{1}}\Phi^{k_{2}}\psi_{\geq\max\{0,\ell-2\}}]\cdot\mathbf{n}_{\tau_{1}}\,r^{2}d\omega d\uprho.\end{split}

(3.15)

(iii)

Let
n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
. For
R_{0}>2M
suitably large and
R_{1}>R_{0}
, there exists a constant
C=C(M,a,n,R_{0},R_{1})>0
, such that for any
\tau_{1}\geq 0
:

\begin{split}&\sum_{0\leq k_{1}+k_{2}+k_{3}\leq n}\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\infty}\left[\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{R_{0}\leq r\leq R_{1}\}}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{k_{1}}T^{k_{2}}Y^{k_{3}}\psi_{\geq\ell}|^{2}d\omega d\uprho\right]\,d\tau\\
\leq&\>C\sum_{k=0}^{n}\left[\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{1}}}J^{N}[T^{k}\psi_{\geq\ell}]\cdot\mathbf{n}_{\tau_{1}}\,r^{2}d\omega d\uprho+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq l\leq\lceil\ell/2\rceil\\
0\leq l_{1}+l_{2}\leq l\end{subarray}}a^{2}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{1}}}J^{N}[T^{k+2l}T^{l_{1}}\Phi^{l_{2}}\psi_{\geq\max\{\ell-2l,0\}}\cdot\mathbf{n}_{\tau_{1}}\,r^{2}d\omega d\uprho\right].\end{split}

(3.16)

Proof.

Let
\psi
be a solution to (
3.1
) arising from suitably regular initial data with respect to
\widehat{\mathcal{R}}
. Then
\psi_{\geq\ell}
satisfies the following inhomogeneous equation:

\rho^{2}\square_{g}\psi_{\geq\ell}=a^{2}T^{2}[\pi_{\geq\ell},\sin^{2}\theta]\psi=:F_{\geq\ell}.

We can generalize the Morawetz estimate in Proposition 9.1.1 of
[
DRSR16
]
to inhomogeneous wave equations where the inhomogeneity
F_{\geq\ell}
is given by the above expression and we consider a hyperboloidal foliation by
\Sigma_{\tau}
rather than an asymptotically flat foliation; see also Proposition 9.8.1 of
[
DRSR16
]
. We obtain the following estimate after assuming without loss of generality that
M=1
:

\begin{split}\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\infty}&\left[\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}r^{1-\delta}[\zeta(T\psi_{\geq\ell})^{2}+(Y\psi_{\geq\ell})^{2}]+r^{-1}\zeta|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\psi_{\geq\ell}|^{2}+r^{-1-\delta}\psi_{\geq\ell}^{2}\,d\omega d\uprho\right]d\tau\\
\leq&\>C\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\infty}\left[\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}O_{\infty}(r^{0})F_{\geq\ell}\cdot(T\psi_{\geq\ell}+\Phi\psi_{\geq\ell}+Y\psi_{\geq\ell}+r^{-1}\psi_{\geq\ell})\,d\omega d\uprho\right]d\tau+C\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{1}}}\mathbf{J}^{N}[\psi_{\geq\ell}]\cdot\mathbf{n}_{0}\,r^{2}d\omega d\uprho,\end{split}

with
\delta>0
and
\zeta
a smooth cut-off function that vanishes in an interval
[-s_{1,3M}+3M,3M+s_{2,3M}]
, with
-s_{1,3M}+3M>r_{+}
and
s_{2,3M}<\infty
depending on
a
and
M
.

After integrating by parts in
T
and taking
\epsilon>0
arbitrarily small, we can further estimate:

\begin{split}\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\infty}&\left[\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}O_{\infty}(r^{0})F_{\geq\ell}\cdot(T\psi_{\geq\ell}+\Phi\psi_{\geq\ell}+Y\psi+\psi_{\geq\ell})\,d\omega d\uprho\right]d\tau\\
\leq&\>\epsilon\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\infty}\left[\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}r^{1-\delta}[\zeta(T\psi_{\geq\ell})^{2}+(Y\psi_{\geq\ell})^{2}]+r^{-1}\zeta|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\psi_{\geq\ell}|^{2}+r^{-1-\delta}\psi_{\geq\ell}^{2}\,d\omega d\uprho\right]d\tau\\
&+C\epsilon^{-1}\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\infty}\left[\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}r^{-1+\delta}F_{\geq\ell}^{2}\,d\omega d\uprho\right]d\tau\\
&+C\epsilon^{-1}\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\infty}\left[\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{-s_{3,3M}+3M\leq r\leq 3M+s_{4,3M}\}}O_{\infty}(r^{0})(TF_{\geq\ell}+\Phi F_{\geq\ell})\cdot\psi_{\geq\ell}\,d\omega d\uprho\right]d\tau\\
&+C\epsilon^{-1}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{1}}\cap\{-s_{3,3M}+3M\leq r\leq 3M+s_{4,3M}\}}|F_{\geq\ell}||\psi_{\geq\ell}|\,d\omega d\uprho,\end{split}

with suitable
s_{1,3M}<s_{3,3M}<(3M-r_{+})
and
s_{2,3M}<s_{4,3M}<\infty
. We have that

\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}F_{\geq\ell}^{2}\,d\omega\leq C\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}(T^{2}\psi_{\geq\ell-2})^{2}\,d\omega.

We obtain (
3.14
) by combining the above estimates. The estimate (
3.15
) follows by additionally applying the above estimates with
\psi
replaced by
T\psi
or
\Phi\psi
(using that
T
and
\Phi
are Killing vector fields), to remove the degenerate factor
\zeta
on the left-hand side. The estimate (
3.16
) follows from standard higher-order elliptic estimates in the far-away region
2M<R_{0}<r<R_{1}
, where
T
is timelike, together with a repeated application of (
3.14
), using moreover that all estimates apply to
T^{k}\psi
replacing
\psi
.
∎

4
Newman–Penrose charges

In this section we derive conservation laws for weighted derivatives of
\phi
along
\mathcal{I}^{+}
by constructing Newman–Penrose charges in Kerr spacetimes. These Newman–Penrose charges will play a key role in the analysis in the remainder of the paper, both when deriving sharp decay estimates and precise late-time asymptotics for solutions to (
3.1
).

We first introduce the following
renormalized derivatives
of
\phi
:

\displaystyle\check{\phi}^{(1)}:=

\displaystyle\>\left[1+(\alpha+\alpha_{\Phi}\Phi)r^{-1}+(\beta+\beta_{\Phi}\Phi+\beta_{\Phi^{2}}\Phi^{2})r^{-2}\right]\frac{2(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}{\Delta}L\phi,

(4.1)

\displaystyle\check{\phi}^{(2)}:=

\displaystyle\>\left[1+(\gamma+\gamma_{\Phi}\Phi)r^{-1}\right]\frac{2(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}{\Delta}L\check{\phi}^{(1)},

(4.2)

with

\displaystyle\alpha=

\displaystyle\>-M,

\displaystyle\beta=-\frac{1}{3}M^{2}-\frac{1}{6}a^{2},

\displaystyle\gamma=-2M,

\displaystyle\alpha_{\Phi}=

\displaystyle\>a,

\displaystyle\beta_{\Phi}=0,\>\beta_{\Phi^{2}}=\frac{1}{3}a^{2}

\displaystyle\gamma_{\Phi}=0.

It will be useful to consider a decompositions into
azimuthal modes

\psi=\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}(\psi)_{m}
, where
(\psi)_{m}
can be expressed via the following spherical harmonic decomposition:

(\psi)_{m}=\sum_{\ell=|m|}^{\infty}\psi_{\ell,m}Y_{\ell,m}.

Note that
\Phi(\psi)_{m}=im(\psi)_{m}
and the azimuthal modes
(\psi)_{m}
are
decoupled
, i.e. they independently satisfy (
3.1
):
\square_{g_{M,a}}(\psi)_{m}=0
, so it is possible to consider each
m
-th azimuthal mode independently.

We can then split

\phi_{1}=\pi_{1}(\phi)=\sum_{m=-1}^{1}(\phi_{1})_{m}\quad\textnormal{and}\quad\phi_{2}=\pi_{2}(\phi)=\sum_{m=-2}^{2}(\phi_{2})_{m}.

with
\Phi(\phi_{j})_{m}=im(\phi_{j})_{m}
for
j=1,2
. When restricted to fixed azimuthal modes, (
4.1
) and (
4.2
) take the form:

\displaystyle(\check{\phi}^{(1)})_{m}:=

\displaystyle\>\left[1+(\alpha+im\alpha_{\Phi})r^{-1}+(\beta+im\beta_{\Phi}-m^{2}\beta_{\Phi^{2}})r^{-2}\right]\frac{2(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}{\Delta}L(\phi)_{m},

\displaystyle(\check{\phi}^{(2)})_{m}:=

\displaystyle\>\left[1+(\gamma+im\gamma_{\Phi})r^{-1}\right]\frac{2(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}{\Delta}L(\check{\phi}^{(1)})_{m}.

In a slight abuse of notation, when restricting to fixed azimuthal modes with azimuthal number
m
, we will therefore also use
\alpha
,
\beta
and
\gamma
to denote also the complex numbers
\alpha+im\alpha_{\Phi}
,
\beta+im\beta_{\Phi}+m^{2}\beta_{\Phi^{2}}
and
\gamma+im\gamma_{\Phi}
, respectively.

Proposition 4.1
.

Let
\psi\in C^{\infty}(\mathcal{R}\to\mathbb{C})
denote a solution to (
3.1
) that is supported on a fixed azimuthal mode with azimuthal number
m
, i.e. it satisfies
\Phi\psi=im\psi
. Then:

\begin{split}4\frac{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}{\Delta}\underline{L}L\check{\phi}^{(1)}_{1}=&\>4\frac{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}{\Delta}\underline{L}L\pi_{1}(\check{\phi}^{(1)})=\left[a^{2}\pi_{1}(\sin^{2}\theta T^{2}\check{\phi}^{(1)})+2aT\Phi\check{\phi}^{(1)}_{1}\right]\\
&+[-\alpha-\alpha_{\Phi}\Phi+O_{\infty}(r^{-1})][a^{2}\pi_{1}(\sin^{2}\theta T^{2}\phi)+2aT\Phi\phi_{1}]\\
&+[-4r+O_{\infty}(1)]L\check{\phi}^{(1)}_{1}+O_{\infty}(r^{-1})\check{\phi}^{(1)}_{1}+O_{\infty}(r^{-1})\Phi\check{\phi}^{(1)}_{1}+O_{\infty}(r^{-1})\phi_{1}\end{split}

(4.3)

and

\begin{split}4\frac{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}{\Delta}\underline{L}L\check{\phi}^{(2)}_{2}=&\>\left[a^{2}\pi_{2}(\sin^{2}\theta T^{2}\check{\phi}^{(2)})+2aT\Phi\check{\phi}^{(2)}_{2}\right]\\
&+[M-\gamma-a\Phi+O_{\infty}(r^{-1})][a^{2}\pi_{2}(\sin^{2}\theta T^{2}\check{\phi}^{(1)})+2aT\Phi\check{\phi}^{(1)}_{2}]\\
&+[2\beta+2\beta_{\Phi}\Phi+2\beta_{\Phi^{2}}\Phi^{2}+\gamma(a\Phi-M)+O_{\infty}(r^{-1})][a^{2}\pi_{2}(\sin^{2}\theta T^{2}\phi)+2aT\Phi\phi_{2}]\\
&+[-8r+O_{\infty}(r^{0})]L\check{\phi}^{(2)}_{2}+O_{\infty}(r^{-1})\check{\phi}^{(2)}_{2}+O_{\infty}(r^{-1})\Phi\check{\phi}^{(2)}_{2}\\
&+O_{\infty}(r^{-1})\Phi\check{\phi}^{(1)}_{2}+O_{\infty}(r^{-1})\check{\phi}^{(1)}_{2}+O_{\infty}(r^{-1})\Phi\phi_{2}+O_{\infty}(r^{-1})\phi_{2},\end{split}

(4.4)

where we allow the constants in the terms in
O_{\infty}(r^{-k})
,
k=0,1
, to depend also on
m
.

Proof.

We have that

\check{\phi}^{(1)}=(1+\alpha r^{-1}+\beta r^{-2})\frac{2(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}{\Delta}L\phi,

and therefore

\begin{split}4\underline{L}\check{\phi}^{(1)}=&\>\overbrace{2\underline{L}\left((1+\alpha r^{-1}+\beta r^{-2})\frac{2(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}{\Delta}\right)(1+\alpha r^{-1}+\beta r^{-2})^{-1}\frac{\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\check{\phi}^{(1)}}^{=:T_{1}}\\
&+\overbrace{(1+\alpha r^{-1}+\beta r^{-2})\frac{2(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}{\Delta}4\underline{L}L\phi}^{=:T_{2}}.\end{split}

We can write

T_{1}=\frac{\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}[-4r+2(\alpha+2M)+O_{\infty}(r^{-1})]\check{\phi}^{(1)},

where we allow the terms in
O_{\infty}(r^{-1})
to depend on
\alpha
and
\beta
.

Hence,

L(T_{1})=\frac{\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}[-4r+2(\alpha+2M)+O_{\infty}(r^{-1})]L\check{\phi}^{(1)}+\frac{\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\left[2-(2\alpha+12M)r^{-1}+O_{\infty}(r^{-2})\right]\check{\phi}^{(1)}.

Furthermore, we can rewrite (
3.5
) as follows:

\begin{split}4\underline{L}L\phi=&\>\frac{\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\left[a^{2}\sin^{2}\theta T^{2}\phi+2aT\Phi\phi+\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\phi+2a\frac{r}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\Phi\phi\right]\\
&+\frac{\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}[-2Mr^{-1}-a^{2}r^{-2}+O_{\infty}(r^{-3})]\phi.\end{split}

Therefore,

T_{2}=2(1+\alpha r^{-1}+\beta r^{-2})\left[a^{2}\sin^{2}\theta T^{2}\phi+2aT\Phi\phi+\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\phi+2a\frac{r}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\Phi\phi+(-2Mr^{-1}-a^{2}r^{-2}+O_{\infty}(r^{-3}))\phi\right].

We then obtain

\begin{split}L(T_{2})=&\>\frac{\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\left[a^{2}\sin^{2}\theta T^{2}\check{\phi}^{(1)}+2aT\Phi\check{\phi}^{(1)}+2a\frac{r}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\Phi\check{\phi}^{(1)}+\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\check{\phi}^{(1)}\right]\\
&+\frac{\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}[-2Mr^{-1}-a^{2}r^{-2}+O_{\infty}(r^{-3})]\check{\phi}^{(1)}\\
&+\frac{\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}[-\alpha-2\beta r^{-1}+O_{\infty}(r^{-2})][a^{2}\sin^{2}\theta T^{2}\phi+2aT\Phi\phi+\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\phi]\\
&+\frac{\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}[-2a-4a\alpha r^{-1}+O_{\infty}(r^{-2})]\Phi\phi+\frac{\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\left[2M+(2a^{2}+4\alpha M)r^{-1}+O_{\infty}(r^{-2})\right]\phi\end{split}

Combining the expressions for
L(T_{1})
and
L(T_{2})
and commuting
L
and
\underline{L}
, we obtain:

\begin{split}4\frac{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}{\Delta}\underline{L}L\check{\phi}^{(1)}=&\>\left[a^{2}\sin^{2}\theta T^{2}\check{\phi}^{(1)}+2aT\Phi\check{\phi}^{(1)}+6a\frac{r}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\Phi\check{\phi}^{(1)}\right]\\
&+[-4r+2(\alpha+2M)+O_{\infty}(r^{-1})]L\check{\phi}^{(1)}+\left[2+\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\right]\check{\phi}^{(1)}\\
&+[(-2\alpha-14M)r^{-1}+O_{\infty}(r^{-2})]\check{\phi}^{(1)}\\
&+[-\alpha-2\beta r^{-1}+O_{\infty}(r^{-2})][a^{2}\sin^{2}\theta T^{2}\phi+2aT\Phi\phi+\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\phi]\\
&+[-2a-4a\alpha r^{-1}+O_{\infty}(r^{-2})]\Phi\phi+\left[2M+(2a^{2}+4\alpha M)r^{-1}+O_{\infty}(r^{-2})\right]\phi\end{split}

(4.5)

In order to guarantee that the right-hand side above either contains a
T
-derivative or vanishes when we act with
\pi_{1}
on both sides and take
r\to\infty
, we use that

\alpha=-M+iam.

Now let

\check{\phi}^{(2)}=(1+\gamma r^{-1})\frac{2(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}{\Delta}L\check{\phi}^{(1)},

so

4\underline{L}\check{\phi}^{(2)}=\overbrace{2\underline{L}\left((1+\gamma r^{-1})\frac{2(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}{\Delta}\right)(1+\gamma r^{-1})^{-1}\frac{\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\check{\phi}^{(2)}}^{=:T_{3}}+\overbrace{(1+\gamma r^{-1})\frac{2(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}{\Delta}4\underline{L}L\check{\phi}^{(1)}}^{=:T_{4}}.

Note that

L(T_{3})=\frac{\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}[-4r+O_{\infty}(r^{0})]L\check{\phi}^{(2)}+\frac{\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\left[2+O_{\infty}(r^{-1})\right]\check{\phi}^{(2)},

where the terms in
O_{\infty}(r^{-1})
depend on
\gamma
.

By (
4.5
) with
\alpha=-M+iam
, we have that:

\begin{split}T_{4}=&\>2(1+\gamma r^{-1})[a^{2}\sin^{2}\theta T^{2}\check{\phi}^{(1)}+2aT\Phi\check{\phi}^{(1)}+6ar(r^{2}+a^{2})^{-1}\Phi\check{\phi}^{(1)}]\\
&+\Delta(r^{2}+a^{2})^{-2}[-4r+2(M+iam)+O_{\infty}(r^{-1})]\check{\phi}^{(2)}+2(1+\gamma r^{-1})[2+\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}]\check{\phi}^{(1)}\\
&+2(1+\gamma r^{-1})[(-12M-2iam)r^{-1}+O_{\infty}(r^{-2})]\check{\phi}^{(1)}\\
&+2(1+\gamma r^{-1})[M-iam-2\beta r^{-1}+O_{\infty}(r^{-2})][a^{2}\sin^{2}\theta T^{2}\phi+2aT\Phi\phi+\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\phi]\\
&+2(1+\gamma r^{-1})[-2a+(4Ma-4ima^{2})r^{-1}+O_{\infty}(r^{-2})]\Phi\phi\\
&+2(1+\gamma r^{-1})[2M+(2a^{2}-4M^{2}+4iamM)r^{-1}+O_{\infty}(r^{-2})]\phi\end{split}

and hence,

\begin{split}\frac{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}{\Delta}L(T_{4})=&\>[a^{2}\sin^{2}\theta T^{2}\check{\phi}^{(2)}+2aT\Phi\check{\phi}^{(2)}+6ar(r^{2}+a^{2})^{-1}\Phi\check{\phi}^{(2)}]\\
&+[-4r+O_{\infty}(r^{0})]L\check{\phi}^{(2)}+[2+\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}+O_{\infty}(r^{-1})]\check{\phi}^{(2)}\\
&-[\gamma+O_{\infty}(r^{-1})][a^{2}\sin^{2}\theta T^{2}\check{\phi}^{(1)}+2aT\Phi\check{\phi}^{(1)}]-[6a+O_{\infty}(r^{-1}]\Phi\check{\phi}^{(1)}\\
&+[2+O_{\infty}(r^{-1})]\check{\phi}^{(2)}-[\gamma+O_{\infty}(r^{-1})](2+\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}})\check{\phi}^{(1)}+[12M+2iam+O_{\infty}(r^{-1})]\check{\phi}^{(1)}\\
&+[M-iam+O_{\infty}(r^{-1})](a^{2}\sin^{2}\theta T^{2}\check{\phi}^{(1)}+2aT\Phi\check{\phi}^{(1)}+\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\check{\phi}^{(1)})\\
&+[-2a+O_{\infty}(r^{-1})]\Phi\check{\phi}^{(1)}+[2M+O_{\infty}(r^{-1})]\check{\phi}^{(1)}\\
&+[-\gamma(M-iam)+2\beta+O_{\infty}(r^{-1})](a^{2}\sin^{2}\theta T^{2}\phi+2aT\Phi\phi+\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\phi)\\
&+[2a\gamma-4Ma+4ima^{2}+O_{\infty}(r^{-1})]\Phi\phi\\
&+[-2M\gamma-(2a^{2}-4M^{2}+4iamM)+O_{\infty}(r^{-1})]\phi.\end{split}

Combining the above expression with the expression for
L(T_{3})
, using that
\Phi\phi=im\phi
(and commuting
L
and
\underline{L}
), we obtain

\begin{split}4\frac{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}{\Delta}\underline{L}L\check{\phi}^{(2)}=&\>\left[a^{2}\sin^{2}\theta T^{2}\check{\phi}^{(2)}+2aT\Phi\check{\phi}^{(2)}+10a\frac{r}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\Phi\check{\phi}^{(2)}\right]\\
&+[-8r+O_{\infty}(r^{0})]L\check{\phi}^{(2)}+\left[6+\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\right]\check{\phi}^{(2)}+O_{\infty}(r^{-1})\check{\phi}^{(2)}\\
&+[M-aim-\gamma+O_{\infty}(r^{-1})][a^{2}\sin^{2}\theta T^{2}\check{\phi}^{(1)}+2aT\Phi\check{\phi}^{(1)}]\\
&+[-\gamma(2+\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}})+14M-6iam+(M-iam)\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}+O_{\infty}(r^{-1})]\check{\phi}^{(1)}\\
&+[2\beta-M\gamma+iam\gamma+O_{\infty}(r^{-1})](a^{2}\sin^{2}\theta T^{2}\phi+2aT\Phi\phi)\\
&+[(-M\gamma+iam\gamma+2\beta)\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}-2M\gamma-2a^{2}+4M^{2}-4iamM+2iam\gamma-4iamM\\
&-4m^{2}a^{2}+O_{\infty}(r^{-1})]\phi\end{split}

(4.6)

In order to guarantee that the terms involving
\check{\phi}^{(2)}
and
\check{\phi}^{(1)}
on the right-hand side above either contains a
T
-derivative or vanishes when we act with
\pi_{2}
on both sides and take
r\to\infty
, we use that:

\gamma=-2M.

With the above value of
\gamma
, it follows that if we moreover use that

\beta=-\frac{1}{3}M^{2}-\frac{1}{6}a^{2}-\frac{1}{3}a^{2}m^{2},

then all the terms involving
\phi
on the right-hand side above either contain a
T
-derivative or vanish when we act with
\pi_{2}
on both sides and take
r\to\infty
.
∎

We define the following special linear combinations of derivatives of
\check{\phi}^{(j)}
,
j=0,1,2
:

\displaystyle\frac{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}{\Delta}P_{0}:=

\displaystyle\>\frac{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}{\Delta}L\phi_{0}-\frac{1}{4}a^{2}\pi_{0}(\sin^{2}\theta T\phi),

(4.7)

\displaystyle\frac{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}{\Delta}P_{1}:=

\displaystyle\>\frac{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}{\Delta}L\check{\phi}_{1}^{(1)}-\frac{1}{4}\left[a^{2}\pi_{1}(\sin^{2}\theta T\check{\phi}^{(1)})+2a\Phi\check{\phi}^{(1)}_{1}\right]

(4.8)

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{4}[-\alpha-\alpha_{\Phi}\Phi][a^{2}\pi_{1}(\sin^{2}\theta T\phi)+2a\Phi\phi_{1}],

\displaystyle\frac{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}{\Delta}P_{2}:=

\displaystyle\>\frac{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}{\Delta}L\check{\phi}_{2}^{(2)}-\frac{1}{4}\left[a^{2}\pi_{2}(\sin^{2}\theta T\check{\phi}^{(2)})+2a\Phi\check{\phi}^{(2)}_{2}\right]

(4.9)

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{4}[M-\gamma-a\Phi][a^{2}\pi_{2}(\sin^{2}\theta T\check{\phi}^{(1)})+2a\Phi\check{\phi}^{(1)}_{2}]

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{4}[2\beta+2\beta_{\Phi^{2}}\Phi^{2}+\gamma(a\Phi-M)][a^{2}\pi_{2}(\sin^{2}\theta T\phi)+2a\Phi\phi_{2}].

Corollary 4.2
.

Let
\psi\in C^{\infty}(\mathcal{R}\to\mathbb{C})
denote a solution to (
3.1
) that is supported on a fixed azimuthal mode with azimuthal number
m
, i.e. it satisfies
\Phi\psi=im\psi
. Then:

\displaystyle 4\underline{L}P_{0}=

\displaystyle\>O_{\infty}(r^{-3})\phi_{0}+a^{2}O_{\infty}(r^{-3})T\pi_{0}(\sin^{2}\theta\phi)+a^{2}O_{\infty}(r^{-2})LT\pi_{0}(\sin^{2}\theta\phi),

(4.10)

\displaystyle 4\underline{L}P_{1}=

\displaystyle\>[-4r^{-1}+O_{\infty}(r^{-2})]P_{1}+O_{\infty}(r^{-3})[\check{\phi}^{(1)}_{1}+\phi_{1}]+a^{2}O_{\infty}(r^{-3})[T\pi_{1}(\sin^{2}\theta\check{\phi}^{(1)})+T\pi_{1}(\sin^{2}\theta\phi)]

(4.11)

\displaystyle+a^{2}O_{\infty}(r^{-2})[LT\pi_{1}(\sin^{2}\theta\check{\phi}^{(1)})+LT\pi_{1}(\sin^{2}\theta\phi)],

\displaystyle 4\underline{L}P_{2}=

\displaystyle\>[-8r^{-1}+O_{\infty}(r^{-2})]P_{2}+O_{\infty}(r^{-3})[\check{\phi}^{(2)}_{2}+\check{\phi}^{(1)}_{2}+\phi_{2}]

(4.12)

\displaystyle+a^{2}O_{\infty}(r^{-3})[T\pi_{2}(\sin^{2}\theta\check{\phi}^{(2)})+T\pi_{2}(\sin^{2}\theta\check{\phi}^{(1)})+T\pi_{2}(\sin^{2}\theta\phi)]

\displaystyle+a^{2}O_{\infty}(r^{-2})[LT\pi_{2}(\sin^{2}\theta\check{\phi}^{(2)})+LT\pi_{2}(\sin^{2}\theta\check{\phi}^{(1)})+LT\pi_{2}(\sin^{2}\theta\phi)],

where we allow the constants in the terms in
O_{\infty}(r^{-k})
,
k=0,1
, to depend also on
m
.

Furthermore, if
\phi\in C^{4}(\widehat{\mathcal{R}})
, then

\displaystyle\lim_{\uprho\to\infty}\underline{L}(r^{2}P_{0})(\tau,\uprho)=

\displaystyle\>0,

(4.13)

\displaystyle\lim_{\uprho\to\infty}\underline{L}(r^{2}P_{1})(\tau,\uprho,\theta,\varphi_{*})=

\displaystyle\>0,

(4.14)

\displaystyle\lim_{\uprho\to\infty}\underline{L}(r^{2}P_{2})(\tau,\uprho,\theta,\varphi_{*})=

\displaystyle\>0.

(4.15)

Proof.

By the rewriting the equations in Proposition
4.1
in terms of the quantities
P_{i}
,
i=0,1,2
, using the definitions (
4.7
)–(
4.9
), we obtain (
4.10
)–(
4.12
).

Now suppose
\phi\in C^{4}(\widehat{\mathcal{R}})
, then the limits on the left-hand sides of (
4.13
)–(
4.15
) are well-defined. By applying (
4.10
)–(
4.12
), we moreover show that these limits must vanish.
∎

Definition 4.1
.

Let
\psi
be a solution to such that
\phi=\sqrt{r^{2}+a^{2}}\psi\in C^{4}(\widehat{\mathcal{R}})
. Then we define the
Newman–Penrose

charges
as the following limits:

\displaystyle I_{0}[\psi]=

\displaystyle\>\lim_{\uprho\to\infty}2r^{2}P_{0}(\tau,\uprho),

\displaystyle I_{1}[\psi](\theta,\varphi_{*})=

\displaystyle\>\lim_{\uprho\to\infty}2r^{2}P_{1}(\tau,\uprho,\theta,\varphi_{*}),

\displaystyle I_{2}[\psi](\theta,\varphi_{*})=

\displaystyle\>\lim_{\uprho\to\infty}2r^{2}P_{2}(\tau,\uprho,\theta,\varphi_{*}),

which are well-defined and
conserved in
\tau
by (
4.13
)–(
4.15
).

5
Hierarchies of
r^{p}
-weighted energy estimates

In this section, we derive the key weighted hierarchies of estimates that are involved in establishing sharp energy decay estimates.

We will use
R
to denote the area radius that appears in the definitions of
D^{\tau_{2}}_{\tau_{1}}
and
N_{\tau}
in Section
2.3
. We will need to take
R>r_{+}
appropriately large for the estimates in the sections below to hold.

5.1
Hierarchies of
r^{p}
-weighted energy estimates for
\phi^{(n)}

This section is concerned with establishing hierarchies of weighted energy estimates for the quantities
\phi^{(n)}
, which were introduced in Section
3.1
. In Proposition
5.1
below, we show that we can derive weighted energy estimates for a larger range of
n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, provided we restrict to
\phi^{(n)}_{\geq\ell}
with
\ell
appropriately large.

Proposition 5.1
.

Let
n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
,
\ell\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, such that
\ell\geq n
and
-4n<p\leq 2
. For
R>r_{+}
suitably large, there exists a constant
C=C(M,a,n,\ell,R,p)>0
, such that

\begin{split}\int_{N_{\tau_{2}}}&r^{p}(L\phi^{(n)}_{\geq\ell})^{2}+r^{p-4}[|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\phi^{(n)}_{\geq\ell}|^{2}-n(n+1)(\phi_{\geq n}^{(n)})^{2}]\,d\omega d\uprho\\
&+\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\left[\int_{N_{\tau}}r^{p-1}(L\phi^{(n)}_{\geq\ell})^{2}+(2-p)r^{p-3}\left(|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\phi^{(n)}_{\geq\ell}|^{2}-n(n+1)(\phi_{\geq\ell}^{(n)})^{2}+\delta_{n0}\delta_{\ell 0}a^{2}\sin^{2}\theta(T\phi)^{2}\right)\,d\omega d\uprho\right]d\tau\\
\leq&\>C\int_{N_{\tau_{1}}}r^{p}(L\phi^{(n)}_{\geq\ell})^{2}+r^{p-4}\left(|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\phi^{(n)}_{\geq\ell}|^{2}-n(n+1)(\phi_{\geq\ell}^{(n)})^{2}\right)\,d\omega d\uprho\\
&+C(n+\ell)\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\left[\int_{N_{\tau}}r^{p-3}a^{4}(T^{2}\phi^{(n)}_{\geq\max\{\ell-2,0\}})^{2}+r^{p-3}a^{2}(T\Phi\phi^{(n)}_{\geq\ell})^{2}\,d\omega d\uprho\right]d\tau\\
&+Cn\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\left[\int_{N_{\tau}}r^{p-1}(L\Phi\phi_{\geq\ell}^{(n-1)})^{2}+r^{p-3}(LT\phi_{\geq\ell}^{(n-1)})^{2}\,d\omega d\uprho\right]d\tau\\
&+Cn\sum_{m=0}^{n-1}\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\left[\int_{N_{\tau}}a^{2}r^{p-3}(\Phi\phi_{\geq\ell}^{(m)})^{2}+r^{p-3}(\phi_{\geq\ell}^{(m)})^{2}\,d\omega d\uprho\right]d\tau\\
&+C\sum_{m=0}^{n}\left[\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{1}}}J^{N}[T^{m}\psi_{\geq\ell}]\cdot\mathbf{n}_{\tau_{1}}\,r^{2}d\omega d\uprho+a^{2}(1-\delta_{n0}\delta_{\ell 0})\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq l\leq\lceil\ell/2\rceil\\
0\leq l_{1}+l_{2}\leq l\end{subarray}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{1}}}J^{N}[T^{m+2l}T^{l_{1}}\Phi^{l_{2}}\psi_{\geq\max\{\ell-2k,0\}}\cdot\mathbf{n}_{\tau_{1}}\,r^{2}d\omega d\uprho\right]\end{split}

(5.1)

Furthermore, the estimate (
5.1
) also holds with the last line replaced by

C\sum_{m=0}^{n}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{1}}}J^{N}[T^{m}\psi]\cdot\mathbf{n}_{\tau_{1}}\,r^{2}d\omega d\uprho.

(5.2)

Proof.

We apply
\pi_{\geq\ell}
and then multiply both sides of (
3.7
) by
-\frac{1}{2}r^{p-2}\frac{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}{\Delta}L\phi^{(n)}_{\geq\ell}
to obtain:

\begin{split}0=&-\frac{1}{2}r^{p-2}L\phi^{(n)}_{\geq\ell}\Bigg[-4\frac{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}{\Delta}\underline{L}L\phi^{(n)}_{\geq\ell}+a^{2}T^{2}\pi_{\geq\ell}(\sin^{2}\theta\phi^{(n)})+2aT\Phi\phi^{(n)}_{\geq\ell}+2(1+2n)a\frac{r}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\Phi\phi^{(n)}_{\geq\ell}\\
&+\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\phi^{(n)}_{\geq\ell}+[n(n+1)+O_{\infty}(r^{-1})]\phi^{(n)}_{\geq\ell}-[4nr^{-1}+O_{\infty}(r^{-2})]\frac{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}{\Delta}L\phi^{(n)}_{\geq\ell}\\
&+a\sum_{m=0}^{n-1}O_{\infty}(r^{0})\Phi\phi^{(m)}_{\geq\ell}+n\sum_{k=0}^{n-1}O_{\infty}(r^{0})\phi^{(m)}_{\geq\ell}\Bigg]\\
=:&\>L(\mathcal{F}^{r^{p}L}_{L})+\underline{L}(\mathcal{F}^{r^{p}L}_{\underline{L}})+\not{\rm div}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\mathcal{F}_{\not{\nabla}}^{r^{p}L}+\Phi(\mathcal{F}^{r^{p}L}_{\Phi})+\mathcal{J}^{r^{p}_{L}}.\end{split}

We will determine
\mathcal{F}^{r^{p}L}_{L}
,
\mathcal{F}^{r^{p}L}_{\underline{L}}
and
\mathcal{J}^{r^{p}_{L}}
below so that the second equality above holds.

2r^{p-2}\frac{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}{\Delta}L\phi^{(n)}_{\geq\ell}\cdot\underline{L}L\phi^{(n)}_{\geq\ell}
:

We can write

2r^{p-2}\frac{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}{\Delta}L\phi^{(n)}_{\geq\ell}\cdot\underline{L}L\phi^{(n)}_{\geq\ell}=\underline{L}\left(r^{p-2}\frac{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}{\Delta}(L\phi^{(n)}_{\geq\ell})^{2}\right)+\left(\frac{p}{2}+O_{\infty}(r^{-1})\right)r^{p-1}(L\phi^{(n)}_{\geq\ell})^{2}.

-
\frac{1}{2}r^{p-2}L\phi^{(n)}_{\geq\ell}\cdot(\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}+n(n+1))\phi^{(n)}_{\geq\ell}
:

We can write

\begin{split}-\frac{1}{2}r^{p-2}L\phi^{(n)}_{\geq\ell}\cdot(\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}+n(n+1))\phi^{(n)}_{\geq\ell}=&\>\frac{1}{4}L\left(r^{p-2}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\phi^{(n)}_{\geq\ell}|^{2}-n(n+1)r^{p-2}(\phi_{\geq\ell}^{(n)})^{2}\right)\\
&+\frac{1}{8}(2-p)r^{p-3}\frac{\Delta}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\left[|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\phi^{(n)}_{\geq\ell}|^{2}-n(n+1)(\phi_{\geq\ell}^{(n)})^{2}\right].\end{split}

-\frac{1}{2}r^{p-2}L\phi^{(n)}_{\geq\ell}\cdot a^{2}T^{2}\pi_{\geq\ell}(\sin^{2}\theta\phi^{(n)})
:

We only rewrite this term if
n=0
and
\ell=0
, as
\pi_{\geq 0}=\textnormal{id}
(for
n\neq 0
or
\ell\neq 0
we directly group it with
J^{r^{P}L}
. We can write

\begin{split}-\frac{1}{2}r^{p-2}L\phi\cdot a^{2}\sin^{2}\theta T^{2}\phi=&\>-T\left(\frac{1}{2}r^{p-2}L\phi\cdot a^{2}\sin^{2}\theta T\phi\right)+\frac{1}{4}r^{p-2}a^{2}\sin^{2}\theta L((T\phi)^{2})\\
=&\>-(L+\underline{L})\left(\frac{1}{2}r^{p-2}L\phi\cdot a^{2}\sin^{2}\theta T\phi\right)+L\left(\frac{1}{4}r^{p-2}a^{2}\sin^{2}\theta(T\phi)^{2}\right)\\
&+\Phi\left(\frac{a^{2}}{2(r^{2}+a^{2})}r^{p-2}L\phi\cdot a^{2}\sin^{2}\theta T\phi\right)+\frac{1}{8}(2-p)\frac{\Delta}{r^{2}+a^{2}}r^{p-3}a^{2}\sin^{2}\theta(T\phi)^{2}.\end{split}

-\frac{1}{2}r^{p-2}L\phi^{(n)}_{\geq\ell}\cdot 2aT\Phi\phi^{(n)}_{\geq\ell}
:

Again, we only rewrite this term if
n=0
and
\ell=0
:

\begin{split}-ar^{p-2}L\phi\cdot T\Phi\phi=&\>-ar^{p-2}L\phi\cdot(L+\underline{L}-\frac{a}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\Phi)\Phi\phi=-\Phi\left(\frac{a}{2}r^{p-2}(L\phi)^{2}+\frac{a^{2}}{r^{2}+a^{2}}L\phi\Phi\phi\right)\\
&+r^{p-2}\frac{a^{2}}{2(r^{2}+a^{2})}L((\Phi\phi)^{2})-\underline{L}(ar^{p-2}L\phi\Phi\phi)+\underline{L}(ar^{p-2}L\phi)\Phi\phi\\
=&\>-\Phi\left(\frac{a}{2}r^{p-2}(L\phi)^{2}+\frac{a^{2}}{r^{2}+a^{2}}L\phi\Phi\phi\right)+L\left(r^{p-2}\frac{a^{2}}{2(r^{2}+a^{2})}(\Phi\phi)^{2}\right)+ar^{p-2}\Phi\phi\underline{L}L\phi\\
&+\left[\frac{1}{2}(4-p)a^{2}r^{p-5}+O_{\infty}(r^{p-6})\right](\Phi\phi)^{2}+O_{\infty}(r^{p-3})L\phi\Phi\phi.\end{split}

By using (
3.5
), we can further write:

\begin{split}ar^{p-2}\Phi\phi\underline{L}L\phi=&\>\frac{1}{4}a^{3}r^{p-2}\sin^{2}\theta\frac{\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\Phi\phi T^{2}\phi+\frac{1}{2}a^{2}\frac{\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}r^{p-2}\Phi\phi T\Phi\phi+\frac{a}{4}\frac{\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}r^{p-2}\Phi\phi\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\phi\\
&+\frac{1}{2}a^{2}\frac{r^{p-1}\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{3}}(\Phi\phi)^{2}+O_{\infty}(r^{p-6})\phi\Phi\phi.\end{split}

We have that

\begin{split}\frac{1}{4}a^{3}r^{p-2}\sin^{2}\theta&\frac{\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\Phi\phi T^{2}\phi+\frac{1}{2}a^{2}\frac{\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}r^{p-2}\Phi\phi T\Phi\phi=T\left(\frac{1}{4}a^{3}r^{p-2}\sin^{2}\theta\frac{\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\Phi\phi T\phi\right)\\
&+T\left(\frac{1}{4}a^{2}\frac{\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}r^{p-2}(\Phi\phi)^{2}\right)+\Phi\left(\frac{1}{8}a^{3}r^{p-2}\sin^{2}\theta\frac{\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}(T\phi)^{2}\right)\end{split}

and

\frac{a}{4}\frac{\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}r^{p-2}\Phi\phi\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\phi=\not{\rm div}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\left(\frac{a}{4}\frac{\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}r^{p-2}\Phi\phi\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\phi\right)+\Phi\left(\frac{a}{8}\frac{\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}r^{p-2}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\phi|^{2}\right).

We combine the above identities to obtain:

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}^{r^{p}L}_{L}=

\displaystyle\>\frac{1}{4}r^{p-2}(|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\phi^{(n)}_{\geq\ell}|^{2}-n(n+1)(\phi_{\geq\ell}^{(n)})^{2})+\delta_{n0}\delta_{\ell 0}\Bigg[\frac{1}{4}a^{2}\sin^{2}\theta r^{p-2}(T\phi)^{2}-\frac{1}{2}r^{p-2}a^{2}\sin^{2}\theta L\phi T\phi

\displaystyle+r^{p-2}\frac{3a^{2}}{4(r^{2}+a^{2})}(\Phi\phi)^{2}+\frac{1}{4}a^{3}r^{p-2}\sin^{2}\theta\frac{\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\Phi\phi T\phi\Bigg],

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}^{r^{p}L}_{\underline{L}}=

\displaystyle\>r^{p-2}\frac{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}{\Delta}(L\phi^{(n)}_{\geq\ell})^{2}+\delta_{n0}\delta_{\ell 0}\Bigg[-\frac{1}{2}r^{p-2}a^{2}\sin^{2}\theta L\phi T\phi+\frac{1}{4}a^{2}\frac{\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}r^{p-2}(\Phi\phi)^{2}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{4}a^{3}r^{p-2}\sin^{2}\theta\frac{\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\Phi\phi T\phi\Bigg],

\displaystyle\mathcal{J}^{r^{p}L}=

\displaystyle\>\left(\frac{p+4n}{2}+O_{\infty}(r^{-1})\right)r^{p-1}(L\phi^{(n)}_{\geq\ell})^{2}+\frac{1}{8}(2-p)r^{p-3}\frac{\Delta}{r^{2}+a^{2}}[|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\phi^{(n)}_{\geq\ell}|^{2}-n(n+1)(\phi_{\geq\ell}^{(n)})^{2}]

\displaystyle+O_{\infty}(r^{p-3})\phi^{(n)}_{\geq\ell}L\phi^{(n)}_{\geq\ell}+O_{\infty}(r^{p-3})\Phi\phi^{(n)}_{\geq\ell}L\phi^{(n)}_{\geq\ell}

\displaystyle+a(1-\delta_{n0}\delta_{\ell 0})\sum_{m=0}^{n-1}O_{\infty}(r^{p-2})\Phi\phi^{(m)}_{\geq\ell}L\phi^{(n)}_{\geq\ell}+(1-\delta_{n0}\delta_{\ell 0})\sum_{m=0}^{n-1}O_{\infty}(r^{p-2})\phi^{(m)}_{\geq\ell}L\phi^{(n)}_{\geq\ell}

\displaystyle+\delta_{n0}\delta_{\ell 0}\left[\frac{1}{8}(2-p)\frac{\Delta}{r^{2}+a^{2}}r^{p-3}a^{2}\sin^{2}\theta(T\phi)^{2}+\left(\frac{1}{2}(5-p)r^{p-5}+O_{\infty}(r^{p-6})\right)(\Phi\phi)^{2}+O_{\infty}(r^{p-6})\phi\Phi\phi\right]

\displaystyle+(1-\delta_{n0}\delta_{\ell 0})\left[-\frac{1}{2}a^{2}r^{p-2}L\phi^{(n)}_{\geq\ell}T^{2}\pi_{\geq\ell}(\sin^{2}\theta\phi^{(n)})-ar^{p-2}L\phi^{(n)}_{\geq\ell}T\Phi\phi^{(n)}_{\geq\ell}\right].

We will now apply Lemma
2.3
. We deal with the boundary term at
r=R
by a standard averaging argument: we multiply the
\mathcal{F}^{r^{p}L}_{\square}
terms with a suitable smooth cut-off function
\chi:[r_{+},\infty)\to 0
, with
\chi(\uprho)=0
for
r\leq R-M
and
\chi(\uprho)=1
for
r\geq R
, see for example the proof of Proposition 6.5 of
[
AAG20
]
. Note that when
n\geq 1
, the flux terms on
N_{\tau}
and
I_{s}
coming from the terms in
\mathcal{F}^{r^{p}L}_{L}
and
\mathcal{F}^{r^{p}L}_{\underline{L}}
are non-negative definite for
R>0
suitably large and
-4n<p\leq 2
. When
n=0
, we can easily estimate the flux terms without a good sign by applying (
3.13
).

The terms without a sign in
\mathcal{J}^{r^{p}L}
are estimated by applying a weighted Young’s inequality. In the
n=\ell=0
case we additionally apply (
3.10
) and (
2.21
). For example: we estimate

r^{p-3}|\Phi\phi||L\phi^{(n)}|+r^{p-3}|\phi||L\phi^{(n)}|\leq\epsilon r^{p-1}(L\phi)^{2}+C\epsilon^{-1}r^{p-5}(\Phi\phi)^{2}+C\epsilon^{-1}r^{p-5}\phi^{2}

and apply (
3.10
) to estimate the
(\Phi\phi)^{2}
term for
p\leq 2
, whereas we estimate the
\phi
term using (
2.21
) (after multiplying with the cut-off
\chi^{2}
):

\begin{split}\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}&\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq R-M\}}\epsilon^{-1}r^{p-5}\chi^{2}\phi^{2}\,d\omega d\uprho\leq C\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq R-M\}}\epsilon^{-1}r^{p-3}\chi^{2}(L\phi)^{2}+r^{p-7}\chi^{2}(T\phi)^{2}\\
&+r^{p-7}\chi^{2}(\Phi\phi)^{2}+r^{p-3}(\chi^{\prime})^{2}\phi^{2}\,d\omega d\uprho,\end{split}

where we absorb the
(L\phi)^{2}
term into the left-hand side and control the remaining terms with (
3.11
) and (
3.10
).

In the
n\geq 1
case, we instead apply the higher-order integrated local energy decay estimate (
3.16
) and we moreover estimate via (
2.21
):

\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\int_{N_{\tau}}r^{p-5}(\phi^{(n)})^{2}\,d\omega d\uprho d\tau\leq C\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\int_{N_{\tau}}r^{p-3}(L\phi^{(n)}_{\geq\ell})^{2}+r^{p-3}(LT\phi^{(n-1)}_{\geq\ell})^{2}+r^{p-3}(L\Phi\phi^{(n-1)}_{\geq\ell})^{2}\,d\omega d\uprho d\tau+\ldots,

where
\ldots
denotes boundary terms at
r=R
that can easily be absorbed via an averaging argument and (
3.16
), as above.

The estimate (
5.1
) then follows. Similarly, we obtain the alternate version of (
5.1
), with the right-hand side (
5.2
) by applying (
3.12
) instead of (
3.16
).
∎

5.2
Hierarchies of
r^{p}
-weighted energy estimates for
P_{0}
,
P_{1}
and
P_{2}

When restricting to
\psi_{\ell}
, with
\ell=0,1,2
, we can obtain weighted hierarchies of energy estimates with larger weights in
r
compared to those derived in Section
5.1
by considering the quantities
P_{i}
,
i=0,1,2
defined in Section
4
.

Proposition 5.2
.

Let
0<p<4
. Then the following estimates hold: for
R>r_{+}
suitably large, there exists a constant
C(M,a,R,p)>0
such that:

for
\ell=0
:

\begin{split}\int_{N_{\tau_{2}}}&r^{p}(P_{0})^{2}\,d\omega d\uprho+\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\left[\int_{N_{\tau}}r^{p-1}(P_{0}^{2}+(L\phi_{0})^{2})\,d\omega d\uprho\right]d\tau\leq C\int_{N_{\tau_{1}}}r^{p}(P_{0})^{2}\,d\omega d\uprho\\
&+C\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\left[\int_{N_{\tau}}a^{2}r^{p-3}(LT\phi)^{2}+r^{p-5}(T\phi)^{2}\,d\omega d\uprho\right]d\tau+C\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{1}}}\mathbf{J}^{T}[\psi]\cdot\mathbf{n}_{\tau_{1}}\,r^{2}d\omega d\uprho,\end{split}

for
\ell=1
:

\begin{split}\int_{N_{\tau_{2}}}&r^{p}(P_{1})^{2}\,d\omega d\uprho+\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\left[\int_{N_{\tau}}r^{p-1}(P_{1}^{2}+(L\check{\phi}_{1}^{(1)})^{2})\,d\omega d\uprho\right]d\tau\leq C\int_{N_{\tau_{1}}}r^{p}(P_{1})^{2}\,d\omega d\uprho\\
&+Ca^{2}\sum_{i=0}^{1}\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\left[\int_{N_{\tau}}r^{p-3}(LT\phi^{(i)}_{\geq 3})^{2}+r^{p-5}(T\phi^{(i)}_{\geq 3})^{2}\,d\omega d\uprho\right]d\tau\\
&+C\sum_{j=0}^{1}\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\left[\int_{N_{\tau}}r^{p-1}(L(rL)^{j}T\phi_{1})^{2}+r^{p-5}(T\phi_{1})^{2}\,d\omega d\uprho\right]d\tau+C\sum_{m=0}^{1}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{1}}}\mathbf{J}^{T}[T^{m}\psi]\cdot\mathbf{n}_{\tau_{1}}\,r^{2}d\omega d\uprho,\end{split}

for
\ell=2
:

\begin{split}\int_{N_{\tau_{2}}}&r^{p}(P_{2})^{2}\,d\omega d\uprho+\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\left[\int_{N_{\tau}}r^{p-1}(P_{2}^{2}+(L\check{\phi}_{2}^{(2)})^{2})\,d\omega d\uprho\right]d\tau\leq C\int_{N_{\tau_{1}}}r^{p}(P_{2})^{2}\,d\omega d\uprho\\
&+Ca^{2}\sum_{i=0}^{2}\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\left[\int_{N_{\tau}}r^{p-3}(LT\phi^{(i)}_{\geq 4})^{2}+r^{p-5}(T\phi^{(i)}_{\geq 4})^{2}\,d\omega d\uprho\right]d\tau\\
&+C\sum_{i=0}^{1}\sum_{j=0}^{1}\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\left[\int_{N_{\tau}}r^{p-1}(L(rL)^{j}T\phi^{(i)}_{2})^{2}+r^{p-5}(T\phi_{2})^{2}\,d\omega d\uprho\right]d\tau\\
&+Ca^{2}\sum_{j=0}^{2}\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\left[\int_{N_{\tau}}r^{p+1}(L(rL)^{j}T\phi_{0})^{2}+r^{p-5}(T\phi_{0})^{2}\,d\omega d\uprho\right]d\tau+C\sum_{m=0}^{2}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{1}}}\mathbf{J}^{T}[T^{m}\psi]\cdot\mathbf{n}_{\tau_{1}}\,r^{2}d\omega d\uprho.\end{split}

Proof.

Note first of all that for any function
f\in L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{2})
, we can decompose
f=\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}f_{m}
, with
\Phi f_{m}=imf_{m}
such that

\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}|f|^{2}\,d\omega=\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}|f_{m}|^{2}\,d\omega.

We take the azimuthal modes
\psi_{m}
to satisfy the above equality and multiply both sides of (
4.10
)–(
4.12
) with
-\frac{1}{2}P_{j}
,
j=0,1,2
, sum over
m=-j,\ldots,j
, respectively, and apply Lemma
2.4
to obtain the following (schematic) identities:

\displaystyle 0=

\displaystyle\>\underline{L}(r^{p}P_{0}^{2})+\left[\frac{p}{2}+O_{\infty}(r^{-1})\right]r^{p-1}P_{0}^{2}+O_{\infty}(r^{p-3})P_{0}\left[\phi_{0}+a^{2}rLT\phi_{0}+a^{2}T\phi_{0}+a^{2}rLT\phi_{2}+a^{2}T\phi_{2}\right],

\displaystyle 0=

\displaystyle\>\underline{L}(r^{p}P_{1}^{2})+\left[\frac{p+4}{2}+O_{\infty}(r^{-1})\right]r^{p-1}P_{1}^{2}+O_{\infty}(r^{p-3})P_{1}\left[\check{\phi}^{(1)}_{1}+\phi_{1}+a^{2}rLT\check{\phi}^{(1)}_{1,3}+a^{2}rLT{\phi}_{1,3}+a^{2}T\check{\phi}^{(1)}_{1,3}+a^{2}T\phi_{1,3}\right],

\displaystyle 0=

\displaystyle\>\underline{L}(r^{p}P_{2}^{2})+\left[\frac{p+8}{2}+O_{\infty}(r^{-1})\right]r^{p-1}P_{2}^{2}+O_{\infty}(r^{p-3})P_{2}\sum_{i=1}^{2}\Big[\check{\phi}^{(i)}_{2}+\phi_{2}+a^{2}rLT\check{\phi}^{(2)}_{0,2,4}+a^{2}rLT{\phi}_{0,2,4}

\displaystyle+a^{2}T\check{\phi}^{(i)}_{0,2,4}+a^{2}T\phi_{0,2,4}\Big].

Note that we can estimate (suppressing
\Phi
derivatives):

\displaystyle(L\phi_{0})^{2}\leq

\displaystyle\>CP_{0}^{2}+Cr^{-4}(T\phi_{2})^{2},

\displaystyle(L\check{\phi}^{(1)}_{1})^{2}\leq

\displaystyle\>CP_{1}^{2}+C\sum_{i=0}^{1}r^{-4}(\phi_{1}^{(i)})^{2}+r^{-4}(T\phi_{3}^{(i)})^{2}+r^{-4}(T\phi_{1}^{(i)})^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\>CP_{1}^{2}+C\sum_{i=0}^{1}r^{-4}(\phi_{1}^{(i)})^{2}+r^{-4}(T\phi_{3}^{(i)})^{2}+r^{-4}(T\phi_{1})^{2}+(LT\phi_{1})^{2},

\displaystyle(L\check{\phi}^{(2)}_{2})^{2}\leq

\displaystyle\>CP_{2}^{2}+C\sum_{i=0}^{2}r^{-4}(\phi_{2}^{(i)})^{2}+r^{-4}(T\phi_{0}^{(i)})^{2}+r^{-4}(T\phi_{2}^{(i)})^{2}+r^{-4}(T\phi_{4}^{(i)})^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\>CP_{2}^{2}+C[r^{-4}(\check{\phi}_{2}^{(2)})^{2}+r^{-4}(T\phi_{4}^{(2)})^{2}]

\displaystyle+C\sum_{i=0}^{1}r^{-4}(\phi_{2}^{(i)})^{2}+r^{-4}(T\phi_{2,4}^{(i)})^{2}+(LT\phi_{2}^{(1)})^{2}+[r^{4}(L^{2}T\phi_{0})^{2}+r^{2}(LT\phi_{0})^{2}+r^{-4}(T\phi_{0})^{2}].

Furthermore, we apply (
2.21
), to estimate for suitably large
R>0

\displaystyle\int_{N_{\tau}}r^{p-5}\phi_{0}^{2}\,d\omega d\uprho\leq

\displaystyle\>C\int_{N_{\tau}}r^{p-3}(L\phi_{0})^{2}+r^{p-7}(T\phi_{0})^{2}\,d\omega d\uprho+\ldots,

\displaystyle\int_{N_{\tau}}r^{p-5}[(\check{\phi}_{1}^{(1)})^{2}+\phi_{1}^{2}]\,d\omega d\uprho\leq

\displaystyle\>C\int_{N_{\tau}}r^{p-3}(L\check{\phi}_{1}^{(1)})^{2}+r^{p-3}(LT\phi_{1})^{2}+r^{p-7}(T\phi_{1})^{2}\,d\omega d\uprho+\ldots,

\displaystyle\int_{N_{\tau}}r^{p-5}[(\check{\phi}_{2}^{(2)})^{2}+(\phi_{2}^{(1)})^{2}+\phi_{2}^{2}]\,d\omega d\uprho\leq

\displaystyle\>C\int_{N_{\tau}}r^{p-3}(L\check{\phi}_{2}^{(2)})^{2}+r^{p-3}(LT\phi^{(1)}_{2})^{2}+r^{p-3}(LT\phi_{2})^{2}+r^{p-7}(T\phi_{2})^{2}\,d\omega d\uprho+\ldots,

where
\ldots
denotes omitted terms arising from averaging the boundary terms at
r=R
via a cut-off function.

We conclude the proof by applying Lemma
2.3
together with the above identities and estimates.
∎

Remark 5.1
.

Observe that the integrals of
r^{p-5}(T\phi)^{2}
on the right-hand sides of of the estimates in Proposition
5.2
can be further estimated for
p<4
using the energy boundedness property in (
3.11
).

5.3
Additional hierarchies for higher-order derivatives

In this section we extend the hierarchies of
r^{p}
-weighted estimates established in Sections
5.1
and
5.2
to higher-order quantities of the form
\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{\alpha}(rL)^{k}T^{m}\phi^{(n)}
and
(rL)^{k}T^{m}P_{j}
by commuting the wave operator
\square_{g}
with the vector fields
rL
,
T
and the angular derivative operator
\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}
.

Note that
T
is a Killing vector field, so
T^{k}\psi
is a solution to (
3.1
) for any
k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
and the estimates derived in Sections
5.1
and
5.2
immediately apply when
\phi
is replaced by
T^{k}\phi
.

The operator
\square_{g}
, however, does
not
commute with
rL
and
\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}
, so the aim of the this section is to show that nevertheless analogues of the
r^{p}
-weighted energy estimates derived in Sections
5.1
and
5.2
still hold for
\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{\alpha}(rL)^{k}\phi^{(n)}
.

The higher-order hierarchies of
r^{p}
-weighted estimates derived in this section are essential for dealing with the coupling of spherical harmonic modes when proving improved energy decay estimates for the projections
\psi_{\geq\ell}
, with
\ell\geq 1
.

5.3.1
Commuting with
(rL)^{k}

We will show in this section that
r^{p}
-weighted energy estimates that are analogous to those derived in Sections
5.1
and
5.2
still hold when
\phi
is replaced by the higher-order quantities
(rL)^{k}\phi
, with
k\in\mathbb{N}
.

Lemma 5.3
.

Let
\psi\in C^{\infty}(\mathcal{R})
be a solution to (
3.1
). Let
n,k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
. Then
(rL)^{k}\phi^{(n)}
satisfies the following equation

\begin{split}4\underline{L}L(rL)^{k}\phi^{(n)}=&\>a^{2}\sin^{2}\theta\frac{\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}T^{2}(rL)^{k}\phi^{(n)}+2a\frac{\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}T\Phi(rL)^{k}\phi^{(n)}+\Delta(r^{2}+a^{2})^{-2}\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}(rL)^{k}\phi^{(n)}\\
&+(-kr^{-2}+O_{\infty}(r^{-3}))[a^{2}\sin^{2}\theta T^{2}(rL)^{k-1}\phi^{(n)}+2aT\Phi(rL)^{k-1}\phi^{(n)}+\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}(rL)^{k-1}\phi^{(n)}]\\
&-\left[\left(4n+\frac{1}{2}k\right)r^{-1}+O_{\infty}(r^{-2})\right]L(rL)^{k}\phi^{(n)}+r^{-2}\left[n(n+1)+\left(2n+\frac{1}{2}\right)k+O_{\infty}(r^{-1})\right](rL)^{k}\phi^{(n)}\\
&+\sum_{j=1}^{k-1}O_{\infty}(r^{-2})(rL)^{j}\phi^{(n)}+\sum_{j=0}^{k}O_{\infty}(r^{-3})\Phi(rL)^{j}\phi^{(n)}\\
&+k(k-1)\sum_{j=0}^{k-2}O_{\infty}(r^{-2})[a^{2}\sin^{2}\theta T^{2}(rL)^{j}\phi^{(n)}+2aT\Phi(rL)^{j}\phi^{(n)}+\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}(rL)^{j}\phi^{(n)}]\\
&+a\sum_{m=0}^{n-1}\sum_{j=0}^{k}O_{\infty}(r^{-2})\Phi(rL)^{j}\phi^{(m)}+n\sum_{m=0}^{n-1}\sum_{j=0}^{k}O_{\infty}(r^{-2})(rL)^{j}\phi^{(m)}.\end{split}

(5.3)

Furthermore, for
\psi
supported on a fixed azimuthal mode, we have that, schematically,

\displaystyle 4\underline{L}(rL)^{k}P_{0}=

\displaystyle\>-k\left[\frac{1}{2}r^{-1}+O_{\infty}(r^{-2})\right](rL)^{k}P_{0}+k\sum_{j=0}^{k-1}O_{\infty}(r^{-1})(rL)^{j}P_{0}+\sum_{j=0}^{k}O_{\infty}(r^{-3})(rL)^{j}\phi_{0}

(5.4)

\displaystyle+\sum_{j=0}^{k+1}O_{\infty}(r^{-3})T(rL)^{j}\pi_{0}(\sin^{2}\theta\phi),

\displaystyle 4\underline{L}(rL)^{k}P_{1}=

\displaystyle\>\left[-\left(4+\frac{k}{2}\right)r^{-1}+O_{\infty}(r^{-2})\right](rL)^{k}P_{1}+k\sum_{j=0}^{k-1}O_{\infty}(r^{-1})(rL)^{j}P_{1}+\sum_{j=0}^{k}O_{\infty}(r^{-3})[(rL)^{j}\check{\phi}^{(1)}_{1}+(rL)^{j}\phi_{1}]

(5.5)

\displaystyle+\sum_{j=0}^{k+1}O_{\infty}(r^{-3})[(rL)^{j}T\pi_{1}(\sin^{2}\theta\check{\phi}^{(1)})+(rL)^{j}T\pi_{1}(\sin^{2}\theta\phi)],

\displaystyle 4\underline{L}(rL)^{k}P_{2}=

\displaystyle\>\left[-\left(8+\frac{k}{2}\right)r^{-1}+O_{\infty}(r^{-2})\right](rL)^{k}P_{2}+k\sum_{j=0}^{k-1}O_{\infty}(r^{-1})(rL)^{j}P_{2}

(5.6)

\displaystyle+\sum_{j=0}^{k}O_{\infty}(r^{-3})[(rL)^{j}\check{\phi}^{(2)}_{2}+(rL)^{j}\check{\phi}^{(1)}_{2}+(rL)^{j}\phi_{2}]

\displaystyle+\sum_{j=0}^{k+1}O_{\infty}(r^{-3})[(rL)^{j}T\pi_{2}(\sin^{2}\theta\check{\phi}^{(2)})+(rL)^{j}T\pi_{2}(\sin^{2}\theta\check{\phi}^{(2)})+(rL)^{j}T\pi_{2}(\sin^{2}\theta\phi)].

Proof.

We have that

\begin{split}\underline{L}L(rL)^{k}\phi^{(n)}=&\>L(r\underline{L}L(rL)^{k-1}\phi^{(n)})-\frac{1}{2}\frac{\Delta}{r^{2}+a^{2}}r^{-1}L(rL)^{k}\phi-L\left(\frac{1}{2}\frac{\Delta}{r^{2}+a^{2}}r^{-1}\right)(rL)^{k}\phi+[\underline{L},L](rL)^{k}\phi\\
=&\>L(r\underline{L}L(rL)^{k-1}\phi^{(n)})-\frac{1}{2}\frac{\Delta}{r^{2}+a^{2}}r^{-1}L(rL)^{k}\phi+\left[\frac{1}{4}kr^{-2}+O_{\infty}(r^{-3})\right](rL)^{k}\phi+O_{\infty}(r^{-3})(rL)^{k}\Phi\phi.\end{split}

Furthermore,

\begin{split}\underline{L}(rL)^{k}P_{j}=&\>rL(\underline{L}(rL)^{k-1}P_{j})-\frac{1}{2}\frac{\Delta}{r^{2}+a^{2}}r^{-1}(rL)^{k}P_{j}+r[\underline{L},L](rL)^{k-1}P_{j}\\
=&\>rL(\underline{L}(rL)^{k-1}P_{j})-\frac{1}{2}\frac{\Delta}{r^{2}+a^{2}}r^{-1}(rL)^{k}P_{j}+O_{\infty}(r^{-2})(rL)^{k-1}\Phi P_{j}\end{split}

The equations in the proposition then follow by an easy induction argument, where we suppress the
\Phi
derivatives when acting on functions supported on a fixed azimuthal mode.
∎

Proposition 5.4
.

Let
n,\ell\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, with
\ell\geq n
,
K\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
and
-4n<p\leq 2
. For
R>r_{+}
suitably large, there exists a constant
C=C(M,a,n,\ell,K,R,p)>0
, such that

\begin{split}\int_{N_{\tau_{2}}}&r^{p}(L\phi^{(n)}_{\geq\ell})^{2}+r^{p-4}\left(|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\phi^{(n)}_{\geq\ell}|^{2}-n(n+1)(\phi^{(n)}_{\geq\ell})^{2}\right)\,d\omega d\uprho+K\sum_{k=1}^{K}\int_{N_{\tau_{2}}}r^{p}(L(rL)^{k}\phi^{(n)}_{\geq\ell})^{2}+r^{p-4}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}(rL)^{k}\phi^{(n)}_{\geq\ell}|^{2}\,d\omega d\uprho\\
&+\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\left[\int_{N_{\tau}}r^{p-1}(L\phi^{(n)}_{\geq\ell})^{2}+(2-p)r^{p-3}\left(|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\phi^{(n)}_{\geq\ell}|^{2}-n(n+1)(\phi^{(n)}_{\geq\ell})^{2}\right)\,d\omega d\uprho\right]d\tau\\
&+K\sum_{k=1}^{K}\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\left[\int_{N_{\tau}}r^{p-1}(L(rL)^{k}\phi^{(n)}_{\geq\ell})^{2}+(2-p)r^{p-3}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}(rL)^{k}\phi^{(n)}_{\geq\ell}|^{2}\,d\omega d\uprho\right]d\tau\\
\leq\>&C\int_{N_{\tau_{1}}}r^{p}(L\phi^{(n)}_{\geq\ell})^{2}+r^{p-4}\left(|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\phi^{(n)}_{\geq\ell}|^{2}-n(n+1)(\phi^{(n)}_{\geq\ell})^{2}\right)\,d\omega d\uprho\\
&+CK\sum_{k=1}^{K}\int_{N_{\tau_{1}}}r^{p}(L(rL)^{k}\phi^{(n)}_{\geq\ell})^{2}+r^{p-4}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}(rL)^{k}\phi^{(n)}_{\geq\ell}|^{2}\,d\omega d\uprho\\
&+C(n+\ell)\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\left[\int_{N_{\tau}}r^{p-3}a^{4}((rL)^{K}T^{2}\phi^{(n)}_{\geq\max\{\ell-2,0\}})^{2}+r^{p-3}a^{2}((rL)^{K}T\Phi\phi^{(n)}_{\geq\ell})^{2}\,d\omega d\uprho\right]d\tau\\
&+CK\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\left[\int_{N_{\tau}}r^{p-3}a^{4}((rL)^{k}T^{2}\phi^{(n)}_{\geq\max\{\ell-2,0\}})^{2}+r^{p-3}a^{2}((rL)^{k}T\Phi\phi^{(n)}_{\geq\ell})^{2}\,d\omega d\uprho\right]d\tau\\
&+Cn\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\left[\int_{N_{\tau}}r^{p-1}(L\Phi\phi_{\geq\ell}^{(n-1)})^{2}+r^{p-1}(LT\phi_{\geq\ell}^{(n-1)})^{2}\,d\omega d\uprho\right]d\tau\\
&+Cn\sum_{k=0}^{K}\sum_{m=0}^{n-1}\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\left[\int_{N_{\tau}}a^{2}r^{p-3}((rL)^{k}\Phi\phi_{\geq\ell}^{(m)})^{2}+r^{p-3}((rL)^{k}\phi_{\geq\ell}^{(m)})^{2}\,d\omega d\uprho\right]d\tau\\
&+C\delta_{\ell 0}\delta_{n0}K\sum_{i=1}^{2}\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\int_{N_{\tau_{i}}}a^{2}r^{p-2}(L(rL)^{k}T\phi)^{2}\,d\omega d\uprho\\
&+C\sum_{m=0}^{n+K}\left[\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{1}}}J^{N}[T^{m}\psi_{\geq\ell}]\cdot\mathbf{n}_{\tau_{1}}\,r^{2}d\omega d\uprho+a^{2}(1-\delta_{\ell 0}\delta_{n0})\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq l\leq\lceil\ell/2\rceil\\
0\leq l_{1}+l_{2}\leq l\end{subarray}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{1}}}J^{N}[T^{m+2l}T^{l_{1}}\Phi^{l_{2}}\psi_{\geq\max\{\ell-2l,0\}}\cdot\mathbf{n}_{\tau_{1}}\,r^{2}d\omega d\uprho\right].\end{split}

(5.7)

Furthermore, the estimate (
5.7
) also holds with the last line replaced by

C\sum_{m=0}^{n+K}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{1}}}J^{N}[T^{m}\psi]\cdot\mathbf{n}_{\tau_{1}}\,r^{2}d\omega d\uprho.

Proof.

We will prove the proposition via induction. The
K=0
case follows from Proposition
5.1
. Now suppose (
5.7
) holds for some
K\in\mathbb{N}
.

We then proceed analogously to the proof of Proposition
5.1
by considering the multiplier
-\frac{1}{2}r^{p-2}\frac{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}{\Delta}L(rL)^{K+1}\phi^{(n)}_{\geq\ell}
together with (
5.3
) with
k=K+1
:

\begin{split}0=&-\frac{1}{2}r^{p-2}L(rL)^{K+1}\phi^{(n)}_{\geq\ell}\Bigg[-4\frac{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}{\Delta}\underline{L}L(rL)^{K+1}\phi^{(n)}_{\geq\ell}+a^{2}\sin^{2}\theta\frac{\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}T^{2}(rL)^{K+1}\phi^{(n)}\\
&+2a\frac{\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}T\Phi(rL)^{K+1}\phi^{(n)}+\Delta(r^{2}+a^{2})^{-2}\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}(rL)^{K+1}\phi^{(n)}\\
&+(-(K+1)r^{-2}+O_{\infty}(r^{-3}))[a^{2}\sin^{2}\theta T^{2}(rL)^{K}\phi^{(n)}+2aT\Phi(rL)^{K}\phi^{(n)}+\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}(rL)^{K}\phi^{(n)}]\\
&-\left[\left(4n+\frac{1}{2}(K+1)\right)r^{-1}+O_{\infty}(r^{-2})\right]L(rL)^{K+1}\phi^{(n)}+r^{-2}\left[n(n+1)+\left(2n+\frac{1}{2}\right)(K+1)+O_{\infty}(r^{-1})\right](rL)^{K+1}\phi^{(n)}\\
&+\sum_{j=1}^{K}O_{\infty}(r^{-2})(rL)^{j}\phi^{(n)}+\sum_{j=0}^{K+1}O_{\infty}(r^{-3})\Phi(rL)^{j}\phi^{(n)}\\
&+K(K+1)\sum_{j=0}^{k-2}O_{\infty}(r^{-2})[a^{2}\sin^{2}\theta T^{2}(rL)^{j}\phi^{(n)}+2aT\Phi(rL)^{j}\phi^{(n)}+\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}(rL)^{j}\phi^{(n)}]\\
&+a\sum_{m=0}^{n-1}\sum_{j=0}^{K+1}O_{\infty}(r^{-2})\Phi(rL)^{j}\phi^{(m)}+n\sum_{m=0}^{n-1}\sum_{j=0}^{K+1}O_{\infty}(r^{-2})(rL)^{j}\phi^{(m)}\Bigg]\\
=:&\>L(\mathcal{F}^{r^{p}L}_{L})+\underline{L}(\mathcal{F}^{r^{p}L}_{\underline{L}})+\not{\rm div}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\mathcal{F}_{\not{\nabla}}^{r^{p}L}+\Phi(\mathcal{F}_{\Phi}^{r^{p}L})+\mathcal{J}^{r^{p}_{L}}.\end{split}

The rest of the proof proceeds analogously to the proof of Proposition
5.1
, applying in addition the induction hypothesis (
5.7
) where needed, with the only term that requires an additional, new, argument being of the form:

\left[\frac{1}{2}(K+1)r^{p-2}+O_{\infty}(r^{p-3})\right]\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}(rL)^{K}\phi^{(n)}_{\geq\ell}\cdot L(rL)^{K+1}\phi^{(n)}_{\geq\ell}.

We start by splitting:
\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}(rL)^{K}\phi^{(n)}_{\geq\ell}=-\ell(\ell+1)(rL)^{K}\phi^{(n)}_{\ell}+\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}(rL)^{K}\phi^{(n)}_{\geq\ell+1}
. Then we apply a Young’s inequality to obtain:

r^{p-2}|(rL)^{K}\phi^{(n)}_{\ell}||L(rL)^{K+1}\phi^{(n)}_{\geq\ell}|\leq\epsilon r^{p-1}(L(rL)^{K+1}\phi^{(n)}_{\geq\ell}))^{2}+C_{\epsilon}r^{p-3}((rL)^{K}\phi^{(n)}_{\ell})^{2}.

We can immediately absorb the first term on the right-hand side. We can also control the second term, using the induction hypothesis (
5.7
) if
K\geq 1
. If
K=0
, we instead apply (
2.21
) to estimate

\int_{\mathcal{N}_{\tau}}r^{p-3}(\phi^{(n)}_{\ell})^{2}\,d\omega d\uprho\leq C\int_{\mathcal{N}_{\tau_{i}}}r^{p-1}(L\phi^{(n)}_{\geq\ell})^{2}+r^{p-5}(T\phi^{(n)}_{\geq\ell})^{2}+r^{p-5}(\Phi\phi^{(n)}_{\geq\ell})^{2}\,d\omega d\uprho+(\ldots),

with
(\ldots)
here denoting boundary terms that can be estimated via the use of a smooth cut-off function.

We can similarly split
(rL)^{K+1}\phi^{(n)}_{\geq\ell}=(rL)^{K}\phi^{(n)}_{\ell}+(rL)^{K}\phi^{(n)}_{\geq\ell+1}
and repeat the argument above.

We apply a Leibniz rule in the
L
and angular directions to estimate the remaining terms:

\begin{split}&\left[\frac{1}{2}(K+1)r^{p-2}+O_{\infty}(r^{p-3})\right]\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}(rL)^{K}\phi^{(n)}_{\geq\ell+1}\cdot L(rL)^{K+1}\phi^{(n)}_{\geq\ell+1}\\
=&\>L\left[-\left(\frac{1}{2}(K+1)r^{p-2}+O_{\infty}(r^{p-3})\right)\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}(rL)^{K}\phi^{(n)}_{\geq\ell+1}\cdot\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}(rL)^{K+1}\phi^{(n)}_{\geq\ell+1}\right]\\
&+\left[\frac{1}{2}(K+1)r^{p-3}+O_{\infty}(r^{p-4})\right]|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}(rL)^{k+1}\phi^{(n)}_{\geq\ell+1}|^{2}\\
&-\frac{1}{2}(2-p)[r^{p-3}+O_{\infty}(r^{p-4})]\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}(rL)^{K}\phi^{(n)}_{\geq\ell+1}\cdot\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}(rL)^{K+1}\phi^{(n)}_{\geq\ell+1}+\not{\rm div}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}(\ldots).\end{split}

Note moreover that the second term on the right-hand side above has a good sign that we use to control the third term after applying Young’s inequality. The first term on the right-hand side above contributes to the term
\mathcal{F}^{r^{p}L}_{L}
. Note that

\begin{split}&\left|\left(\frac{1}{2}(K+1)r^{p-2}+O_{\infty}(r^{p-3})\right)\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}(rL)^{K}\phi^{(n)}_{\geq\ell+1}\cdot\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}(rL)^{K+1}\phi^{(n)}_{\geq\ell+1}\right|\\
\leq&\>\epsilon r^{p-2}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}(rL)^{K+1}\phi^{(n)}_{\geq\ell+1}|^{2}+C_{\epsilon}r^{p-2}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}(rL)^{K}\phi^{(n)}_{\geq\ell+1}|^{2}.\end{split}

We can absorb the terms in the right-hand side above into the remaining terms in
\mathcal{F}^{r^{p}L}_{L}
together with the terms on the left-hand side of the induction hypothesis (
5.7
).

Finally, note that when
n=\ell=0
, we cannot no longer energy boundedness (
3.13
) to control the following integrals along
\mathcal{N}_{\tau_{i}}
that show up when repeating the arguments of the proof of Proposition
5.1
:

\sum_{i=1}^{2}\int_{N_{\tau_{i}}}a^{2}r^{p-4}((rL)^{K+1}T\phi)^{2}\,d\omega d\uprho.

∎

Proposition 5.5
.

Let
K\in\mathbb{N}
and
0<p<4
. Then the following estimates hold: for
R>r_{+}
suitably large, there exists a constant
C(M,a,R,p)>0
such that:

for
\ell=0
:

\begin{split}\sum_{k=0}^{K}&\int_{N_{\tau_{2}}}r^{p}((rL)^{k}P_{0})^{2}\,d\omega d\uprho+\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\left[\int_{N_{\tau}}r^{p-1}((rL)^{k}P_{0})^{2}+r^{p-1}(L(rL)^{k}\phi_{0})^{2}\,d\omega d\uprho\right]d\tau\\
\leq&\>C\sum_{k=0}^{K}\int_{N_{\tau_{1}}}r^{p}((rL)^{k}P_{0})^{2}\,d\omega d\uprho+C\sum_{k=0}^{K+1}\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\left[\int_{N_{\tau}}r^{p-5}((rL)^{k}T\phi)^{2}\,d\omega d\uprho\right]d\tau\\
&+C\sum_{m=0}^{K}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{1}}}\mathbf{J}^{T}[T^{m}\psi]\cdot\mathbf{n}_{\tau_{1}}\,r^{2}d\omega d\uprho,\end{split}

for
\ell=1
:

\begin{split}\sum_{k=0}^{K}&\int_{N_{\tau_{2}}}r^{p}((rL)^{k}P_{1})^{2}\,d\omega d\uprho+\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\left[\int_{N_{\tau}}r^{p-1}((rL)^{k}P_{1})^{2}+r^{p-1}(L(rL)^{k}\check{\phi}^{(1)}_{1})^{2}\,d\omega d\uprho\right]d\tau\\
\leq&\>C\sum_{k=0}^{K}\int_{N_{\tau_{1}}}r^{p}((rL)^{k}P_{1})^{2}\,d\omega d\uprho\\
&+C\sum_{k=0}^{K+1}\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\left[\int_{N_{\tau}}r^{p-5}((rL)^{k}T\phi_{\geq 1})^{2}+r^{p-5}((rL)^{k}T\phi^{(1)}_{\geq 3})^{2}+r^{p-1}(L(rL)^{k}T\phi_{\geq 1})^{2}\,d\omega d\uprho\right]d\tau\\
&+C\sum_{m=0}^{K+1}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{1}}}\mathbf{J}^{T}[T^{m}\psi]\cdot\mathbf{n}_{\tau_{1}}\,r^{2}d\omega d\uprho,\end{split}

for
\ell=2
:

\begin{split}\sum_{k=0}^{K}&\int_{N_{\tau_{2}}}r^{p}((rL)^{k}P_{2})^{2}\,d\omega d\uprho+\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\left[\int_{N_{\tau}}r^{p-1}((rL)^{k}P_{2})^{2}+r^{p-1}(L(rL)^{k}\check{\phi}^{(2)}_{2})^{2}\,d\omega d\uprho\right]d\tau\\
\leq&\>C\sum_{k=0}^{K}\int_{N_{\tau_{1}}}r^{p}((rL)^{k}P_{2})^{2}\,d\omega d\uprho\\
&+Ca^{2}\sum_{k=0}^{K+1}\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\left[\int_{N_{\tau}}r^{p+1}(L(rL)^{k}T\phi_{0})^{2}+r^{p-5}((rL)^{k}T\phi_{0})^{2},d\omega d\uprho\right]d\tau\\
&+C\sum_{k=0}^{K+1}\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\left[\int_{N_{\tau}}r^{p-5}((rL)^{k}T\phi_{\geq 2})^{2}+r^{p-5}((rL)^{k}T\phi^{(1)}_{\geq 2})^{2}+r^{p-5}((rL)^{k}T\phi^{(2)}_{\geq 4})^{2}+r^{p-1}(L(rL)^{k}T\phi_{\geq 2}^{(1)})^{2}\,d\omega d\uprho\right]d\tau\\
&+C\sum_{m=0}^{K+2}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{1}}}\mathbf{J}^{T}[T^{m}\psi]\cdot\mathbf{n}_{\tau_{1}}\,r^{2}d\omega d\uprho.\end{split}

Proof.

We repeat the proof of Proposition
5.2
, using equations (
5.4
)–(
5.6
) instead of (
4.10
)–(
4.12
), with the role of
P_{i}
,
i=0,1,2
, replaced by
(rL)^{k}P_{i}
, with
0\leq k\leq K
.
∎

5.3.2
Commuting with
rL
,
\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}
and
T

In this section we show that we can also obtain
r^{p}
-weighted energy estimates when
\phi
is replaced with
\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{\alpha}\phi
,
\alpha\in\mathbb{N}
, by making use of the estimates derived in Section
5.3.1
.

For the sake of convenience, will consider separately
\phi^{(n)}
with
n=0
(Proposition
5.6
) and
n\geq 1
(Proposition
5.7
).

Proposition 5.6
.

Let
K,J\in\mathbb{N}
and
0<p<2
. For
R>r_{+}
suitably large, there exists a constant
C=C(M,a,K,J,R,p)>0
, such that

\begin{split}\sum_{j+\alpha\leq J}&\sum_{k=0}^{K}\int_{N_{\tau_{2}}}r^{p}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{\alpha}T^{j}L(rL)^{k}\phi|^{2}+r^{p-4}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{\alpha+1}T^{j}(rL)^{k}\phi|^{2}\,d\omega d\uprho\\
&+\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\left[\int_{N_{\tau}}r^{p-1}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{\alpha}T^{j}L(rL)^{k}\phi|^{2}+(2-p)r^{p-3}\left(|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{\alpha+1}(rL)^{k}T^{j}\phi|^{2}+a^{2}\sin^{2}\theta|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{\alpha}T^{j+1}\phi|^{2}\right)\,d\omega d\uprho\right]d\tau\\
\leq&\>C\sum_{j+\alpha\leq J}\Bigg\{\sum_{k=0}^{K}\int_{N_{\tau_{1}}}r^{p}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{\alpha}T^{j}L(rL)^{k}\phi|^{2}+r^{p-4}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{\alpha+1}T^{j}(rL)^{k}\phi|^{2}\,d\omega d\uprho\\
&+K\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\left[\int_{N_{\tau}}r^{p-3}a^{4}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{\alpha}(rL)^{k}T^{2+j}\phi|^{2}+r^{p-3}a^{2}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{\alpha}(rL)^{k}T^{1+j}\Phi\phi|^{2}\,d\omega d\uprho\right]d\tau\\
&+K\sum_{i=1}^{2}\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\int_{N_{\tau_{i}}}a^{2}r^{p-2}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{\alpha}L(rL)^{k}T^{j+1}\phi)^{2}\,d\omega d\uprho\Bigg\}+C\sum_{m=0}^{n+K+J}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{1}}}J^{N}[T^{m}\psi]\cdot\mathbf{n}_{\tau_{1}}\,r^{2}d\omega d\uprho.\end{split}

(5.8)

Proof.

Note that the
J=0
case follows from Proposition
5.4
. We then carry out an inductive argument in
J
by proceeding analogously to the proof of Proposition
5.4
with
n=0
, but considering multipliers of the form
-(-1)^{\alpha}\frac{1}{2}r^{p-2}\frac{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}{\Delta}\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{\alpha}L(rL)^{k}T^{j}\phi
. Note that the estimates in the proof of Proposition
5.4
automatically hold when
\phi
is replaced by
T^{j}\phi
, since
T
commutes with the differential operators in (
5.3
) by the Killing property of
T^{j}
. Furthermore, we integrate by parts an additional
\alpha
times by parts in the angular direction in order to deal with the
\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{\alpha}
derivative. For this step, we moreover use that
\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}
commutes with most of the terms in (
5.3
).

We will see, for example, the term:

2(-1)^{\alpha}r^{p-2}\frac{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}{\Delta}\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{\alpha}L(rL)^{k}\phi\cdot\underline{L}L(rL)^{k}\phi

If
\alpha\in 2\mathbb{N}_{0}
, we integrate by parts on
\mathbb{S}^{2}
to obtain:

\begin{split}2(-1)^{\alpha}r^{p-2}\frac{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}{\Delta}\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{\alpha}L(rL)^{k}\phi\cdot\underline{L}L(rL)^{k}\phi=&\>\underline{L}\left[r^{p-2}\frac{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}{\Delta}(\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{\frac{\alpha}{2}}L(rL)^{k}\phi)^{2}\right]\\
+\left(\frac{p}{2}+O_{\infty}(r^{-1})\right)r^{p-1}(\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{\frac{\alpha}{2}}L(rL)^{k}\phi)^{2}+\ldots,\end{split}

where
\ldots
denotes terms that vanish after integration over
\mathbb{S}^{2}
.

If
\alpha\in 2\mathbb{N}_{0}+1
, we instead obtain:

\begin{split}2(-1)^{\alpha}r^{p-2}\frac{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}{\Delta}\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{\alpha}L(rL)^{k}\phi\cdot\underline{L}L(rL)^{k}\phi=&\>\underline{L}\left[r^{p-2}\frac{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}{\Delta}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{\frac{\alpha}{2}-1}L(rL)^{k}\phi|^{2}\right]\\
+\left(\frac{p}{2}+O_{\infty}(r^{-1})\right)r^{p-1}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{\frac{\alpha}{2}-1}L(rL)^{k}\phi|^{2}+\ldots.\end{split}

In both cases, control over the desired covariant derivatives on
\mathbb{S}^{2}
follows from (
2.18
). Other terms in (
5.3
) that commute with
\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}
can be treated similarly.

Hence, the only term that requires an additional argument when
K=0
is the following:

-(-1)^{\alpha}\frac{1}{2}r^{p-2}(r^{2}+a^{2})\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{\alpha}LT^{j}\phi\cdot a^{2}\sin^{2}\theta T^{2+j}\phi,

because
[\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}},\sin^{2}\theta]\neq 0
.

After integrating by parts in the angular directions, we obtain

-(-1)^{\alpha}\frac{1}{2}r^{p-2}(r^{2}+a^{2})\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{\alpha}LT^{j}\phi\cdot a^{2}\sin^{2}\theta T^{2+j}\phi=-\frac{1}{2}r^{p-2}(r^{2}+a^{2})\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{s}\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{\frac{\alpha-s}{2}}LT^{j}\phi\cdot a^{2}\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{s}\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{\frac{\alpha-s}{2}}(\sin^{2}\theta T^{2+j}\phi)+\ldots,

with
s=0
if
\alpha
is even and
s=1
if
\alpha
is odd.

By using the following estimates:

\displaystyle|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}(\sin^{2}\theta)|^{2}\leq

\displaystyle\>N_{1}\sin^{2}\theta,

\displaystyle|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{s}\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{k}(\sin^{2}\theta)|^{2}\leq

\displaystyle\>N_{k},

for all
k\in\mathbb{N}
and
s=0,1
, with
N_{1},N_{k}
numerical constants together with (
2.18
), we can estimate

\begin{split}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}-\frac{1}{2}&r^{p-2}(r^{2}+a^{2})\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{s}\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{\frac{\alpha-s}{2}}LT^{j}\phi\cdot a^{2}\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{s}\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{\frac{\alpha-s}{2}}(\sin^{2}\theta T^{2+j}\phi)\,d\omega\\
