\geq&\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}-\frac{1}{2}r^{p-2}(r^{2}+a^{2})\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{s}\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{\frac{\alpha-s}{2}}LT^{j}\phi\cdot a^{2}\sin^{2}\theta\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{s}\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{\frac{\alpha-s}{2}}T^{2+j}\phi\\
&-\epsilon r^{p-1}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{s}\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{\frac{\alpha-s}{2}}LT^{j}\phi|^{2}-C_{\epsilon}r^{p-3}\sin^{2}\theta(\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{\frac{\alpha-s}{2}}T^{2+j}\phi)^{2}-C_{\epsilon}(\alpha-1)r^{p-3}\sin^{2}\theta\sum_{\beta=0}^{\alpha-2}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{\beta}T^{2+j}\phi|^{2}\,d\omega,\end{split}

with
\epsilon>0
suitably small so that we can absorb the corresponding term. When
\alpha=1
, we can estimate the integral of the term on the right-hand side above with the factor
C_{\epsilon}
by applying (
3.11
), using that
p<2
. If
\alpha\geq 2
, we instead appeal to the induction step to estimate both terms with the factor
C_{\epsilon}
, using again that
p<2
.

We then estimate the integral of the remaining term:

-\frac{1}{2}r^{p-2}(r^{2}+a^{2})\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{s}\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{\frac{\alpha-s}{2}}LT^{j}\phi\cdot a^{2}\sin^{2}\theta\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{s}\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{\frac{\alpha-s}{2}}T^{2+j}\phi

as in the proof of Proposition
5.1
.
∎

Remark 5.2
.

In contrast with Proposition
5.4
, the Proposition
5.6
requires
p<2
rather than
p\leq 2
. An alternative method for controlling higher-order angular derivatives, which would include the case
p=2
, would be to use that
\square_{g}
commutes with the Carter operator
Q=\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}+a^{2}\sin^{2}\theta T^{2}-\Phi^{2}
, which is an additional symmetry property of the Kerr metric. Since the
p=2
estimate is not relevant for the energy decay estimates in this paper, we have commuted instead with
\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}
in the proof of Proposition
5.6
, as this is less reliant on the symmetry properties of the background metric.

Proposition 5.7
.

Let
n\geq 1
and
\ell\geq n
, or
n=0
and
\ell\geq 1
,
K,J\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
and
0<p<2
. For
R>r_{+}
suitably large, there exists a constant
C=C(M,a,K,J,n,\ell,R,p)>0
, such that

\begin{split}&\sum_{\alpha+j\leq J}\Bigg\{\int_{N_{\tau_{2}}}r^{p}|\not{\nabla}^{\alpha}LT^{j}\phi^{(n)}_{\geq\ell}|^{2}+r^{p-4}\left(|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{\alpha+1}T^{j}\phi^{(n)}_{\geq\ell}|^{2}-n(n+1)|\not{\nabla}^{\alpha}T^{j}\phi^{(n)}_{\geq\ell}|^{2}\right)\,d\omega d\uprho\\
&+K\sum_{k=1}^{K}\int_{N_{\tau_{2}}}r^{p}|\not{\nabla}^{\alpha}L(rL)^{k}T^{j}\phi^{(n)}_{\geq\ell}|^{2}+r^{p-4}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{\alpha+1}(rL)^{k}T^{j}\phi^{(n)}_{\geq\ell}|^{2}\,d\omega d\uprho\\
&+\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\left[\int_{N_{\tau}}r^{p-1}|\not{\nabla}^{\alpha}LT^{j}\phi^{(n)}_{\geq\ell}|^{2}+(2-p)r^{p-3}\left(|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{\alpha+1}T^{j}\phi^{(n)}_{\geq\ell}|^{2}-n(n+1)|\not{\nabla}^{\alpha}T^{j}\phi^{(n)}_{\geq\ell}|^{2}\right)\,d\omega d\uprho\right]d\tau\\
&+K\sum_{k=1}^{K}\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\left[\int_{N_{\tau}}r^{p-1}|\not{\nabla}^{\alpha}L(rL)^{k}T^{j}\phi^{(n)}_{\geq\ell})^{2}+(2-p)r^{p-3}[|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{\alpha+1}(rL)^{k}T^{j}\phi^{(n)}_{\geq\ell}|^{2}\,d\omega d\uprho\right]d\tau\Bigg\}\\
\leq&\>C\sum_{\alpha+j\leq J}\Bigg\{\int_{N_{\tau_{1}}}r^{p}|\not{\nabla}^{\alpha}LT^{j}\phi^{(n)}_{\geq\ell}|^{2}+r^{p-4}\left(|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{\alpha+1}T^{j}\phi^{(n)}_{\geq\ell}|^{2}-n(n+1)|\not{\nabla}^{\alpha}T^{j}\phi^{(n)}_{\geq\ell}|^{2}\right)\,d\omega d\uprho\\
&+K\sum_{k=1}^{K}\int_{N_{\tau_{1}}}r^{p}|\not{\nabla}^{\alpha}L(rL)^{k}T^{j}\phi^{(n)}_{\geq\ell}|^{2}+r^{p-4}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{\alpha+1}(rL)^{k}T^{j}\phi^{(n)}_{\geq\ell}|^{2}\,d\omega d\uprho\\
&+\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\left[\int_{N_{\tau}}r^{p-3}a^{4}|\not{\nabla}^{\alpha}(rL)^{K}T^{2+j}\phi^{(n)}_{\geq\max\{\ell-2,0\}}|^{2}+r^{p-3}a^{2}|\not{\nabla}^{\alpha}(rL)^{K}T^{1+j}\Phi\phi^{(n)}_{\geq\ell}|^{2}\,d\omega d\uprho\right]d\tau\\
&+K\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\left[\int_{N_{\tau}}r^{p-3}a^{4}|\not{\nabla}^{\alpha}(rL)^{k}T^{2+j}\phi^{(n)}_{\geq\max\{\ell-2,0\}}|^{2}+r^{p-3}a^{2}|\not{\nabla}^{\alpha}(rL)^{k}T^{1+j}\Phi\phi^{(n)}_{\geq\ell}|^{2}\,d\omega d\uprho\right]d\tau\\
&+\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\left[\int_{N_{\tau}}a^{2}r^{p-1}|\not{\nabla}^{\alpha}L\Phi T^{j}\phi_{\geq\ell}^{(n-1)}|^{2}+r^{p-5}|\not{\nabla}^{\alpha}LT^{1+j}\phi_{\geq\ell}^{(n-1)}|^{2}\,d\omega d\uprho\right]d\tau\\
&+\sum_{k=0}^{K}\sum_{m=0}^{n-1}\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\left[\int_{N_{\tau}}a^{2}r^{p-3}|\not{\nabla}^{\alpha}(rL)^{k}\Phi T^{j}\phi_{\geq\ell}^{(m)}|^{2}+r^{p-3}|\not{\nabla}^{\alpha}(rL)^{k}T^{j}\phi_{\geq\ell}^{(m)}|^{2}\,d\omega d\uprho\right]d\tau\Bigg\}\\
&+C\sum_{m=0}^{n+K+J}\left[\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{1}}}J^{N}[T^{m}\psi_{\geq\ell}]\cdot\mathbf{n}_{\tau_{1}}\,r^{2}d\omega d\uprho+a^{2}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq l\leq\lceil\ell/2\rceil\\
0\leq l_{1}+l_{2}\leq l\end{subarray}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{1}}}J^{N}[T^{m+2l}T^{l_{1}}\Phi^{l_{2}}\psi_{\geq\max\{\ell-2l,0\}}\cdot\mathbf{n}_{\tau_{1}}\,r^{2}d\omega d\uprho\right].\end{split}

(5.9)

Furthermore, the estimate (
5.7
) also holds with the last line replaced by

C\sum_{m=0}^{n+K+J}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{1}}}J^{N}[T^{m}\psi]\cdot\mathbf{n}_{\tau_{1}}\,r^{2}d\omega d\uprho.

Proof.

We then carry out an inductive argument in
J
by repeating the arguments in the proof of Proposition
5.4
, but considering multipliers of the form
-(-1)^{\alpha}\frac{1}{2}r^{p-2}\frac{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}{\Delta}\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{\alpha}L(rL)^{k}T^{j}\phi^{(n)}_{\leq\ell}
and additionally integrating by parts in the angular directions. In contrast with the proof of Proposition
5.6
, the fact that
[\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}},\sin^{2}\theta]\neq 0
does not affect the argument, as all terms involving factors of
\sin^{2}\theta
are estimated using a straightforward Young’s inequality.
∎

5.3.3
Additional hierarchies for
T
-derivatives

We can obtain control over
additional

r^{p}
-weighted hierarchies when considering
T\phi
rather than
\phi
, by using the favourable commutation properties of
rL
and
\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}
that follow from Propositions
5.6
,
5.7
and
5.5
. These will be important for proving
better
energy decay rates for
additional

T
-derivatives.

The relevant key lemma is the following:

Lemma 5.8
.

Let
n,K,J\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
and
\ell\geq n
. Then there exists a constant
C(M,a,R,p,n,K,J,\ell)>0
such that for all
p\in\mathbb{R}
:

\begin{split}\sum_{k=0}^{K}\sum_{\alpha=0}^{J}&\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}r^{p+1}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{\alpha}L(rL)^{k}T\phi^{(n)}_{\geq\ell}|^{2}\,d\omega\\
\leq&\>CK\sum_{\alpha=0}^{J}\sum_{k=0}^{K}\sum_{\gamma+\beta\leq 1}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}r^{p-1}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{\alpha+\gamma}L(rL)^{k+\beta}\phi^{(n)}_{\geq\ell}|^{2}+r^{p-3}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{\gamma+\alpha+1}(rL)^{k+\beta}\phi^{(n)}_{\geq\ell}|^{2}+r^{p-3}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{\gamma+\alpha}(rL)^{k+\beta}\phi^{(n)}_{\geq\ell}|^{2}\,d\omega\\
&+C\sum_{\alpha=0}^{J}\sum_{k=0}^{K}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}a^{4}r^{p-3}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{\alpha}(rL)^{k}T^{2}\phi^{(n)}_{\geq\max\{\ell-2,0\}}|^{2}+a^{2}r^{p-3}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{\alpha}(rL)^{k}T\Phi\phi^{(n)}_{\geq\ell}|^{2}\,d\omega\\
&+C\sum_{\alpha=0}^{J}\sum_{k=0}^{K}\sum_{m=0}^{n-1}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}a^{2}r^{p-5}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{\alpha}(rL)^{k}\Phi\phi_{\geq\ell}^{(m)}|^{2}+r^{p-5}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{\alpha}(rL)^{k}\phi_{\geq\ell}^{(m)}|^{2}\,d\omega.\end{split}

(5.10)

Furthermore,

\begin{split}\sum_{k=0}^{K}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}r^{p+1}((rL)^{k}TP_{j})^{2}\,d\omega\leq&\>C\sum_{k=0}^{K+1}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}r^{p-1}((rL)^{k}P_{j})^{2}+\sum_{i=0}^{j}r^{p-5}((rL)^{k}\check{\phi}^{(i)}_{j})^{2}\,d\omega\\
&+C\sum_{k=0}^{K+1}\sum_{i=0}^{j}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}a^{4}r^{p-5}[((rL)^{k}T{\phi}^{(i)}_{\geq j})^{2}+((rL)^{k}T{\phi}^{(i)}_{\leq j-2})^{2}]\,d\omega.\end{split}

(5.11)

Proof.

We first split:

\displaystyle TL(rL)^{k}\phi^{(n)}=

\displaystyle\>\underline{L}L(rL)^{k}\phi^{(n)}+LL(rL)^{k}\phi^{(n)}-\frac{a}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\Phi L(rL)^{k}\phi^{(n)},

\displaystyle T(rL)^{k}P_{j}=

\displaystyle\>\underline{L}(rL)^{k}P_{j}+r^{-1}(rL)^{k+1}P_{j}-\frac{a}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\Phi(rL)^{k}P_{j}

and then we square both sides of the equations together with Lemma
5.3
to estimate
(\underline{L}L(rL)^{k}\phi^{(n)})^{2}
and
(\underline{L}(rL)^{k}P_{j})^{2}
and obtain (
5.10
) with
J=0
.

Now take
J\geq 1
. Then we repeat the above procedure, but rather than squaring both sides of Lemma
5.3
, we multiply the right-hand side of Lemma
5.3
with
(-1)^{\alpha}\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{\alpha}
acting on the right-hand side of Lemma
5.3
and integrate by parts in the angular directions, using moreover (
2.18
).
∎

6
Energy decay estimates

In this section, we convert the hierarchy of
r^{p}
-weighted estimates established in Propositions
5.6
,
5.7
and
5.5
into energy decay estimates. We outline below the strategy for obtaining both sharp energy decay rates for
\psi_{\ell}
with
\ell=0,1,2
and suitably strong energy decay estimates for
\psi_{\geq 3}
:

A)

We first obtain a preliminary energy decay estimate for the full solution
\psi
in Proposition
6.1
(
Proposition
6.2
).

B)

We then consider the restriction
\psi_{\geq 1}
and obtain a corresponding improved energy decay estimate using the preliminary energy decay established in Proposition
6.1
to deal with the contributions of the mode
\psi_{0}
that play a role in the estimate. We then consider the further restrictions
\psi_{\geq 2}
and
\psi_{\geq 3}
and keep improving the energy decay estimates successively. (
Proposition
6.2
).

C)

Subsequently, we restrict to the single spherical harmonic modes
\psi_{0}
,
\psi_{1}
and
\psi_{2}
and improve the energy decay estimate even further. The corresponding equations are coupled with
the remaining spherical harmonic modes and the terms that appear in the estimates due to this coupling are controlled using the energy decay estimates already established in Proposition
6.2
. We arrive at energy estimates that are sharp when considering initial data with
non-vanishing
Newman–Penrose charges
I_{\ell}
,
\ell=0,1,2
. (
Proposition
6.3
)

By applying the time-inversion theory from Section
9
below, we will perform an additional step:

D)

Applying the energy decay estimates Proposition
6.3
to the time-integral
T^{-1}\psi
(Proposition
9.9
) allows us to obtain sharp energy decay estimates when considering initial data with
vanishing
Newman–Penrose charges, in particular, compactly supported initial data (
Corollary
9.10
).

Proposition 6.1
.

Let
K\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
and
\delta>0
be arbitrarily small. Then there exists a constant
C=C(M,a,K,R,\delta)>0
,

\begin{split}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}&J^{N}[T^{K}\psi]\cdot\mathbf{n}_{\tau}\,r^{2}d\omega d\uprho+\sum_{m=1}^{2}\sum_{k_{1}+k_{2}+\alpha\leq K}(1+\tau)^{-m-2(K-k_{2})}\int_{N_{\tau}}r^{m-\delta}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{\alpha}L(rL)^{k_{1}}T^{k_{2}}\phi|^{2}d\omega d\uprho\\
\leq&\>C(1+\tau)^{-2+\delta-2K}E_{\geq 0,K,\delta}[\psi]\end{split}

(6.1)

with

\begin{split}E_{\geq 0,K,\delta}[\psi]:=&\>\sum_{0\leq j=j_{1}+j_{2}\leq 1}\sum_{k_{1}+k_{2}+\alpha\leq K}\int_{N_{0}}r^{2-\delta-j}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{\alpha}L(rL)^{k_{1}}T^{j_{1}+2k_{2}}\Phi^{j_{2}}\phi|^{2}+r^{-2-\delta-j}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{1+\alpha}(rL)^{k_{1}}T^{j_{1}+2k_{2}}\Phi^{j_{2}}\phi|^{2}\,d\omega d\uprho\\
&+\sum_{0\leq k_{1}+k_{2}\leq 2+2K}\int_{\Sigma_{0}}J^{N}[T^{k_{1}}\Phi^{k_{2}}\psi]\cdot\mathbf{n}_{0}\,r^{2}d\omega d\uprho.\end{split}

Proof.

We consider first the case
K=0
. We obtain energy decay by applying Proposition
5.1
with
n=\ell=0
in combination with the following ingredients:

1)

the mean value theorem along dyadic time intervals (the “pigeonhole principle”),

2)

an
interpolation inequality
(Lemma
B.1
) ,

3)

energy boundedness estimate
in the form (
3.9
), (
3.13
) and (
3.11
),

4)

an integrated local energy decay estimate (
3.12
).

Let
\{\tau_{i}\}
be a dyadic sequence, then Proposition
5.1
with
p=1
together with (
3.11
) implies that:

\int_{\tau_{i}}^{\tau_{i+1}}\int_{N_{\tau}}(L\phi)^{2}+r^{-2}(\underline{L}\phi)^{2}+r^{-2}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\phi|^{2}\,d\omega d\uprho d\tau\leq C\int_{N_{\tau_{i}}}r(L\phi)^{2}+r^{-3}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\phi|^{2}\,d\omega d\uprho+C\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{i}}}J^{N}[\psi]\cdot\mathbf{n}_{\tau_{i}}\,r^{2}d\omega d\uprho.

(6.2)

By combining (
6.2
) with (
3.10
), applying the mean value theorem and then applying (
3.9
), we obtain
\tau^{-1}
decay for the
N
-energy, with a loss of
T
and
\Phi
derivatives on the right-hand side. We can improve this energy decay rate by considering the spacetime integral of the right-hand side of (
6.2
), applying Proposition
5.1
with
p=2-\delta
together with (
3.11
) and repeating the arguments above together with the interpolation inequality in Lemma
B.1
to obtain (
6.1
) with
K=0
. Note that the decay rate corresponds to the total length of the hierarchy of
r^{p}
-weighted estimates applied minus
\delta
.

Now consider
K=1
. The above arguments hold automatically for
\psi
replaced by
T\psi
, but we can improve the energy decay even further by considering
p=3-\delta
in the following way: we apply Lemma
5.8
with
p=1-\delta
and
J=K=0
, and instead of Proposition
5.1
, we appeal to the
r^{p}
-estimates in Proposition
5.6
with
p=1
,
J=1
, and
K=1
. When then obtain
\tau^{-3+\delta}
decay. Subsequently, we consider Proposition
5.6
with
p=2-\delta
. As the length of our hierarchy is now
4
, we are left
\tau^{-4+\delta}
decay of the
N
-energy. In this process, we automatically obtain control over additional
r
-weighted quantities with slower decay rates.

We can treat the general
K
case inductively, by repeatedly applying Lemma
5.8
with
n=\ell=0
and
p=1-\delta
and suitably high values of
J
and
K
, together with Proposition
5.6
with
p=1
and
p=2-\delta
and suitably high values of
J
and
K
. We obtain in this way a hierarchy of length
2+2K
and hence,
\tau^{-2-2K}
decay for the
N
-energy.
∎

Proposition 6.2
.

Let
K\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
and
\delta>0
be arbitrarily small. Then there exists a constant
C=C(M,a,K,R,\delta)>0
,

\displaystyle\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}

\displaystyle J^{N}[T^{K}\psi_{\geq 1}]\cdot\mathbf{n}_{\tau}\,r^{2}d\omega d\uprho+\sum_{m=1}^{2}\sum_{k_{1}+k_{2}+\alpha\leq K}(1+\tau)^{-m-2(K-k_{2})+\delta}\int_{N_{\tau}}r^{m-\delta}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{\alpha}L(rL)^{k_{1}}T^{k_{2}}\phi_{\geq 1}|^{2}d\omega d\uprho

(6.3)

\displaystyle+\sum_{m=0}^{2}\sum_{k_{1}+k_{2}+\alpha\leq K}(1+\tau)^{-2-m-2(K-k_{2})+\delta}\int_{N_{\tau}}r^{m-\delta}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{\alpha}L(rL)^{k_{1}}T^{k_{2}}\phi^{(1)}_{\geq 1}|^{2}d\omega d\uprho

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\>C(1+\tau)^{-4+\delta-2K}E_{\geq 1,K,\delta}[\psi],

\displaystyle\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}

\displaystyle J^{N}[T^{K}\psi_{\geq 2}]\cdot\mathbf{n}_{\tau}\,r^{2}d\omega d\uprho+\sum_{m=1}^{2}\sum_{k_{1}+k_{2}+\alpha\leq K}(1+\tau)^{-m-2(K-k_{2})+\delta}\int_{N_{\tau}}r^{m-\delta}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{\alpha}L(rL)^{k_{1}}T^{k_{2}}\phi_{\geq 2}|^{2}d\omega d\uprho

(6.4)

\displaystyle+\sum_{m=0}^{2}\sum_{j=1}^{2}\sum_{k_{1}+k_{2}+\alpha\leq K}(1+\tau)^{-2j-m-2(K-k_{2})+\delta}\int_{N_{\tau}}r^{m-\delta}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{\alpha}L(rL)^{k_{1}}T^{k_{2}}\phi^{(j)}_{\geq 2}|^{2}d\omega d\uprho

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\>C(1+\tau)^{-6+\delta-2K}E_{\geq 2,K,\delta}[\psi],

\displaystyle\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}

\displaystyle J^{N}[T^{K}\psi_{\geq 3}]\cdot\mathbf{n}_{\tau}\,r^{2}d\omega d\uprho+\sum_{m=1}^{2}\sum_{k_{1}+k_{2}+\alpha\leq K}(1+\tau)^{-m-2(K-k_{2})+\delta}\int_{N_{\tau}}r^{m-\delta}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{\alpha}L(rL)^{k_{1}}T^{k_{2}}\phi_{\geq 3}|^{2}d\omega d\uprho

(6.5)

\displaystyle+\sum_{m=0}^{2}\sum_{j=1}^{3}\sum_{k_{1}+k_{2}+\alpha\leq K}(1+\tau)^{-2j-m-2(K-k_{2})+\delta}\int_{N_{\tau}}r^{m-\delta}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{\alpha}L(rL)^{k_{1}}T^{k_{2}}\phi^{(j)}_{\geq 3}|^{2}d\omega d\uprho

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\>C(1+\tau)^{-8+\delta-2K}E_{\geq 3,K,\delta}[\psi],

with

\displaystyle E_{\geq\ell,K,\delta}[\psi]:=

\displaystyle\sum_{m_{1}\leq M_{T,\ell},m_{2}\leq M_{\Phi,\ell}}\Bigg\{\sum_{j=0}^{\ell}\sum_{k+\alpha\leq K+3-j}\int_{N_{0}}r^{2-\delta}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{\alpha}L(rL)^{k}T^{m_{1}}\Phi^{m_{2}}\phi^{(j)}_{\geq j}|^{2}

\displaystyle+r^{-2-\delta}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{1+\alpha}(rL)^{k_{1}}T^{m_{1}}\Phi^{m_{2}}\phi^{(j)}_{\geq j}|^{2}\,d\omega d\uprho+\int_{\Sigma_{0}}J^{T}[T^{m_{1}}\Phi^{m_{2}}\psi]\cdot\mathbf{n}_{0}\,r^{2}d\omega d\uprho\Bigg\},

where
\ell=1,2,3
and
M_{T,\ell}
and
M_{\Phi,\ell}
, are suitably large integers.

Proof.

We consider first (
6.3
). Let
K=0
. Note that the estimates of Proposition
6.1
still hold when we replace
\phi
in the left-hand side by
\phi_{\geq 1}
, by orthogonality of the spherical harmonic modes. When considering
\phi_{\geq 1}
, we can further estimate via (
2.21
):

\begin{split}\int_{\tau_{i}}^{\tau_{i+1}}&\int_{N_{\tau}}r^{2-\delta}[(L\phi_{\geq 1})^{2}+r^{-4}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\phi_{\geq 1}|^{2}]\,d\omega d\uprho d\tau\\
\leq&\>C\sum_{m_{1}+m_{2}\leq 1}\int_{\tau_{i}}^{\tau_{i+1}}\int_{N_{\tau}}r^{-\delta}\left[(L\phi_{\geq 1}^{(1)})^{2}+(LT^{m_{1}}\Phi^{m_{2}}\phi_{\geq 1})^{2}+r^{-2}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}T^{m_{1}}\Phi^{m_{2}}\phi_{\geq 1}|^{2}\right]\,d\omega d\uprho d\tau.\end{split}

We then apply Proposition
5.1
with
n=\ell=1
and
p=1-\delta
to obtain:

\begin{split}\int_{\tau_{i}}^{\tau_{i+1}}&\int_{N_{\tau}}r^{2-\delta}[(L\phi_{\geq 1})^{2}+r^{-4}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\phi_{\geq 1}|^{2}]\,d\omega d\uprho d\tau\leq C\int_{N_{\tau_{i}}}r^{1-\delta}(L\phi_{\geq 1}^{(1)})^{2}+r^{-1-\delta}[|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\phi_{\geq 1}^{(1)}|^{2}-2(\phi_{\geq 1}^{(1)})^{2}]\,d\omega d\uprho\\
&+C\sum_{m_{1}+m_{2}\leq 1}\int_{\tau_{i}}^{\tau_{i+1}}\int_{N_{\tau}}r^{2-\delta}(LTT^{m_{1}}\Phi^{m_{2}}\phi)^{2}\,d\omega d\uprho d\tau+C\int_{\tau_{i}}^{\tau_{i+1}}\int_{N_{\tau}}r^{-2-\delta}[(\Phi\phi_{\geq 1})^{2}+(\phi_{\geq 1})^{2}]\,d\omega d\uprho d\tau\\
&+C\sum_{m_{1}+m_{2}\leq 1}E_{0,0,\delta}[T^{m_{1}}\Phi^{m_{2}}\psi]+C\sum_{m=0}^{1}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{i}}}J^{N}[T^{m}\psi]\cdot\mathbf{n}_{i}\,r^{2}d\omega d\uprho.\end{split}

(6.6)

We apply (
2.21
) to estimate further the third integral on the right-hand side of (
6.6
):

\begin{split}\int_{\tau_{i}}^{\tau_{i+1}}&\int_{N_{\tau}}r^{-2-\delta}[(\Phi\phi_{\geq 1})^{2}+(\phi_{\geq 1})^{2}]\,d\omega d\uprho d\tau\leq C\int_{\tau_{i}}^{\tau_{i+1}}\int_{N_{\tau}}r^{-\delta}[(L\Phi\phi_{\geq 1})^{2}+(L\phi_{\geq 1})^{2}]\,d\omega d\uprho d\tau\\
&+C\sum_{m_{1}+m_{2}=1}\int_{\tau_{i}}^{\tau_{i+1}}\int_{N_{\tau}}r^{-4-\delta}[(\Phi T^{m_{1}}\Phi^{m_{2}}\Phi\phi_{\geq 1})^{2}+(T^{m_{1}}\Phi^{m_{2}}\phi_{\geq 1})^{2}]\,d\omega d\uprho d\tau.\end{split}

Both terms can be controlled by applying the estimates in the proof of Proposition
6.1
.

Note that the second integral on the right-hand side of (
6.6
) does not involve
\phi_{\geq 1}
, but rather the full function
\phi
, so we cannot appeal to Proposition
5.1
with
n=1
. However, as it involves
T\phi
rather than
\phi
, we can apply Lemma
5.8
with
p=1
and
J=K=0
to control it using Proposition
6.1
with
K=1
. We are then left with
\tau^{-3+\delta}
energy decay for the
N
-energy of
\psi_{\geq 1}
.

In order to obtain
\tau^{-4+\delta}
decay, we apply Proposition
5.1
with
n=\ell=1
and
p=2-\delta
to control the right-hand side of (
6.6
). Note that the energy boundedness estimate (
3.9
) and local integrated energy decay estimates (
3.10
) and (
3.12
) have
N
-energies of
\psi
on the right-hand side, for which we have merely established
\tau^{-2+\delta}
decay, so after applying the mean-value theorem along dyadic intervals, we are limited to
\tau^{-3+\delta}
energy decay for
\psi_{\geq 1}
.

We therefore appeal instead to the
refined
energy boundedness estimate (
3.14
) and the refined integrated local integrated energy decay estimates (
3.15
) and (
3.16
) for
\psi_{\geq 1}
, together with Proposition
5.1
with
n=0
and
\ell=1
, so that we do not see the
N
-energy of the full solution
\psi
, but rather the
N
-energy of
T
-derivatives of
\psi
, which decay faster in time.

With the above observations, taking into account the additional loss of
T
and
\Phi
derivatives due to the use of the refined energy boundedness and local integrated energy decay estimates, we obtain (
6.3
) with
K=0
. Note that we have extended the total hierarchy of
r^{p}
-weighted estimates by
2
, which results in
\tau^{-4+\delta}
decay for the
N
-energy of
\psi_{\geq 1}
, compared to
\tau^{-2+\delta}
-decay for
\psi_{\geq 0}
.

Proposition
5.1
with
n=\ell=1
and
p=2-\delta
,
p=1-\delta
and
p=-\delta
provides moreover boundedness of the higher-order weighted energies:

\int_{\mathcal{N}_{\tau}}(1+\tau)^{2-m}r^{m-\delta}(L\phi^{(1)})^{2}\,d\omega d\uprho

with
m=0,1,2
.

We deal with the
K\geq 1
cases via an inductive argument, applying repeatedly Lemma
5.8
with
n=\ell=1
and
p=1-\delta
and suitably high values of
J
and
K
, together with Proposition
5.7
with
p=1-\delta
and
p=2-\delta
and suitably high values of
J
and
K
.

Now consider (
6.4
). The estimates for
\psi_{\geq 1}
still apply to
\psi_{\geq 2}
. As in the
\ell\geq 1
case, we apply (
2.21
) to estimate

\begin{split}\int_{\tau_{i}}^{\tau_{i+1}}&\int_{N_{\tau}}r^{2-\delta}[(L\phi_{\geq 2}^{(1)})^{2}+r^{-4}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\phi_{\geq 2}^{(1)}|^{2}]\,d\omega d\uprho d\tau\\
\leq&\>C\sum_{m_{1}+m_{2}\leq 1}\int_{\tau_{i}}^{\tau_{i+1}}\int_{N_{\tau}}r^{-\delta}\left[(L\phi_{\geq 2}^{(2)})^{2}+(LT^{m_{1}}\Phi^{m_{2}}\phi_{\geq 2}^{(1)})^{2}+r^{-2}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}T^{m_{1}}\Phi^{m_{2}}\phi_{\geq 2}^{(1)}|^{2}\right]\,d\omega d\uprho d\tau.\end{split}

We then apply Proposition
5.1
with
n=\ell=2
and
p=1-\delta
and
p=2-\delta
to extend the hierarchy of
r^{p}
-weighted estimates by 2 (in the
K=0
case). Note that we see in particular on the right-hand side the term:

\int_{\tau_{i}}^{\tau_{i+1}}\int_{N_{\tau}}r^{p-3}a^{4}(T^{2}\phi^{(2)})^{2}\,d\omega d\uprho\leq C\sum_{k=0}^{1}\int_{\tau_{i}}^{\tau_{i+1}}\int_{N_{\tau}}r^{p+3}a^{4}(L(rL)^{k}T^{2}\phi)^{2}\,d\omega d\uprho

By applying Lemma
5.8
twice, it follows that we can estimate the resulting integral with the left-hand side of (
6.1
) with
K=2
and additional commutations with
T
and
\Phi
.

We avoid
N
-energies of the full solution
\psi
appearing on the right-hand side of Proposition
5.1
with
n=0,1
and
\ell=2
, at the expense of losing additional
T
and
\Phi
derivatives on the right-hand side. Similarly, we apply (
3.14
), (
3.15
) and (
3.16
) to ensure we only see
N
-energies of sufficiently many
T
-derivatives of the full solution
\psi
on the right-hand side, which decay suitably fast. The rest of the argument proceeds in a similar manner.

Finally, we prove (
6.5
) by naturally following the strategy outlined above. Note that we exploit here the fact that the equation for
\psi_{\geq 3}
is only coupled with
\psi_{\geq 1}
, and the terms involving
T^{2}\phi_{\geq 1}
when
K=0
can be estimated via (
6.3
) with
K=2
. The estimates for higher
K
then follow inductively as above.
∎

Remark 6.1
.

In principle, one can keep precise track of the number of additional
T
and
\Phi
derivatives on the right-hand sides of the inequalities in Proposition
6.2
, but we do not expect this number to be sharp. In order to arrive at a sharp number of
T
and
\Phi
derivatives, one can instead decompose
\psi
into a bounded frequency part with respect to a time frequency
\omega
and azimuthal mode
m
, for which there will not be a loss of
T
and
\Phi
derivatives, and a high-frequency part (supported on suitably large frequencies
\omega
and
m
) that is decoupled with lower
\ell
modes, as these are supported on lower values of
m
. These two parts can be treated separately. We do not carry out this procedure in the present paper.

Proposition 6.3
.

Let
K\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
and
\delta>0
be arbitrarily small. Then there exists a constant

C=C(M,a,K,R,\delta)>0
, such that

\displaystyle\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}

\displaystyle J^{N}[T^{K}\psi_{0}]\cdot\mathbf{n}_{\tau}\,r^{2}d\omega d\uprho+\sum_{m=1}^{2}\sum_{k_{1}+k_{2}\leq K}(1+\tau)^{-m-2(K-k_{2})+\delta}\int_{N_{\tau}}r^{m-\delta}(L(rL)^{k_{1}}T^{k_{2}}\phi_{0})^{2}d\omega d\uprho

(6.7)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\>C(1+\tau)^{-3+\delta-2K}E_{0,K,\delta}[\psi],

\displaystyle\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}

\displaystyle J^{N}[T^{K}\psi_{1}]\cdot\mathbf{n}_{\tau}\,r^{2}d\omega d\uprho+\sum_{m=1}^{2}\sum_{k_{1}+k_{2}\leq K}(1+\tau)^{-m-2(K-k_{2})+\delta}\int_{N_{\tau}}r^{m-\delta}(L(rL)^{k_{1}}T^{k_{2}}\phi_{1})^{2}d\omega d\uprho

(6.8)

\displaystyle+\sum_{m=0}^{2}\sum_{k_{1}+k_{2}\leq K}(1+\tau)^{-2-m-2(K-k_{2})+\delta}\int_{N_{\tau}}r^{m-\delta}(L(rL)^{k_{1}}T^{k_{2}}\phi_{1}^{(1)})^{2}d\omega d\uprho,

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\>C(1+\tau)^{-5+\delta-2K}E_{1,K,\delta}[\psi],

\displaystyle\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}

\displaystyle J^{N}[T^{K}\psi_{2}]\cdot\mathbf{n}_{\tau}\,r^{2}d\omega d\uprho+\sum_{m=1}^{2}\sum_{k_{1}+k_{2}\leq K}(1+\tau)^{-m-2(K-k_{2})+\delta}\int_{N_{\tau}}r^{m-\delta}(L(rL)^{k_{1}}T^{k_{2}}\phi_{2})^{2}d\omega d\uprho

(6.9)

\displaystyle+\sum_{j=0}^{1}\sum_{m=0}^{2}\sum_{k_{1}+k_{2}+\alpha\leq K}(1+\tau)^{-2j-m-2(K-k_{2})+\delta}\int_{N_{\tau}}r^{m-\delta}(L(rL)^{k_{1}}T^{k_{2}}\phi^{(j)}_{2})^{2}d\omega d\uprho

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\>C(1+\tau)^{-7+\delta-2K}E_{2,K,\delta}[\psi].

with

\displaystyle E_{\ell,K}[\psi]:=

\displaystyle\>\sum_{m\leq M_{\ell,T}}\Bigg\{\sum_{j=0}^{\ell}\sum_{k+\alpha\leq K+\ell-j}\int_{N_{0}}r^{2-\delta}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{\alpha}L(rL)^{k}T^{m}\phi^{(j)}_{\geq j}|^{2}+r^{-2-\delta}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{1+\alpha}(rL)^{k}T^{m}\phi^{(j)}_{\geq j}|^{2}

\displaystyle+r^{3-\delta}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{\alpha}(rL)^{k}T^{m}P_{\ell}|^{2}\,d\omega d\uprho+\int_{\Sigma_{0}}J^{N}[T^{m}\psi]\cdot\mathbf{n}_{0}\,r^{2}d\omega d\uprho\Bigg\}.

where
M_{\ell,T}
are suitably large integers.

Proof.

We consider first
\psi_{0}
with
K=0
. Then, we can extend the length of the hierarchy of
r^{p}
-estimates in the proof of Proposition
6.1
by 2, via a consideration of the quantity
P_{0}
and an application of the estimates in Proposition
5.2
for
\ell=0
with
p=3-\delta
and
p=4-\delta
.

Note that the full
\phi
appears on the right-hand side of the relevant estimate of Proposition
5.2
, but it can be easily estimated using Proposition
5.2
. The lengths of the hierarchies in the proof of Proposition
6.1
that we appeal to when
K\geq 1
can similarly be extended by 2 by appealing to Lemma
5.8
together with Proposition
5.5
.

The above argument for extending the length of the relevant hierarchies of
r^{p}
-estimates applies also when considering
\psi_{1}
and
\psi_{2}
. We consider
P_{j}
with
j=1,2
and appeal again to Propositions
5.2
and
5.5
. In order to estimate the integrals on the right-hand side of the estimates in Propositions
5.2
and
5.5
involving the full
\phi
, we apply Proposition
6.2
.
∎

Remark 6.2
.

Note that the estimates for each
\psi_{\ell}
in Proposition
6.3
can be derived independently of each other, i.e. they are not coupled. The mode coupling that occurs in these estimates can instead be dealt with by applying the estimates that we already established in Propositions
6.1
and
6.2
.

6.1
Additional energy decay estimates

It will be useful to establish also energy decay estimates for the commuted quantities of the form
(rX)^{j}\psi_{\ell}
, with
\ell=0,1,2
.

Corollary 6.4
.

Let
\ell=0,1,2
. Let
\delta>0
be arbitrarily small and
K,J\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
. Then there exists a constant
C=C(M,a,R,K,J,\ell,\delta)>0
, such that

\sum_{j=0}^{J}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}J^{N}[(rX)^{j}T^{K}\psi_{\ell}]\cdot\mathbf{n}_{\tau}\,r^{2}d\omega d\uprho\leq C(1+\tau)^{-3-2\ell+\delta-2K}\left(E_{\ell,J+K,\delta}[\psi]+\sum_{j=0}^{J}E_{\ell,K,\delta}[N^{j}\psi]\right)\\

(6.10)

Furthermore, for
\ell=3,4
, there exists a constant
C=C(M,a,R,K,J,\ell,\delta)>0
, such that

\sum_{j=0}^{J}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}J^{N}[(rX)^{j}T^{K}\psi_{\ell}]\cdot\mathbf{n}_{\tau}\,r^{2}d\omega d\uprho\leq C(1+\tau)^{-8+\delta-2K}\left(E_{\geq 3,J+K,\delta}[\psi]+\sum_{j=0}^{J}E_{\geq 3,K,\delta}[N^{j}\psi]\right).\\

(6.11)

Proof.

Let
\ell=0,1,2
and consider the case
K=0
. We will show that (
6.10
) and (
6.10
) hold by induction in
J
. Then
J=0
case follows from Proposition
6.3
for the
\ell=0,1,2
cases and Proposition
6.2
for
\ell=3,4
.

Let us take as our induction step the estimate

\sum_{0\leq j_{1}+j_{2}\leq J}\int_{N_{\tau}}r^{-\delta}(L(rL)^{j_{1}}T^{j_{2}}\phi_{\ell})^{2}\,d\omega d\uprho\leq CE_{\ell,J,\delta}[\psi](1+\tau)^{-3-2\ell+\delta}.

(6.12)

Furthermore, by applying the arguments in the proof of Lemma
5.8
, we obtain:

\begin{split}\int_{N_{\tau}}r^{-\delta}(L(rL)^{J+1}\phi_{\ell})^{2}\,d\omega d\uprho\leq&\>C\sum_{j=0}^{J}\int_{N_{\tau}}r^{2-\delta}(L(rL)^{j}T\phi_{\ell})^{2}\,d\omega d\uprho+C\sum_{j=0}^{J}\int_{N_{\tau}}r^{-\delta}(L(rL)^{j}\phi_{\ell})^{2}\,d\omega d\uprho\\
&+C\sum_{j=0}^{J}\int_{N_{\tau}}r^{-2-\delta}((rL)^{j}T^{2}\phi)^{2}+r^{-4-\delta}((rL)^{j}\phi_{\ell})^{2}\,d\omega d\uprho.\end{split}

By Proposition
6.3
, we have that

\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{J}\int_{N_{\tau}}r^{1-\delta}(L(rL)^{j}\phi_{\ell})^{2}\,d\omega d\uprho\leq CE_{\ell,J,\delta}[\psi](1+\tau)^{-1-2\ell},

(6.13)

\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{J}\int_{N_{\tau}}r^{2-\delta}(L(rL)^{j}\phi_{\ell})^{2}\,d\omega d\uprho\leq CE_{\ell,J,\delta}[\psi](1+\tau)^{-2\ell}.

(6.14)

Using (
6.14
) together with the fact that (
6.12
) also holds if we replace
\psi
with
T\psi
, we therefore obtain (
6.12
) with
J
replaced by
J+1
.

In order to remover the
r^{-\delta}
degeneracy in (
6.12
), we interpolate between (
6.12
) and (
6.13
) using Lemma
B.1
to obtain

\sum_{j=0}^{J}\int_{N_{\tau}}J^{N}[(rL)^{j}\psi_{\ell}]\cdot\mathbf{n}_{\tau}\,r^{2}d\omega d\uprho\leq CE_{\ell,J,\delta}[\psi](1+\tau)^{-3-2\ell+2\delta}.

The
K\geq 1
case proceeds analogously. By expressing
X
in terms of
L
and
T
and using that (
3.1
) commutes with
T
, we obtain the desired estimates on
N_{\tau}=\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq R\}
.

We obtain the desired estimate on
\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r_{+}<r_{0}\leq r\leq R\}
, with
r_{0}
arbitrarily close to
r_{+}
, via standard elliptic estimates and commutation of (
3.1
) with
T
and
\Phi
: in particular, in the
K=0
we have that

\sum_{j=0}^{J}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r_{+}<r_{0}\leq r\leq R\}}J^{N}[(rX)^{j}\psi_{\ell}]\cdot\mathbf{n}_{\tau}\,r^{2}d\omega d\uprho\leq CE_{\ell,J,\delta}[\psi](1+\tau)^{-3-2\ell+2\delta}.

(6.15)

Finally, we consider the region
\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r_{+}\leq r\leq r_{0}\}
, with
r_{0}
suitably small. There we can apply the commuted redshift estimates from Section 7 of
[
DR13
]
to obtain:

\sum_{j=0}^{J}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r_{+}\leq r\leq R\}}J^{N}[(rX)^{j}\psi_{\ell}]\cdot\mathbf{n}_{\tau}\,r^{2}d\omega d\uprho\leq C\left(\sum_{j=0}^{J}E_{\ell,0,\delta}[N^{j}\psi]+E_{\ell,J,\delta}[\psi]\right)(1+\tau)^{-3-2\ell+2\delta}.

We combine the above
N
-energy decay estimates to conclude that (
6.10
) holds.
∎

7
Hierarchies of
r^{-2k}
-weighted elliptic estimates

In this section, we consider the following inhomogeneous equation;

\mathcal{L}\psi=F,

(7.1)

with
\mathcal{L}
defined as follows:

\mathcal{L}\psi=X(\Delta X\psi)+2aX\Phi\psi+\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\psi.

Note that if
\psi
is a solution to (
3.1
), then it is a solution to (
7.1
), with

\begin{split}F=&\>2[h\Delta-(r^{2}+a^{2})](r^{2}+a^{2})^{-1/2}XT\phi+[(\Delta h)^{\prime}-2h\Delta r(r^{2}+a^{2})^{-1}]T\psi+[2h(r^{2}+a^{2})-h^{2}\Delta-a^{2}\sin^{2}\theta]T^{2}\psi\\
&+2a(h-1)T\Phi\psi.\end{split}

(7.2)

One may easily verify that the operator
\mathcal{L}
is elliptic
if and only if
we restrict its domain to lie outside of the ergoregion
\{\Delta-a^{2}\sin^{2}\theta>0\}
. The reason for this is that
T
is timelike if and only if we restrict to the subset of the spacetime that lies outside the ergoregion. In the special case
a=0
, ellipticity only breaks down at the boundary
r=r_{+}
.

In order to deal with the presence of the ergoregion when deriving appropriately weighted
L^{2}
estimates for
\psi
in terms of
F
, we appeal to elliptic versions of the redshift estimates that were introduced in the hyperbolic setting in
[
DR13
]
.
5
5

5

The estimates use strict positivity of
\frac{d\Delta}{dr}
, which near the event horizon
\mathcal{H}^{+}
is a manifestation of the redshift effect and therefore motivates the nomenclature. In contrast with the
hyperbolic
redshift estimates, the elliptic redshift estimates make use of the fact that
\frac{d\Delta}{dr}>0

globally
in
r
, rather than just locally near
r=r_{+}
.

7.1
Spacelike redshift estimates

In contrast with standard elliptic estimates for uniformly elliptic operators, e.g. the Laplacian in Euclidean space, the spacelike redshift estimate below establishes control over weighted
H^{1}
norms of
\psi
, rather than (weighted)
H^{2}
norms of
\psi
.

Proposition 7.1
.

There exists a constant
C=C(M,a)>0
, such that for solutions
\psi
to (
7.1
) that are suitably regular and decaying in
\uprho
:

\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}r(X\psi)^{2}\,d\omega d\uprho\leq C\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}r^{-1}F^{2}\,d\omega d\uprho.

(7.3)

If
\psi
is a solution to (
3.1
), then we moreover have:

\displaystyle\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}r(X\psi)^{2}\,d\omega d\uprho\leq

\displaystyle\>C\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}J^{N}[T\psi]\cdot\mathbf{n}_{\tau}\,d\mu_{\tau}+C\int_{N_{\tau}}r(LT\phi)^{2}\,d\omega d\uprho,

(7.4)

\displaystyle\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}r(X\psi_{\geq 3})^{2}\,d\omega d\uprho\leq

\displaystyle\>C\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}J^{N}[T\psi_{\geq 3}]\cdot\mathbf{n}_{\tau}\,d\mu_{\tau}+C\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}r^{-1}(T^{2}\psi_{1})^{2}+r^{-1}(T^{2}\psi_{2})^{2}\,d\omega d\uprho+C\int_{N_{\tau}}r(LT\phi_{\geq 3})^{2}\,d\omega d\uprho.

(7.5)

Proof.

We multiply both sides of (
7.1
) by
X\psi
, to obtain

\begin{split}X\psi F=&\>\Delta X\psi X^{2}\psi+\Delta^{\prime}(X\psi)^{2}+2aX\psi X\Phi\psi+X\psi\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\psi\\
=&\>X\left(\frac{1}{2}\Delta(X\psi)^{2}-\frac{1}{2}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\psi|^{2}\right)+\frac{1}{2}\Delta^{\prime}(X\psi)^{2}+\Phi(a(X\psi)^{2})+\not{\rm div}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}(X\psi\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\psi).\end{split}

Integrating along
\Sigma_{\tau}
and using that
\lim_{\uprho\to\infty}\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\psi(\tau,\uprho,\theta,\varphi)=0
, we obtain (
7.3
).

In order to obtain (
7.4
), we first apply Young’s inequality to the terms in
r^{-1}F^{2}
to obtain

r^{-1}F^{2}\lesssim r(XT\phi)^{2}+r^{-3}(T^{2}\phi)^{2}+r^{-3}(\Phi T\phi)^{2}+r^{-3}(T\phi)^{2},

absorbing the integral of
r^{-3}(T\phi)^{2}
into the remaining terms, using (
2.20
), and then relating with
J^{T}[T\psi]\cdot\mathbf{n}_{\tau}
by applying (
2.20
) once more.
∎

In the proposition below, we obtain
local
, higher-order versions of the estimate (
7.3
) by projecting to fixed spherical harmonic modes. These provide control inside the ergoregion and will be combined with the elliptic estimates outside the ergoregion derived in Section
7.2
.

Proposition 7.2
.

Let
\psi
be a solution to (
7.1
) that is suitably regular, then:

(i)

For arbitrary
r_{0}>r_{+}
, there exists a constant
C=C(M,a,r_{0},\ell)>0
such that

\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\leq r_{0}\}}(X(\Delta X^{\ell+1}\psi_{\ell}))^{2}+(X^{\ell+1}\psi_{\ell})^{2}\,d\omega d\uprho\leq C\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\leq r_{0}\}}(\pi_{\ell}X^{\ell}F)^{2}\,d\omega d\uprho.

(7.6)

(ii)

For all
r\geq r_{+}
, there exists a constant
C=C(M,a)>0
, such that furthermore:

||X^{\ell+1}\psi_{\ell}||_{L^{2}(S^{2}_{\tau,r^{\prime}})}\leq C\sup_{r_{+}\leq r\leq r^{\prime}}||r^{-1}\pi_{\ell}X^{\ell}F||_{L^{2}(S^{2}_{\tau,r})}.

(7.7)

(iii)

There exists a constant
C=C(M,a,\ell)>0
such that

\begin{split}||X^{\ell+1}\psi_{\ell}||_{L^{2}(S^{2}_{\tau,r^{\prime}})}\leq&\>C\sum_{k=0}^{\ell+1}\sup_{r_{+}\leq r\leq r^{\prime}}||r^{-\ell}(rX)^{k}T\psi_{\ell}||_{L^{2}(S^{2}_{\tau,r})}+C\sum_{k=0}^{\ell}\sup_{r_{+}\leq r\leq r^{\prime}}||r^{-2-\ell}(rX)^{k}T^{2}\psi_{\ell}||_{L^{2}(S^{2}_{\tau,r})}\\
&+Ca^{2}\sup_{r_{+}\leq r\leq r^{\prime}}||r^{-2}X^{\ell}T^{2}\psi_{\ell-2}||_{L^{2}(S^{2}_{\tau,r})}+Ca^{2}\sup_{r_{+}\leq r\leq r^{\prime}}||r^{-2}X^{\ell}T^{2}\psi_{\ell+2}||_{L^{2}(S^{2}_{\tau,r})}.\end{split}

(7.8)

(iv)

There exists a constant
C=C(M,a,\ell)>0
such that for
\ell\geq 1
:

\begin{split}||r^{2}X^{\ell+1}\psi_{\ell}||_{L^{2}(S^{2}_{\tau,r^{\prime}})}\leq&\>C\sum_{k=0}^{\ell+1}\sup_{r_{+}\leq r\leq r^{\prime}}||r^{2-\ell}(rX)^{k}T\psi_{\ell}||_{L^{2}(S^{2}_{\tau,r})}+C\sum_{k=0}^{\ell}\sup_{r_{+}\leq r\leq r^{\prime}}||r^{-\ell}(rX)^{k}T^{2}\psi_{\ell}||_{L^{2}(S^{2}_{\tau,r})}\\
&+Ca^{2}\sup_{r_{+}\leq r\leq r^{\prime}}||X^{\ell}T^{2}\psi_{\ell-2}||_{L^{2}(S^{2}_{\tau,r})}+Ca^{2}\sup_{r_{+}\leq r\leq r^{\prime}}||X^{\ell}T^{2}\psi_{\ell+2}||_{L^{2}(S^{2}_{\tau,r})}.\end{split}

(7.9)

Proof.

It follows easily by induction that for all
n\geq 0
:

X^{n+1}(\Delta X\psi)=X(\Delta X^{n+1}\psi)+n\Delta^{\prime}X^{n+1}\psi+n(n+1)X^{n}\psi.

Hence,

X^{n}F=X(\Delta X^{n+1}\psi)+n\Delta^{\prime}X^{n+1}\psi+n(n+1)X^{n}\psi+2a{X}\Phi X^{n}\psi+\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}(X^{n}\psi).

(7.10)

A cancellation occurs in (
7.10
) when
n=\ell
after projecting onto the
\ell
-th spherical harmonic mode:

X^{\ell}{\pi}_{\ell}F=X(\Delta X^{\ell+1}\psi_{\ell})+\ell\Delta^{\prime}X^{\ell+1}\psi_{\ell}+2a\Phi X^{\ell+1}\psi_{\ell}

(7.11)

Now consider the multiplier
X^{\ell+1}\psi_{\ell}
to obtain:

\begin{split}X^{\ell+1}\psi_{\ell}\cdot X^{\ell}{\pi}_{\ell}F=&\Delta X^{\ell+1}\psi_{\ell}X^{\ell+2}\psi_{\ell}+(\ell+1)\Delta^{\prime}(X^{\ell+1}\psi_{\ell})^{2}+2aX^{\ell+1}\psi_{\ell}X^{\ell+1}\Phi\psi_{\ell}\\
=&\>X\left(\frac{1}{2}\Delta(X^{\ell+1}\psi_{\ell})^{2}\right)+\Phi(a(X^{\ell+1}\psi_{\ell})^{2})+\left(\ell+\frac{1}{2}\right)\Delta^{\prime}(X^{\ell+1}\psi_{\ell})^{2}.\end{split}

Hence, we can integrate the above inequality to obtain for all
r_{0}\geq r_{+}
:

\begin{split}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}&\frac{1}{2}\Delta(X^{\ell+1}\psi_{\ell})|_{r=r_{0},\tau=\tau^{\prime}}^{2}\,d\omega+\left(\ell+\frac{1}{2}\right)\int_{\Sigma_{\tau^{\prime}}\cap\{r\leq r_{0}\}}\Delta^{\prime}(X^{\ell+1}\psi_{\ell})^{2}\,d\omega d\uprho\leq\int_{\Sigma_{\tau^{\prime}}\cap\{r\leq r_{0}\}}|X^{\ell+1}\psi_{\ell}\cdot X^{\ell}{\pi}_{\ell}F|\,d\omega d\uprho\\
\leq&\>\int_{\Sigma_{\tau^{\prime}}\cap\{r\leq r_{0}\}}\epsilon\Delta^{\prime}(X^{\ell+1}\psi_{\ell})^{2}+\frac{1}{2\epsilon}\frac{1}{\Delta^{\prime}}(X^{\ell}{\pi}_{\ell}F)^{2}\,d\omega d\uprho.\end{split}

(7.12)

Then, (
7.6
) immediately follows after absorbing the
(X^{\ell+1}\psi_{\ell})^{2}
term on the very right-hand side of (
7.12
) into the left-hand side (taking
\epsilon>0
suitably small) and including a
(X(\Delta X^{\ell+1}\psi_{\ell})^{2}
term by using:

(X(\Delta X^{\ell+1}\psi_{\ell})^{2}\leq C(\Delta^{\prime}X^{\ell+1}\psi_{\ell})^{2}+Ca^{2}(\Phi X^{\ell+1}\psi_{\ell})^{2}+(X^{\ell}{\pi}_{\ell}F)^{2}.

It moreover follows that for
r^{\prime}\in[r_{+},r_{0}]
:

\begin{split}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\Delta(r^{\prime})(X^{\ell+1}\psi_{\ell})|_{r=r^{\prime},\tau=\tau^{\prime}}^{2}\,d\omega\leq&\>C\int_{\Sigma_{\tau^{\prime}}\cap\{r\leq r^{\prime}\}}r^{-1}(X^{\ell}{\pi}_{\ell}F)^{2}\,d\omega d\rho\\
\leq&\>C\Delta(r^{\prime})r^{\prime-1}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\sup_{r_{+}\leq r\leq r^{\prime}}r^{-1}(X^{\ell}{\pi}_{\ell}F)^{2}|_{\tau=\tau^{\prime}}\,d\omega\\
\end{split}

and hence we obtain (
7.7
) for the range
r^{\prime}\in[r_{+},r_{0}]
, with
r_{0}>r_{+}
arbitrarily large, with a constant
C
depending moreover on
r_{0}
.

In order to prove (
7.7
) for
r^{\prime}\in(r_{0},\infty)
, we appeal again to (
7.12
) and Young’s inequality to estimate:

\begin{split}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}&\frac{1}{2}\sup_{r_{0}\leq\tilde{r}\leq r^{\prime}}\Delta(X^{\ell+1}\psi_{\ell})|_{r=\tilde{r},\tau=\tau^{\prime}}^{2}\,d\omega\leq\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\frac{1}{2}\Delta(X^{\ell+1}\psi_{\ell})|_{r=r_{0}}^{2}\,d\omega+\int_{\Sigma_{\tau^{\prime}}\cap\{r_{0}\leq\tilde{r}\leq r^{\prime}\}}|X^{\ell+1}\psi_{\ell}\cdot X^{\ell}{\pi}_{\ell}F|\,d\omega d\uprho\\
\leq&\>\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\frac{1}{2}\Delta(X^{\ell+1}\psi_{\ell})|_{r=r_{0},\tau=\tau^{\prime}}^{2}\,d\omega+\frac{1}{4(r^{\prime}-r_{0})}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau^{\prime}}\cap\{r_{0}\leq\tilde{r}\leq r^{\prime}\}}\Delta(X^{\ell+1}\psi_{\ell})^{2}\,d\omega d\uprho\\
&+(r^{\prime}-r_{0})\int_{\Sigma_{\tau^{\prime}}\cap\{r_{0}\leq\tilde{r}\leq r^{\prime}\}}\Delta^{-1}(X^{\ell}{\pi}_{\ell}F)^{2}\,d\omega d\uprho\\
\leq&\>\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\frac{1}{2}\Delta(X^{\ell+1}\psi_{\ell})|_{r=r_{0},\tau=\tau^{\prime}}^{2}\,d\omega+\frac{1}{4}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\sup_{r_{0}\leq\tilde{r}\leq r^{\prime}}\Delta(X^{\ell+1}\psi_{\ell})|_{r=\tilde{r},\tau=\tau^{\prime}}^{2}\,d\omega\\
&+(r^{\prime}-r_{0})^{2}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\sup_{r_{0}\leq\tilde{r}\leq r^{\prime}}\Delta^{-1}(X^{\ell}{\pi}_{\ell}F)^{2}|_{r=r^{\prime},\tau=\tau^{\prime}}\,d\omega.\end{split}

Hence, for all
r^{\prime}\in[r_{0},\infty)

\begin{split}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}&\Delta(X^{\ell+1}\psi_{\ell})|_{r=r^{\prime},\tau=\tau^{\prime}}^{2}\,d\omega\leq 4(r^{\prime}-r_{0})^{2}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\sup_{r_{0}\leq\tilde{r}\leq r^{\prime}}\Delta^{-1}(X^{\ell}{\pi}_{\ell}F)^{2}|_{r=r^{\prime},\tau=\tau^{\prime}}\,d\omega\\
&+C\Delta(r_{0})\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\sup_{r_{+}\leq r\leq r_{0}}r^{-2}(X^{\ell}{\pi}_{\ell}F)^{2}|_{\tau=\tau^{\prime}}\,d\omega.\end{split}

from which we can conclude (
7.7
).

We obtain (
7.8
) by applying (
7.7
) and inserting for
F
the right-hand side of (
7.2
).

Finally, we observe that we can rearrange the terms in (
7.11
) and multiply by
\Delta^{\ell}
as follows:

\Delta^{\ell}X^{\ell}{\pi}_{\ell}F=X(\Delta^{\ell+1}X^{\ell+1}\psi_{\ell})+2a\Delta^{\ell}\Phi X^{\ell+1}\psi_{\ell}.

Suppose now that
\ell\geq 1
. Then we integrate to obtain for
\uprho\geq r_{0}
:

|\Delta^{\ell+1}X^{\ell+1}\psi_{\ell}|(\tau,\uprho,\theta,\varphi_{*})\leq Cr^{2\ell+1}||X^{\ell+1}\psi_{\ell}||_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma_{\tau})}+C\Delta^{\ell}||rX^{\ell}{\pi}_{\ell}F||_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma_{\tau})},

and therefore

||rX^{\ell+1}\psi_{\ell}||_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma_{\tau})}\leq C||X^{\ell+1}\psi_{\ell}||_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma_{\tau})}+||rX^{\ell}{\pi}_{\ell}F||_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma_{\tau})}.

(7.13)

Repeating the above step, using now (
7.13
), we obtain for
\ell\geq 1
:

|\Delta^{\ell+1}X^{\ell+1}\psi_{\ell}|(\tau,\uprho,\theta,\varphi_{*})\leq Cr^{2\ell}\Delta^{\ell}||rX^{\ell+1}\psi_{\ell}||_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma_{\tau})}+\Delta^{\ell}||rX^{\ell}{\pi}_{\ell}F||_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma_{\tau})}

and (
7.9
) follows by applying a standard Sobolev inequality on
\mathbb{S}^{2}
.
∎

7.2
Elliptic estimates outside ergoregion

In this section, we derive elliptic estimates outside the ergoregion, making use of the uniform ellipticity of
\mathcal{L}
when restricted to a region where
r
is sufficiently large. In order to couple these estimates with the spacelike redshift estimates from Section
7.1
, we restrict to fixed spherical harmonic modes.

Proposition 7.3
.

Let
\ell\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
and
k\in\mathbb{R}
, with
k<\ell+\frac{1}{2}
. Let
\psi
be a solution to (
7.1
), such that
r\psi\in C^{\infty}(\widehat{\Sigma}_{\tau})
. Then, for
R_{0}>2M\geq r_{+}
suitably large, there exists a constant
C=C(M,a,R_{0},\ell,k)>0
, such that

\begin{split}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq R_{0}\}}r^{2k+2}(X^{\ell+1}\psi_{\ell})^{2}\,d\omega d\uprho+r^{2k}(X(\Delta X^{\ell+1}\psi_{\ell}))^{2}\,d\omega d\uprho\leq&\>C\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}r^{2k}(X^{\ell}{\pi}_{\ell}F)^{2}\,d\omega d\uprho.\end{split}

(7.14)

Proof.

Denote

G_{n}:=X^{n}F-2a\Phi X^{n+1}\psi.

Then we can write

G_{n}=X(\Delta X^{n+1}\psi)+n\Delta^{\prime}X^{n+1}\psi+n(n+1)X^{n}\psi+\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}(X^{n}\psi),

so that

X(\Delta X^{\ell+1}\psi_{\ell})+\ell\Delta^{\prime}X^{\ell+1}\psi=\pi_{\ell}G_{\ell}.

Let
\chi:[r_{+},\infty)
be a cut-off function such that
\chi(r)=1
for
r\geq R_{0}
and
\chi(r)=0
for
r\leq R_{0}-M
, with
R_{0}>r_{+}+M
to be chosen suitably large. Then for any
k\in\mathbb{N}

\chi^{2}r^{2k}(X(\Delta X^{\ell+1}\psi_{\ell}))^{2}+\chi^{2}\ell^{2}(\Delta^{\prime})^{2}r^{2k}(X^{\ell+1}\psi_{\ell})^{2}+\chi^{2}2\ell\Delta^{\prime}r^{2k}X^{\ell+1}\psi X(\Delta X^{\ell+1}\psi_{\ell})=\chi r^{2k}(\pi_{\ell}G_{\ell})^{2}.

We can further estimate

\begin{split}\chi^{2}2\ell\Delta^{\prime}r^{2k}X^{\ell+1}\psi_{\ell}X(\Delta X^{\ell+1}\psi_{\ell})=&\>2\ell\chi^{2}(\Delta^{\prime})^{2}r^{2k}(X^{\ell+1}\psi_{\ell})^{2}+\ell\chi^{2}\Delta^{\prime}\Delta r^{2k}X((X^{\ell+1}\psi_{\ell})^{2})\\
=&\>\ell\left[2(\Delta^{\prime})^{2}r^{2k}-(\Delta^{\prime}\Delta r^{2k})^{\prime}\right]\chi^{2}(X^{\ell+1}\psi_{\ell})^{2}-2\ell\chi^{\prime}\chi\Delta^{\prime}\Delta(X^{\ell+1}\psi_{\ell})^{2}\\
&+X(\ell\chi^{2}\Delta^{\prime}\Delta r^{2k}(X^{\ell+1}\psi_{\ell})^{2})\\
=&\>-\ell[4k-2+O(r^{-1})]r^{2k+2}(X^{\ell+1}\psi_{\ell})^{2}-2\ell\chi^{\prime}\chi\Delta^{\prime}\Delta(X^{\ell+1}\psi_{\ell})^{2}\\
&+X(\ell\chi\Delta^{\prime}r^{2k}(X^{\ell+1}\psi_{\ell})^{2}).\end{split}

Note that

\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}X(\ell\chi^{2}\Delta^{\prime}\Delta r^{2k}(X^{\ell+1}\psi_{\ell})^{2})\,d\omega d\uprho=0

if
\ell=0
or
k<\ell+\frac{1}{2}
.

By (
2.20
) we can moreover estimate for
k<\ell+\frac{1}{2}
and
\epsilon>0
arbitrarily small:

\begin{split}\frac{(2k-1)^{2}}{4}(1-2\epsilon)&\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}r^{2k-2}\Delta^{2}\chi^{2}(X^{\ell+1}\psi_{\ell})^{2}\,d\omega d\uprho\leq(1-\epsilon)\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\chi^{2}r^{2k}(X(\Delta X^{\ell+1}\psi_{\ell}))^{2}\,d\omega d\uprho\\
&+C_{\epsilon}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}(\chi^{\prime})^{2}\Delta^{2}(X^{\ell+1}\psi_{\ell})^{2}\,d\omega d\uprho.\end{split}

Finally, observe that

r^{2k}(\pi_{\ell}G_{\ell})^{2}\leq Cr^{2k}(X^{\ell}{\pi}_{\ell}F)^{2}+a^{2}r^{2k}(\Phi X^{\ell+1}\psi_{\ell})^{2}.

We then combine the above estimates to obtain the following integral inequality:

\begin{split}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}&\chi\epsilon r^{2k}(X(\Delta X^{\ell+1}\psi_{\ell}))^{2}+\left[4\ell^{2}+(1-2k)2\ell+\frac{1}{4}(2k-1)^{2}-2\epsilon\frac{(2k-1)^{2}}{4}+O_{\infty}(r^{-1})\right]\chi^{2}r^{2k+2}(X^{\ell+1}\psi_{\ell})^{2}\,d\omega d\uprho\\
\leq&\>C_{\epsilon,R_{0},\ell}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{R_{0}-M\leq r\leq R_{0}\}}(X^{\ell+1}\psi_{\ell})^{2}\,d\omega d\uprho+C\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}r^{2k}(X^{\ell}{\pi}_{\ell}F)^{2}\,d\omega d\uprho.\end{split}

We can choose
R_{0}
suitably large and
\epsilon>0
suitably small in order to make the left-hand side non-negative definite, provided

0<(2\ell)^{2}+(1-2k)2\ell+\frac{1}{4}(2k-1)^{2}=\left(k-2\ell-\frac{1}{2}\right)^{2},

which follows in particular from the condition
k<\ell+\frac{1}{2}
.

We conclude (
7.14
) by applying additionally (
7.6
).
∎

In the proposition below, we obtain additional control over arbitrarily many
X
-derivatives of
\psi_{\ell}
, starting from the estimates established in Proposition
7.3
.

Proposition 7.4
.

Let
J,\ell\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
. Restrict
-\frac{1}{2}<k<\ell+\frac{1}{2}
when
\ell\geq 1
and
k>-\frac{1}{2}
when
\ell=0
. Let
\psi
be a solution to (
7.1
), such that
r\psi\in C^{\infty}(\widehat{\Sigma}_{\tau})
. Then, for
R_{0}>2M\geq r_{+}
suitably large, there exists a constant
C=C(M,a,R_{0},\ell,k,J)>0
, such that

\begin{split}\sum_{n=0}^{\ell+J}&\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}r^{-2k+2}(X(rX)^{n}\psi_{\ell})^{2}+\ell^{2}r^{-2k}((rX)^{n}\psi_{\ell})^{2}\,d\omega d\uprho\leq C\sum_{n=0}^{\ell+J}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}r^{-2k}((rX)^{n}({\pi}_{\ell}F))^{2}\,d\omega d\uprho.\end{split}

(7.15)

If
\psi
is a solution to (
3.1
), then

\begin{split}\sum_{n=0}^{\ell+J}&\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}r^{-2k+2}(X(rX)^{n}\psi_{\ell})^{2}+\ell^{2}r^{-2k}((rX)^{n}\psi_{\ell})^{2}\,d\omega d\uprho\leq C\sum_{n=0}^{\ell+J}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}r^{-2k+4}(XT(rX)^{n}\psi_{\ell})^{2}\\
&+r^{-2k+2}(T^{2}(rX)^{n}\psi_{\ell})^{2}+a^{2}r^{-2k}(T^{2}(rX)^{n}\psi_{\ell-2})^{2}+a^{2}r^{-2k}(T^{2}(rX)^{n}\psi_{\ell+2})^{2}\,d\omega d\uprho.\end{split}

(7.16)

Proof.

Let
n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
. By (
7.10
) it follows that

\begin{split}r^{m}X^{n+1}\psi_{\ell}X^{n}{\pi}_{\ell}F=&\>\Delta r^{m}X^{n+1}\psi_{\ell}X^{n+2}\psi_{\ell}+(n+1)\Delta^{\prime}r^{m}(X^{n+1}\psi_{\ell})^{2}-\big[\ell(\ell+1)\\
&-n(n+1)\big]r^{m}X^{n+1}\psi_{\ell}X^{n}\psi_{\ell}+2ar^{m}X^{n+1}\psi_{\ell}\Phi X^{n+1}\psi_{\ell}\\
=&\>\Delta r^{m}X^{n+1}\psi_{\ell}X^{n+2}\psi_{\ell}+(n+1)\Delta^{\prime}r^{m}(X^{n+1}\psi_{\ell})^{2}\\
&-X\left(\frac{1}{2}[\ell(\ell+1)-n(n+1)]r^{m}(X^{n}\psi_{\ell})^{2}\right)\\
&+\frac{1}{2}m[\ell(\ell+1)-n(n+1)]r^{m-1}(X^{n}\psi_{\ell})^{2}+\Phi(ar^{m}(X^{n+1}\psi_{\ell})^{2}).\end{split}

(7.17)

We integrate over
\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq R_{0}\}
, where
R_{0}\geq r_{+}
will be chosen appropriately large:

-\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq R_{0}\}}X\left(\frac{1}{2}(\ell(\ell+1)-n(n+1))r^{m}(X^{n}\psi_{\ell})^{2}\right)\,d\omega d\uprho=\int_{S^{2}_{\tau,R_{0}}}\frac{1}{2}(\ell(\ell+1)-n(n+1))r^{m}(X^{n}\psi_{\ell})^{2}\,d\omega

if
m<2n+2
.

J=0
:

We consider first the case
J=0
. If
\ell=0
, then (
7.15
) follows immediately from (
7.14
). Suppose
\ell\geq 1
and
n\leq\ell-1
. Then we estimate in
\{r\geq R_{0}\}
:

\displaystyle|\Delta r^{m}X^{n+1}\psi_{\ell}X^{n+2}\psi_{\ell}|\leq

\displaystyle\>\frac{1}{2}\epsilon\Delta r^{m-1}(X^{n+1}\psi_{\ell})^{2}+\frac{1}{2\epsilon}\Delta r^{m+1}(X^{n+2}\psi_{\ell})^{2},

\displaystyle|r^{m}X^{n+1}\psi_{\ell}X^{n}{\pi}_{\ell}F|\leq

\displaystyle\>\frac{1}{2}\epsilon r^{m+1}(X^{n+1}\psi_{\ell})^{2}+\frac{1}{2\epsilon}r^{m-1}(X^{n}{\pi}_{\ell}F)^{2}.

In the case when
m\leq 0
and
n=\ell-1
, we need to additionally apply (
7.10
) in
\{r\geq R_{0}\}
to further estimate:

\begin{split}-\frac{1}{2}m&[\ell(\ell+1)-(\ell-1)\ell]r^{m-1}(X^{\ell-1}\psi_{\ell})^{2}=-\ell mr^{m-1}(X^{\ell-1}\psi_{\ell})^{2}\\
\leq&\>-m(1+\epsilon)\ell[r^{2}+O(r)]r^{m-1}(X^{\ell}\psi_{\ell})^{2}+C_{\epsilon}r^{2m+2}(X^{\ell+1}\psi_{\ell})^{2}+C_{\epsilon}r^{m-1}(X^{\ell-1}{\pi}_{\ell}F)^{2}\\
\leq&\>-\frac{1}{2}m(1+\epsilon)\ell[1+O(r^{-1})]\Delta^{\prime}r^{m}(X^{\ell}\psi_{\ell})^{2}+C_{\epsilon}r^{m+1}(X^{\ell+1}\psi_{\ell})^{2}+C_{\epsilon}r^{m-1}(X^{\ell-1}{\pi}_{\ell}F)^{2}.\end{split}

Hence, for
m>-2
,
R_{0}
appropriately large and
\epsilon>0
appropriately small, we can absorb the first term on the very right-hand side above into the
(X^{\ell}\psi_{\ell})^{2}=(X^{n+1}\psi_{\ell})^{2}
term on the very right-hand side of (
7.17
) with
n=\ell-1
.

Combining the above estimates and restricting
-2<m<2(\ell-1)+2
, we therefore obtain:

\begin{split}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq R_{0}\}}r^{m+1}(X^{\ell}\psi_{\ell})^{2}+r^{m-1}(X^{\ell-1}\psi_{\ell})^{2}\,d\omega d\uprho\leq&\>C\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq R_{0}\}}r^{m+3}(X^{\ell+1}\psi_{\ell})^{2}\,d\omega d\uprho\\
&+C\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq R_{0}\}}r^{m-1}(X^{\ell-1}{\pi}_{\ell}F)^{2}\,d\omega d\uprho.\end{split}

We can now apply (
7.14
) to estimate the right-hand side further and obtain

\begin{split}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq R_{0}\}}r^{m+1}(X^{\ell}\psi_{\ell})^{2}+r^{m-1}(X^{\ell-1}\psi_{\ell})^{2}\,d\omega d\uprho\leq C\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}r^{m-1}(X^{\ell-1}{\pi}_{\ell}F)^{2}+r^{m+1}(X^{\ell}{\pi}_{\ell}F)^{2}\,d\omega d\uprho.\end{split}

We can easily remove the restriction to
r\geq R_{0}
above by applying additionally (
2.20
) together with (
7.6
).

Suppose
\ell\geq 2
. By (
7.10
), we can moreover estimate, for all
n\leq\ell-2
:

(X^{n}\psi_{\ell})^{2}\leq Cr^{2}(X^{n+1}\psi_{\ell})^{2}+Cr^{4}(X^{n+2}\psi_{\ell})^{2}+(X^{n}{\pi}_{\ell}F)^{2},

so we can in fact control all lower-order derivatives:

\begin{split}\sum_{l=0}^{\ell}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}r^{m+1-2l}(X^{\ell-l}\psi_{\ell})^{2}\,d\omega d\uprho\leq C\sum_{l=0}^{\ell}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}r^{m+1-2l}(X^{\ell-l}{\pi}_{\ell}F)^{2}\,d\omega d\uprho,\end{split}

for
-2<m<2\ell
. By rearranging terms, taking
k:=\ell-\frac{m+1}{2}
and expanding the terms in
(rX)^{n}{\pi}_{\ell}F
, we conclude that (
7.15
) must hold for
J=0
.

J\geq 1
:

We consider the case
J\geq 1
. We will carry out an induction argument in
J
. First of all, we have obtained above the
J=0
case. Suppose (
7.15
) holds for some
J\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
. We will show that it also holds for
J
replaced with
J+1
. Consider (
7.17
) with
n=\ell+J+1
. Then we write

\begin{split}\Delta r^{m}&X^{n+1}\psi_{\ell}X^{n+2}\psi_{\ell}+(n+1)\Delta^{\prime}r^{m}(X^{n+1}\psi_{\ell})^{2}\\
=&\>\left[\left(\ell+J+\frac{3}{2}\right)r\Delta^{\prime}-\frac{m}{2}\Delta\right]r^{m-1}(X^{\ell+J+2}\psi_{\ell})^{2}+X\left(\frac{1}{2}\Delta r^{m}(X^{\ell+J+2}\psi_{\ell})^{2}\right).\end{split}

One can easily verify that the factor in front of the first term on the right-hand side is certainly strictly positive when
m<2(\ell+J+1)+2
. We can therefore integrate the right-hand side of (
7.17
) over
\Sigma_{\tau}
to obtain for
m<2(\ell+J+1)+2
:

\begin{split}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}r^{m+1}(X^{\ell+J+2}\psi_{\ell})^{2}\,d\omega d\uprho\leq&\>C\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}r^{m-1}(X^{\ell+J+1}\psi_{\ell})^{2}\,d\omega d\uprho+C\int_{S^{2}_{\tau,r_{+}}}(X^{\ell+J+1}\psi_{\ell})^{2}\,d\omega\\
&+C\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}r^{m-1}(X^{\ell+J+1}{\pi}_{\ell}F)^{2}\,d\omega d\uprho.\end{split}

We then arrive at (
7.15
) with
J
replaced by
J+1
by taking
k=\ell-\frac{m+1}{2}
and applying the induction assumption.

We finally obtain (
7.16
) by expanding out the terms in
(rX)^{n}{\pi}_{\ell}F
.
∎

7.3
Application of the hierarchy of elliptic estimates

We establish below improved energy decay for energies restricted to
\psi_{0}
,
\psi_{1}
and
\psi_{2}
containing additional
r
-weights with negative powers, via an application of an
r^{-2k}
-weighted hierarchy of elliptic estimates from Proposition
7.4
. These are important for establishing almost-sharp pointwise decay in regions of bounded
r
.

Proposition 7.5
.

Let
\delta>0
be arbitrarily small and
K,J\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
. Then there exists a constant
C=C(M,a,R,K,J,\ell,\delta)>0
, such that

\displaystyle\sum_{n=0}^{J}

\displaystyle\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}r^{-2}J^{N}[(rX)^{n}T^{K}\psi_{0}]\cdot\mathbf{n}_{\tau}\,r^{2}d\omega d\uprho

(7.18)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\>C\sum_{\ell\in\{0,2\}}(1+\tau)^{-5+\delta-2K}\left[E_{\ell,J+K+1,\delta}[\psi]+\sum_{j=0}^{J}E_{\ell,K+1,\delta}[N^{j}\psi]\right],

\displaystyle\sum_{n=0}^{1+J}

\displaystyle\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}r^{-3+\eta}J^{N}[(rX)^{n}T^{K}\psi_{1}]\cdot\mathbf{n}_{\tau}\,r^{2}d\omega d\uprho

(7.19)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\>C(1+\tau)^{-8+\eta+\delta-2K}\left(E_{1,J+K+3,\delta}[\psi]+\sum_{j=0}^{1+J}E_{1,K+2,\delta}[N^{j}\psi]\right),

\displaystyle\sum_{n=0}^{2+J}

\displaystyle\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}r^{-1-\eta}J^{N}[(rX)^{n}T^{K}\psi_{2}]\cdot\mathbf{n}_{\tau}\,r^{2}d\omega d\uprho

(7.20)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\>C(1+\tau)^{-8+\eta+\delta-2K}\left(E_{2,J+K+5,\delta}[\psi]+E_{0,J+K+5,\delta}[\psi]+\sum_{j=0}^{2+J}E_{2,K+3,\delta}[N^{j}\psi]+\sum_{j=0}^{2+J}E_{0,K+3,\delta}[N^{j}\psi]\right),

\displaystyle\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}

\displaystyle r^{-1}J^{N}[T^{K}\psi_{\geq 3}]\cdot\mathbf{n}_{\tau}\,r^{2}d\omega d\uprho\leq C(1+\tau)^{-9+\eta+\delta-2K}\left(E_{\geq 3,K+1,\delta}[\psi]+E_{1,K+2,\delta}[\psi]+E_{2,K+2,\delta}[\psi]\right).

(7.21)

Proof.

We start by considering
\psi_{0}
. By (
7.16
) with
k=1
, together with (
2.20
) we have that

\begin{split}\sum_{n=0}^{J}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}r^{-2}J^{N}[(rX)^{n}\psi_{0}]\cdot\mathbf{n}_{\tau}\,r^{2}d\omega d\uprho\leq&\>C\sum_{n=0}^{J}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}(X(rX)^{n}\psi_{0})^{2}+r^{-2}(T(rX)^{n}\psi_{0})^{2}d\omega d\uprho\\
\leq&\>C\sum_{n=0}^{J}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}r^{2}(XT(rX)^{n}\psi_{0})^{2}+(T(rX)^{n}\psi_{0})^{2}+r^{-2}(T^{2}(rX)^{n}\psi_{2})^{2}d\omega d\uprho\\
\leq&\>C\sum_{n=0}^{J}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}(J^{N}[(rX)^{n}T\psi_{0}]+J^{N}[(rX)^{n}T\psi_{2}])\cdot\mathbf{n}_{\tau}\,r^{2}d\omega d\uprho.\end{split}

(7.22)

We conclude that (
7.18
) must hold by applying (
6.10
)
for
\ell=0
and
\ell=2
.

We apply (
7.16
) with
k=\frac{3}{2}-\eta
and then
k=\frac{1}{2}-\eta
, with
\eta>0
appropriately small, for
\ell=1
to obtain:

\begin{split}\sum_{n=0}^{1+J}&\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}r^{-3+\eta}J^{N}[(rX)^{n}\psi_{1}]\cdot\mathbf{n}_{\tau}\,r^{2}d\omega d\uprho\\
\leq&\>C\sum_{n=0}^{1+J}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}r^{-1+\eta}(X(rX)^{n}\psi_{1})^{2}+r^{-3+\eta}((rX)^{n}\psi_{1})^{2}+r^{-3+\eta}(T(rX)^{n}\psi_{1})^{2}d\omega d\uprho\\
\leq&\>C\sum_{n=0}^{1+J}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}r^{1+\eta}(X(rX)^{n}T\psi_{1})^{2}+r^{-1+\eta}((rX)^{n}T\psi_{1})^{2}+r^{-3+\eta}(T^{2}(rX)^{n}\psi_{1})^{2}\\
&+r^{-3+\eta}(T^{2}(rX)^{n}\psi_{3})^{2}d\omega d\uprho\\
\leq&\>C\sum_{n=0}^{1+J}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}r^{3+\eta}(X(rX)^{n}T^{2}\psi_{1})^{2}+r^{1+\eta}((rX)^{n}T^{2}\psi_{1})^{2}+r^{-3+\eta}(T^{2}(rX)^{n}\psi_{3})^{2}d\omega d\uprho\\
\leq&\>C\sum_{n=0}^{1+J}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\left(r^{1+\eta}J^{N}[(rX)^{n}T^{2}\psi_{1}]+J^{N}[(rX)^{n}T\psi_{3}]\right)\cdot\mathbf{n}_{\tau}\,r^{2}d\omega d\uprho.\end{split}

By combining the energy decay estimates with
r^{2-\delta}
weights in (
6.5
) and (
6.8
) with the energy decay estimates from Corollary
6.4
via the interpolation inequality in Lemma
B.1
, we therefore obtain:

\sum_{n=0}^{1+J}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}r^{-3+\eta}J^{N}[(rX)^{n}T^{K}\psi_{1}]\cdot\mathbf{n}_{\tau}\,r^{2}d\omega d\uprho\leq C(1+\tau)^{-8-2\ell+\delta+\eta-2K}\left(E_{1,J+K+3,\delta}[\psi]+\sum_{j=0}^{1+J}E_{1,K+2,\delta}[N^{j}\psi]\right).

We can similarly apply (
7.16
) with
k=\frac{1}{2}+\delta
for
\ell=2
to obtain:

\begin{split}\sum_{n=0}^{2+J}&\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}r^{-1-\delta}J^{N}[(rX)^{n}\psi_{2}]\cdot\mathbf{n}_{\tau}\,r^{2}d\omega d\uprho\\
\leq&\>C\sum_{n=0}^{2+J}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}r^{1-\delta}(X(rX)^{n}T\psi_{2})^{2}+r^{-1-\delta}((rX)^{n}T\psi_{2})^{2}\\
&+r^{-1-\delta}(T^{2}(rX)^{n}\psi_{2})^{2}+r^{-1-\eta}(T^{2}(rX)^{n}\psi_{0})^{2}+r^{-1-\delta}(T^{2}(rX)^{n}\psi_{4})^{2}\,\omega d\uprho\\
\leq&\>\sum_{n=0}^{2+J}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}r^{1-\delta}J^{N}[(rX)^{n}T\psi_{2}]\cdot\mathbf{n}_{\tau}+J^{N}[(rX)^{n}T\psi_{4}]\cdot\mathbf{n}_{\tau}\,r^{2}d\omega d\uprho\\
&+\sum_{n=0}^{2+J}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}r^{-1-\delta}(T^{2}(rX)^{n}\psi_{0})^{2}\,d\omega d\uprho.\end{split}

Note that by (
A.4
) with
h=\psi_{0}
, we can further estimate:

\begin{split}\sum_{n=0}^{2+J}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}r^{-1-\delta}(T^{2}(rX)^{n}\psi_{0})^{2}\,d\omega d\uprho\leq&\>C\sum_{n=0}^{2+J}||T^{2}(rX)^{n}\psi_{0}||^{2}_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma_{\tau})}\\
\leq&\>C\sqrt{\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}r^{-2}J^{N}[T^{2}\psi_{0}]\cdot\mathbf{n}_{\tau}\,r^{2}d\omega d\uprho}\sqrt{\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}J^{N}[T^{2}\psi_{0}]\cdot\mathbf{n}_{\tau}\,r^{2}d\omega d\uprho}.\end{split}

Hence, after applying (
6.10
), (
6.11
) and (
7.18
), we conclude that

\begin{split}\sum_{n=0}^{2+J}&\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}r^{-1-\delta}J^{N}[(rX)^{n}T^{K}\psi_{2}]\cdot\mathbf{n}_{\tau}\,r^{2}d\omega d\uprho\\
\leq&\>C(1+\tau)^{-8-2\ell-2K}\left(E_{2,J+K+5,\delta}[\psi]+E_{0,J+K+5,\delta}[\psi]+\sum_{j=0}^{2+J}E_{2,K+3,\delta}[N^{j}\psi]+\sum_{j=0}^{2+J}E_{0,K+3,\delta}[N^{j}\psi]\right).\end{split}

Finally, we note that by (
7.5
) with
\psi
replaced by
T^{K}\psi
, we can estimate

\begin{split}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}r^{-1}J^{N}[T^{K}\psi_{\geq 3}]\cdot\mathbf{n}_{\tau}\,r^{2}d\omega d\uprho\leq&\>C\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}J^{N}[T^{K+1}\psi_{\geq 3}]\cdot\mathbf{n}_{\tau}\,r^{2}d\omega d\uprho+C\int_{N_{\tau}}r(LT^{K+1}\phi_{\geq 3})^{2}\cdot\mathbf{n}_{\tau}\,r^{2}d\omega d\uprho\\
&+C\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}(J^{N}[T^{K+2}\psi_{1}]+J^{N}[T^{K+2}\psi_{2}])\cdot\mathbf{n}_{\tau}\,r^{2}d\omega d\uprho.\end{split}

Hence, (
7.21
) follows by combining the above equation with the energy decay estimates (
6.5
), (
6.8
) and (
6.9
).
∎

Remark 7.1
.

In the proof of Proposition
7.5
, we applied (
7.16
) with
k=\frac{1}{2}+\delta
in the
\ell=2
case. Note that (
7.16
) in fact applies with
\frac{1}{2}-\eta\leq k\leq\frac{5}{2}-\eta
when
\ell=2
. However, as we already applied an almost-sharp decay estimate for
T^{2}\psi_{0}
, which does not further improve by considering additional weights in
r^{-1}
, we cannot exploit the full hierarchy of elliptic estimates to improve the above decay rate when considering
r^{-5+\eta}J^{N}[(rX)^{n}\psi_{2}]
instead of
r^{-1-\eta}J^{N}[(rX)^{n}\psi_{2}]
.
This is a manifestation of angular mode coupling limiting the sharp decay rate of the
\ell=2
mode.

8
Pointwise decay estimates

We apply the energy decay estimates of Section
6
and
7.3
to obtain
L^{\infty}
estimates for
\psi
,
r\psi
and various higher-order quantities of the form
(rX)^{J}T^{K}\psi
. These pointwise estimates will be used in subsequent sections to determine the
precise
late-time behaviour of
\psi_{\geq\ell}
with
\ell=0,1,2
.

Proposition 8.1
.

Let
\delta>0
be arbitrarily small and
K,J\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
. Then there exists a constant
C=C(M,a,R,\delta,K,J)>0
, such that

\displaystyle||T^{K}\phi_{0}||_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma_{\tau})}\leq

\displaystyle\>C(1+\tau)^{-1-K+2\delta}\sqrt{E_{0,K,\delta}[\psi]},

(8.1)

\displaystyle||(rX)^{J}T^{K}\phi_{0}||_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma_{\tau})}\leq

\displaystyle\>C(1+\tau)^{-1-K+2\delta}\sqrt{\sum_{\ell\in\{0,2\}}E_{\ell,K+J,\delta}[\psi]+\sum_{j=0}^{J}E_{\ell,K,\delta}[N^{j}\psi]},

(8.2)

\displaystyle||\sqrt{r}T^{K}\psi_{0}||_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma_{\tau})}\leq

\displaystyle\>C(1+\tau)^{-\frac{3}{2}-K+2\delta}\sqrt{E_{0,K,\delta}[\psi]},

(8.3)

\displaystyle||T^{K}\psi_{0}||_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma_{\tau})}\leq

\displaystyle\>C(1+\tau)^{-2-K+2\delta}\sqrt{\sum_{\ell\in\{0,2\}}E_{\ell,K+1,\delta}[\psi]},

(8.4)

\displaystyle||(rX)T^{K}\psi_{0}||_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma_{\tau})}\leq

\displaystyle\>C(1+\tau)^{-2-K+2\delta}\sqrt{\sum_{\ell\in\{0,2\}}E_{\ell,K+2,\delta}[\psi]+\sum_{j=0}^{1}E_{\ell,K+1,\delta}[N^{j}\psi]},

(8.5)

Proof.

We obtain (
8.1
) and (
8.3
) by applying (
A.1
) and (
A.3
) with
f=\phi_{0}
and

together with the energy decay estimate (
6.7
).

The estimate (

) follows by applying (
A.4
) with
h=\psi_{0}
in combination with (
6.7
) and (
7.18
). In order to obtain (

) and (

), we instead apply the higher-order energy decay estimate (

) in combination with (

).
∎

Proposition 8.2
.

be arbitrarily small and

. Then there exists a constant

(8.6)

\displaystyle||\sqrt{r}T^{K}\psi_{1}||_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma_{\tau})}\leq

\displaystyle\>C(1+\tau)^{-\frac{5}{2}-K+2\delta}\sqrt{E_{1,K,\delta}[\psi]},

(8.7)

\displaystyle||T^{K}\phi^{(1)}_{1}||_{L^{\infty}(N_{\tau})}

\displaystyle+(1+\tau)^{\frac{1}{2}(1+\delta)}||r^{-\frac{1}{2}(1+\delta)}T^{K}\phi^{(1)}_{1}||_{L^{\infty}(N_{\tau})}

(8.8)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\>C(1+\tau)^{-1-K+2\delta}\sqrt{E_{1,K,\delta}[\psi]},

\displaystyle||(rX)^{J}T^{K}\phi^{(m)}_{1}||_{L^{\infty}(N_{\tau})}

\displaystyle+(1+\tau)^{\frac{1}{2}(1+\delta)}||r^{-\frac{1}{2}(1+\delta)}(rX)^{J}T^{K}\phi^{(m)}_{1}||_{L^{\infty}(N_{\tau})}

(8.9)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\>C(1+\tau)^{-2+j-K+2\delta}\sqrt{E_{1,K+J,\delta}[\psi]+\sum_{j=0}^{J}E_{1,K,\delta}[N^{j}\psi]},

\displaystyle r^{-1}||T^{K}\psi_{1}||_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma_{\tau})}

\displaystyle+||XT^{K}\psi_{1}||_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma_{\tau})}

(8.10)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\>C(1+\tau)^{-4-K+2\delta}\sqrt{E_{1,K+3,\delta}[\psi]+\sum_{j=0}^{1}E_{1,K+2,\delta}[N^{j}\psi]},

\displaystyle||rX^{2}T^{K}\psi_{1}||_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma_{\tau})}\leq

\displaystyle\>C(1+\tau)^{-4-K+2\delta}\sqrt{E_{1,K+4,\delta}[\psi]+\sum_{j=0}^{2}E_{1,K+2,\delta}[N^{j}\psi]}.

(8.11)

Proof.

We note first of all that by a standard Sobolev inequality on
\mathbb{S}^{2}
together with (
2.17
), the
L^{\infty}(\mathbb{S}^{2})
norm of
\psi_{1}
and can be uniformly bounded by its
L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{2})
norm.

We obtain (

) and (

) by applying (

) and (

) with

and

together with the energy decay estimate (

) and (

and (

).

) with

, together with the energy decay estimates

) and (

) and Lemma

. The estimate (

.

Finally, (

8.8
) but via the higher-order energy decay estimates (
7.19
).
∎

Proposition 8.3
.

Let
\delta>0
be arbitrarily small and
K,J\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
. Then there exists a constant
C=C(M,a,R,K,J,\delta)>0
, such that for

:

\displaystyle||rT^{K}\psi_{2}||_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma_{\tau})}\leq

\displaystyle\>C(1+\tau)^{-3-K+2\delta}\sqrt{E_{2,K,\delta}[\psi]},

(8.12)

\displaystyle||\sqrt{r}T^{K}\psi_{2}||_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma_{\tau})}\leq

\displaystyle\>C(1+\tau)^{-\frac{7}{2}-K+2\delta}\sqrt{E_{2,K,\delta}[\psi]},

(8.13)

\displaystyle||T^{K}\phi^{(m)}_{2}||_{L^{\infty}(N_{\tau})}+

\displaystyle(1+\tau)^{\frac{1}{2}+\delta}||r^{-\frac{1}{2}(1+\delta)}T^{K}\phi^{(m)}_{2}||_{L^{\infty}(N_{\tau})}\leq C(1+\tau)^{-1-(2-m)-K+2\delta}\sqrt{E_{2,K,\delta}[\psi]},

(8.14)

\displaystyle||(rX)^{J}T^{K}\phi^{(m)}_{2}||_{L^{\infty}(N_{\tau})}+

\displaystyle(1+\tau)^{\frac{1}{2}+\delta}||r^{-\frac{1}{2}(1+\delta)}(rX)^{J}T^{K}\phi^{(m)}_{2}||_{L^{\infty}(N_{\tau})}

(8.15)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\>C(1+\tau)^{-1-(2-m)-K+2\delta}\sqrt{E_{2,K+J,\delta}[\psi]+\sum_{j=0}^{J}E_{2,K,\delta}[N^{j}\psi]},

\displaystyle\sum_{n=0}^{J+2}||(rX)^{n}T^{K}\psi_{2}||_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma_{\tau})}\leq

\displaystyle\>C(1+\tau)^{-4-K+2\delta}\Bigg(\sqrt{E_{2,K+J+5,\delta}[\psi]+\sum_{j=0}^{2}E_{2,K+J+3,\delta}[N^{j}\psi]}

(8.16)

\displaystyle+\sqrt{E_{0,K+J+5,\delta}[\psi]+\sum_{j=0}^{2}E_{0,K+J+3,\delta}[N^{j}\psi]\Bigg)}.

Proof.

Note that as in the
\ell=1
case the
L^{\infty}(\mathbb{S}^{2})
norm of
\psi_{2}
and can be uniformly bounded by its
L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{2})
norm.

We obtain (
8.12
) and (
8.13
) by applying (
A.1
) and (

) with

and

together with the energy decay estimate (

). Similarly, (

) follows from (

) and (

) with

,

, and (
6.8
).

By applying (

) for

).

Finally, (

).
∎

Proposition 8.4
.

Let
\delta>0
be arbitrarily small and
K,J\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
. Let
Q
denote the Carter operator:

Q=\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}+a^{2}\sin^{2}\theta T^{2}-\Phi^{2}.

Then there exists a constant
C=C(M,a,R,K,J,\delta)>0
, such that for
m=0,1,2,3
:

\displaystyle||T^{K}\phi_{\geq 3}||_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma_{\tau})}\leq

\displaystyle\>C(1+\tau)^{-\frac{7}{2}-K+2\delta}\sqrt{\sum_{j_{1}+j_{2}+j_{3}\leq 1}E_{\geq 3,K,\delta}[Q^{j_{1}}T^{2j_{2}}\Phi^{2j_{3}}\psi]},

(8.17)

\displaystyle||\sqrt{r}T^{K}\psi_{\geq 3}||_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma_{\tau})}\leq

\displaystyle\>C(1+\tau)^{-4-K+2\delta}\sqrt{\sum_{j_{1}+j_{2}+j_{3}\leq 1}E_{\geq 3,K,\delta}[Q^{j_{1}}T^{2j_{2}}\Phi^{2j_{3}}\psi]},

(8.18)

\displaystyle||T^{K}\psi_{\geq 3}||_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma_{\tau})}\leq

\displaystyle\>C(1+\tau)^{-\frac{9}{2}-K+2\delta}\Bigg[\sum_{j_{1}+j_{2}+j_{3}\leq 1}E_{\geq 3,K+1,\delta}[Q^{j_{1}}T^{2j_{2}}\Phi^{2j_{3}}\psi]+E_{1,K+2,\delta}[Q^{j_{1}}T^{2j_{2}}\Phi^{2j_{3}}\psi]

(8.19)

\displaystyle+E_{2,K+2,\delta}[Q^{j_{1}}T^{2j_{2}}\Phi^{2j_{3}}\psi]\Bigg].

We also have the following additional estimates for
\ell=3,4
:

\displaystyle||(rX)^{J}T^{K}\phi^{(m)}_{\ell}||_{L^{\infty}(N_{\tau})}+

\displaystyle(1+\tau)^{\frac{1}{2}+\delta}||r^{-\frac{1}{2}(1+\delta)}(rX)^{J}T^{K}\phi^{(m)}_{\ell}||_{L^{\infty}(N_{\tau})}

(8.20)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\>C(1+\tau)^{-\frac{7}{2}+m-K+2\delta}\sqrt{E_{\geq 3,K+J,\delta}[\psi]+\sum_{j=0}^{J}E_{\geq 3,K,\delta}[N^{j}\psi]},

\displaystyle||r^{2}XT^{K}\psi_{\ell}||_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma_{\tau})}\leq

\displaystyle\>C(1+\tau)^{-\frac{7}{2}-K+2\delta}\Bigg[\sqrt{E_{\geq 3,K+1,\delta}[\psi]}+\sum_{j=0}^{1}\sqrt{E_{\geq 3,K,\delta}[\psi]}\Bigg].

(8.21)

Proof.

In order to bound the
L^{\infty}(\mathbb{S}^{2})
norm of
\psi_{\geq 3}
we consider the Carter operator
Q
and note that:

\begin{split}||\psi_{\geq 3}||^{2}_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{S}^{2})}\leq&\>C\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\psi_{\geq 3}^{2}+|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\psi_{\geq 3}|^{2}+|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{2}\psi_{\geq 3}|^{2}\,d\omega\\
\leq&\>C\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\psi_{\geq 3}^{2}+(\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\psi_{\geq 3})^{2}\,d\omega\\
\leq&\>C\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\psi_{\geq 3}^{2}+(Q\psi_{\geq 3})^{2}+(T^{2}\psi_{\geq 3})^{2}+(\Phi^{2}\psi_{\geq 3})^{2}\,d\omega.\end{split}

Furthermore, since
[T,\square_{g}]=[\Phi,\square_{g}]=[Q,\square_{g}]=0
, all estimates derived for
\psi
automatically hold for
\psi
replaced by
Q\psi
,
T^{2}\psi
or
\Phi^{2}\psi
. It therefore remains only to establish control of the
L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{2})
-norm of
\psi_{\geq 3}
.

We obtain (
8.17
) and (
8.18
) by applying (
A.1
) and (
A.3
) with
f=\phi_{\geq 3}
and
h=\psi_{\geq 3}
together with the energy decay estimate (
6.5
). The estimate (
8.21
) follows similarly by taking
h=rX\psi_{3}
or
h=rX\psi_{4}
and applying the energy decay estimate (
6.10
) with
\ell=3,4
, respectively.

Finally, (
8.19
) and (
8.20
) follow from (
A.5
) with
k=0
and
h=\psi_{\geq 3}
, and (
A.1
), (
A.2
), respectively, combined with (
7.21
).
∎

In the following proposition we establish
improved
decay rates for certain
X
-derivatives of
\psi_{\ell}
compared to the decay of
\psi_{\ell}
itself. These estimates will be crucial for propagating asymptotics of
\psi_{\ell}
from a region near infinity to the rest of the spacetime.

Proposition 8.5
.

Let
\delta>0
be arbitrarily small and
K\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
. Then there exists a constant
C=C(M,a,R,K,\delta)>0
, such that:

\displaystyle||XT^{K}\psi_{0}||_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma_{\tau})}\leq

\displaystyle\>C(1+\tau)^{-3-K+2\delta}\left[\sum_{\ell\in\{0,2\}}\sqrt{E_{\ell,K+2,\delta}[\psi]}+\sum_{j=0}^{1}\sqrt{E_{\ell,K+1,\delta}[N^{j}\psi]}\right],

(8.22)

\displaystyle||X^{2}T^{K}\psi_{1}||_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma_{\tau})}\leq

\displaystyle\>C(1+\tau)^{-5-K+2\delta}\Bigg[\sqrt{E_{1,K+5,\delta}[\psi]}+\sum_{j=0}^{2}\sqrt{E_{1,K+3,\delta}[N^{j}\psi]}+\sqrt{E_{\geq 3,K+2,\delta}[\psi]}

(8.23)

\displaystyle+\sum_{j=0}^{1}\sqrt{E_{\geq 3,K+2,\delta}[N^{j}\psi]}\Bigg],

\displaystyle||r^{2}X^{3}T^{K}\psi_{2}||_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma_{\tau})}+

\displaystyle||rX^{2}T^{K}\psi_{2}||_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma_{\tau})}

(8.24)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\>C(1+\tau)^{-5-K+2\delta}\Bigg[\sqrt{E_{2,K+7,\delta}[\psi]}+\sum_{j=0}^{3}\sqrt{E_{2,K+4,\delta}[N^{j}\psi]}

\displaystyle+\sqrt{E_{0,K+7,\delta}[\psi]}+\sum_{j=0}^{3}\sqrt{E_{0,K+4,\delta}[N^{j}\psi]}

\displaystyle+\sqrt{E_{\geq 3,K+2,\delta}[\psi]}+\sum_{j=0}^{1}\sqrt{E_{\geq 3,K+2,\delta}[N^{j}\psi]}\Bigg].

Proof.

Recall from (
7.8
) that

\begin{split}||X^{\ell+1}\psi_{\ell}||_{L^{2}(S^{2}_{\tau,r^{\prime}})}\leq&\>C\sum_{k=0}^{\ell+1}\sup_{r_{+}\leq r\leq r^{\prime}}||r^{-\ell}(rX)^{k}T\psi_{\ell}||_{L^{2}(S^{2}_{\tau,r})}+C\sum_{k=0}^{\ell}\sup_{r_{+}\leq r\leq r^{\prime}}||r^{-2-\ell}(rX)^{k}T^{2}\psi_{\ell}||_{L^{2}(S^{2}_{\tau,r})}\\
&+Ca^{2}\sup_{r_{+}\leq r\leq r^{\prime}}||r^{-2}X^{\ell}T^{2}\psi_{\ell-2}||_{L^{2}(S^{2}_{\tau,r})}+Ca^{2}\sup_{r_{+}\leq r\leq r^{\prime}}||r^{-2}X^{\ell}T^{2}\psi_{\ell+2}||_{L^{2}(S^{2}_{\tau,r})}.\end{split}

Hence,

\begin{split}||XT^{K}\psi_{0}||_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma_{\tau})}\leq&\>C\sum_{k=0}^{1}||(rX)^{k}T\psi_{0}||_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma_{\tau})}+C||r^{-2}T^{2}\psi_{0}||_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma_{\tau})}+Ca^{2}||r^{-2}T^{2}\psi_{2}||_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma_{\tau})}\\
\leq&\>C(1+\tau)^{-3-K+2\delta}\sqrt{\sum_{\ell\in\{0,2\}}E_{\ell,K+2,\delta}[\psi]+\sum_{j=0}^{1}E_{\ell,K+1,\delta}[N^{j}\psi]}.\end{split}

Furthermore, by (
8.10
) and (
8.11
).

\begin{split}||X^{2}T^{K}\psi_{1}||_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma_{\tau})}\leq&\>C\sum_{k=0}^{2}||r^{-1}(rX)^{k}T\psi_{1}||_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma_{\tau})}+C\sum_{k=0}^{1}||r^{-3}(rX)^{k}T^{2}\psi_{1}||_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma_{\tau})}+Ca^{2}||r^{-2}XT^{2}\psi_{3}||_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma_{\tau})}\\
\leq&\>C(1+\tau)^{-5-K+2\delta}\Bigg[\sqrt{E_{1,K+5,\delta}[\psi]}+\sum_{j=0}^{2}\sqrt{E_{1,K+3,\delta}[N^{j}\psi]}+\sqrt{E_{\geq 3,K+2,\delta}[\psi]}\\
&+\sum_{j=0}^{1}\sqrt{E_{\geq 3,K+2,\delta}[N^{j}\psi]}\Bigg].\end{split}

We apply (
7.9
) and (
8.16
) to obtain:

\begin{split}||r^{2}X^{3}T^{K}\psi_{2}||_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma_{\tau})}\leq&\>C\sum_{k=0}^{3}||(rX)^{k}T\psi_{2}||_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma_{\tau})}+C\sum_{k=0}^{2}||r^{-2}(rX)^{k}T^{2}\psi_{2}||_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma_{\tau})}\\
&+Ca^{2}||XT^{2}\psi_{0}||_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma_{\tau})}+Ca^{2}||XT^{2}\psi_{4}||_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma_{\tau})}\\
\leq&\>C(1+\tau)^{-5-K+2\delta}\Bigg[\sqrt{E_{2,K+7,\delta}[\psi]}+\sum_{j=0}^{3}\sqrt{E_{2,K+4,\delta}[N^{j}\psi]}\\
&+\sqrt{E_{0,K+7,\delta}[\psi]}+\sum_{j=0}^{3}\sqrt{E_{0,K+4,\delta}[N^{j}\psi]}\\
&+\sqrt{E_{\geq 3,K+2,\delta}[\psi]}+\sum_{j=0}^{1}\sqrt{E_{\geq 3,K+2,\delta}[N^{j}\psi]}\Bigg].\end{split}

We obtain an estimate for
||rX^{2}T^{K}\psi_{2}||_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma_{\tau})}
by integrating from
\rho=\infty
.
∎

9
Elliptic theory of time inversion

The aim of this section is to construct a solution
\widetilde{\psi}
to (
3.1
), such that

T\widetilde{\psi}=\psi,

with
\psi
another solution to (
3.1
) arising from smooth, compactly supported initial data on
\Sigma_{0}
. We will denote
T^{-1}\psi=\widetilde{\psi}
. This propositions in this section are self-contained and independent from the estimates in Sections
5
–
8
.

The construction of
\widetilde{\psi}
relies on the invertibility of the differential operator
\mathcal{L}
:

\mathcal{L}f=X(\Delta Xf)+2aX\Phi f+\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}f,

which was introduced in Section
7
, acting on suitable Hilbert spaces.

Let
\mathbf{H}_{k}
denote the completion of the space

C^{\infty}_{\rm rad}(\Sigma_{0}):=\{f\in C^{\infty}(\Sigma_{0})\,|\,(r^{2}+a^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}f\in C^{\infty}(\widehat{\Sigma}_{0})\},

with respect to the norm
||\cdot||_{k}
, defined as follows:

||f||_{k}^{2}:=\sum_{k_{1}+k_{2}\leq k}\int_{\Sigma_{0}}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{k_{1}}(rX)^{k_{2}}f|^{2}\,d\omega d\uprho.

Let
D_{k}(\mathcal{L})
denote the closure of
C^{\infty}_{\rm rad}(\Sigma_{0})
under the norm:

||f||_{k}+||\mathcal{L}f||_{k}.

Then

\mathcal{L}:D_{k}(\mathcal{L})\to\mathbf{H}_{k}

is a densely defined, closed, linear operator.

9.1
Inverting
\mathcal{L}

In this section, we will establish invertibility and regularity properties of
\mathcal{L}
. The strategy for obtaining invertibility of
\mathcal{L}
can be compared to the strategy of obtaining invertibility of the resolvent operators considered in Section 4 of
[
War15
]
. In particular, as in
[
War15
]
, the use of (elliptic) redshift estimates will be important.

The following proposition contains the key estimates that are relevant for invertibility:

Proposition 9.1
.

Let
(r^{2}+a^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}f\in C^{\infty}(\widehat{\Sigma}_{0})
. Then

\int_{\Sigma_{0}}f^{2}+r^{2}(Xf)^{2}+|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}f|^{2}\,d\omega d\uprho\leq C\int_{\Sigma_{0}}(\mathcal{L}f)^{2}\,d\omega d\uprho.

(9.1)

More generally,

||f||_{k}+||\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}f||_{k}+||rXf||_{k}\leq C||\mathcal{L}f||_{k}.

(9.2)

Furthermore,
\mathbf{H}_{k+1}\subset D_{k}(\mathcal{L})
and the equation (
9.2
) holds also for all
f\in D_{k}(\mathcal{L})
.

Proof.

Let
q\in\mathbb{R}
. After applying the Leibniz rule, we obtain the following identity:

\begin{split}r^{q}Xf\mathcal{L}f=&\>\frac{1}{2}X(r^{q}\Delta(Xf)^{2})+\frac{1}{2}\left(\Delta^{\prime}-q\Delta r^{-1}\right)r^{q}(Xf)^{2}+a\Phi(r^{q}(Xf)^{2})+\textnormal{div}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}(r^{q}Xf\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}f)\\
&-\frac{1}{2}X(r^{q}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}f|^{2})+\frac{1}{2}qr^{q-1}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}f|^{2}.\end{split}

(9.3)

Integrating the above identity with
q=1
over
\Sigma_{0}
and using that
(r^{2}+a^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}f\in C^{\infty}(\widehat{\Sigma}_{0})
, we therefore obtain:

\int_{\Sigma_{0}}\frac{1}{2}(r^{2}-a^{2})(Xf)^{2}+\frac{1}{2}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}f|^{2}\,d\omega d\uprho+\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Sigma_{0}\cap\{\uprho=r_{+}\}}r|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}f|^{2}\,d\omega\leq\int_{\Sigma_{0}}r|Xf||\mathcal{L}f|\,d\omega d\uprho

By applying a weighted Young’s inequality to right-hand side and absorbing the resulting
(Xf)^{2}
term into the left-hand side, we are left with:

\int_{\Sigma_{0}}r^{2}(Xf)^{2}+|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}f|^{2}\,d\omega d\uprho+\int_{\Sigma\cap\{\uprho=r_{+}\}}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}f|^{2}\,d\omega\leq C\int_{\Sigma_{0}}(\mathcal{L}f)^{2}\,d\omega d\uprho.

(9.4)

By applying (
2.20
) we obtain (
9.1
).

In order to obtain (
9.2
), we first observe that:

X^{k_{2}}(\mathcal{L}f-k_{2}(k_{2}+1)f)=X(\Delta X^{k_{2}+1}f)+k_{2}\Delta^{\prime}X^{k_{2}+1}f+2a\Phi X^{k_{2}+1}f+\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}X^{k_{2}}f.

We then multiply both sides by
(-1)^{k_{1}}\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{k_{1}}r^{2k_{2}+1}X^{k_{2}+1}f
and apply the Leibniz rule multiple times to obtain:

\begin{split}r^{2k_{2}+1}&\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{s}\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{\frac{k_{1}-s}{2}}X^{k_{2}+1}f\cdot\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{s}\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{\frac{k_{1}-s}{2}}X^{k_{2}}(\mathcal{L}f-k_{2}(k_{2}+1)f)=\left(k_{2}+\frac{1}{2}\right)[\Delta^{\prime}-\Delta r^{-1}]r^{2k_{2}+1}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{s}\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{\frac{k_{1}-s}{2}}X^{k_{2}+1}f|^{2}\\
&+\left(k_{2}+\frac{1}{2}\right)r^{2k_{2}}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{1-s}\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{\frac{k_{1}+s}{2}}X^{k_{2}}f|^{2}-\frac{1}{2}X(r^{2k_{2}+1}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{1-s}\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{\frac{k_{1}+s}{2}}X^{k_{2}}f|^{2})+\ldots\end{split}

where
s=0
if
k_{1}
is even and
s=1
if
k_{1}
is odd, and the terms in
\ldots
on the right-hand side are total derivatives that vanish after integrating over
\Sigma_{0}
. We therefore obtain:

\begin{split}\int_{\Sigma_{0}}&r^{2k_{2}+2}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{s}\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{\frac{k_{1}-s}{2}}X^{k_{2}+1}f|^{2}+r^{2k_{2}}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{1-s}\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{\frac{k_{1}+s}{2}}X^{k_{2}}f|^{2}\,d\omega d\uprho\\
\leq&\>C\int_{\Sigma_{0}}r^{2k_{2}}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{s}\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{\frac{k_{1}-s}{2}}X^{k_{2}}(\mathcal{L}f-k_{2}(k_{2}+1)f)|^{2}\,d\omega d\uprho.\end{split}

(9.5)

We then obtain (
9.2
) via (
2.18
) and a straightforward induction argument in
k_{2}
.

Now suppose
f\in D_{k}(\mathcal{L})
. By definition, there exists a sequence
\{f_{j}\}
such that
(r^{1}+a^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}f_{k}\in C^{\infty}(\widehat{\Sigma}_{0})
and
f_{j}\to f
,
\mathcal{L}f_{k}\to\mathcal{L}f
with respect to
||\cdot||_{k}
. Applying the estimates above to the difference
f_{j}-f_{i}
,
j,i\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, we can conclude that
\{f_{j}\}
is Cauchy with respect to
||\cdot||_{k+1}
and hence the limit
f\in\mathbf{H}_{k+1}
. It follows moreover that (
9.2
) holds for
f\in D_{k}(\mathcal{L})
.
∎

Let
\mathcal{L}^{*}:D_{0}(\mathcal{L}^{*})\to\mathbf{H}_{0}
denote the Hilbert space adjoint operator of
\mathcal{L}
with respect to the standard
L^{2}
norm on
[r_{+},\infty)\times\mathbb{S}^{2}
, i.e.
v\in D_{0}(\mathcal{L}^{*})
if and only if: there exists a
f\in\mathbf{H}_{0}
such that for all
u\in C^{\infty}_{c}({\Sigma}_{0})

\langle\mathcal{L}u,v\rangle_{L^{2}([r_{+},\infty)\times\mathbb{S}^{2})}=\langle u,f\rangle_{L^{2}([r_{+},\infty)\times\mathbb{S}^{2})}

and
\mathcal{L}^{*}v:=f
.

We moreover define the operator
\mathcal{L}^{{\dagger}}:D_{0}(\mathcal{L}^{{\dagger}})\to\mathbf{H}_{k}
, with
\mathcal{L}^{{\dagger}}u=\mathcal{L}u
and where
D_{0}(\mathcal{L}^{{\dagger}})
is the closure of

C^{\infty}_{\rm rad,*}(\Sigma_{0})=\{f\in C^{\infty}_{\rm rad}(\Sigma_{0})\,|\,f|_{r=r_{+}}=0\}

under the norm
||u||_{0}+||\mathcal{L}^{{\dagger}}u||_{0}
.

Lemma 9.2
.

The following identity holds:
\mathcal{L}^{{\dagger}}=\mathcal{L}^{*}
, with
D_{0}(\mathcal{L}^{{\dagger}})=D_{0}(\mathcal{L}^{*})
.

Proof.

We will establish the equivalent statement:
(\mathcal{L}^{{\dagger}})^{*}=\mathcal{L}
with
D_{0}((\mathcal{L}^{{\dagger}})^{*})=D_{0}(\mathcal{L})
.

By definition of the adjoint
(\mathcal{L}^{{\dagger}})^{*}
, we have that for all
u\in C^{\infty}_{\rm rad,*}(\Sigma_{0})
and
v\in D_{0}((\mathcal{L}^{{\dagger}})^{*})
:

\langle\mathcal{L}^{{\dagger}}u,v\rangle_{L^{2}}=\langle u,(\mathcal{L}^{{\dagger}})^{*}v\rangle_{L^{2}}.

(9.6)

Now, let
u\in C^{\infty}_{\rm rad,*}(\Sigma_{0})
and
