v\in C^{\infty}_{\rm rad}(\Sigma_{0})
. Then we can integrate by parts and use that
u|_{r=r_{+}}=0
and
u,v
are compactly supported to obtain:

\langle\mathcal{L}^{{\dagger}}u,v\rangle_{L^{2}}=\langle u,\mathcal{L}v\rangle_{L^{2}}.

(9.7)

Since
\mathcal{L}
and
\mathcal{L}^{{\dagger}}
are closed operators, by construction, we have that (
9.7
) must also hold for
u\in D_{0}(\mathcal{L}^{{\dagger}})
and
v\in D_{0}(\mathcal{L})
. Applying both (
9.6
) and (
9.7
), we can therefore infer that:

D_{0}(\mathcal{L})\subseteq D_{0}((\mathcal{L}^{{\dagger}})^{*})

and
(\mathcal{L}^{{\dagger}})^{*}|_{D_{0}(\mathcal{L})}=\mathcal{L}
.

In order to conclude that
D_{0}(\mathcal{L})=D_{0}((\mathcal{L}^{{\dagger}})^{*})
, it remains to show that for any element
v\in D_{0}((\mathcal{L}^{{\dagger}})^{*})
, there exists a sequence
(v_{k})
, with
v_{k}\in C_{\rm rad}^{\infty}(\Sigma_{0})
such that

||v-v_{k}||_{L^{2}([r_{+},\infty)\times\mathbb{S}^{2})}+||\mathcal{L}(v-v_{k})||_{L^{2}([r_{+},\infty)\times\mathbb{S}^{2})}\to 0\quad\textnormal{as $k\to\infty$.}

(9.8)

First, observe that if
v\in D_{0}((\mathcal{L}^{{\dagger}})^{*})
, then by (
9.6
) combined with (
9.7
),
\mathcal{L}v\in\mathbf{H}_{0}
exists in a weak sense. Then, we can take
v_{k}
to be a convolution of
v
with a suitable mollifier to conclude that (
9.8
) must hold.
∎

Proposition 9.3
.

The operator
\mathcal{L}:D_{k}(\mathcal{L})\to\mathbf{H}_{k}
is invertible and the inverse
\mathcal{L}^{-1}:\mathbf{H}_{k}\to D_{k}(\mathcal{L})
satisfies

||\mathcal{L}^{-1}(F)||_{k}+||rX\mathcal{L}^{-1}(F)||_{k}+||\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\mathcal{L}^{-1}(F)||_{k}\leq C||F||_{k},

(9.9)

for any
F\in\mathbf{H}_{k}
.

Proof.

We first consider
k=0
. By Proposition
9.1
, we have that
\mathcal{L}
is injective, i.e.
\ker\mathcal{L}=\{0\}
. We will conclude that
\mathcal{L}
is bijective by showing that
\textnormal{Ran}\,\mathcal{L}=\mathbf{H}_{0}=L^{2}([r_{+},\infty)\times\mathbb{S}^{2})
.

Let
v\in(\textnormal{Ran}\,\mathcal{L})^{\perp}
and
u\in C_{\rm rad}^{\infty}(\Sigma_{0})
. Then

0=\langle v,\mathcal{L}u\rangle_{L^{2}}=\langle\mathcal{L}^{*}v,u\rangle_{L^{2}},

so
v\in D_{0}(\mathcal{L}^{*})
and moreover
v\in\ker\mathcal{L}^{*}
. Hence,
(\textnormal{Ran}\,\mathcal{L})^{\perp}\subseteq\ker\mathcal{L}^{*}
. Furthermore, if
v\in\ker\mathcal{L}^{*}
, then we can similarly conclude that
v\in(\textnormal{Ran}\,\mathcal{L})^{\perp}
and therefore
(\textnormal{Ran}\,\mathcal{L})^{\perp}=\ker\mathcal{L}^{*}
.

By Lemma
9.2
, we have that
\mathcal{L}^{*}=\mathcal{L}^{{\dagger}}
. By definition of
\mathcal{L}^{{\dagger}}
, we moreover have that
\mathcal{L}^{{\dagger}}|_{C_{\rm rad,*}^{\infty}(\Sigma_{0})}=\mathcal{L}|_{C_{\rm rad,*}^{\infty}(\Sigma_{0})}
, so we can apply Proposition
9.1
again to obtain
\ker\mathcal{L}^{*}=\{0\}
.

Note, by Proposition
9.1
together with the fact that
\mathcal{L}
is closed, it follows that
\textnormal{Ran}\,\mathcal{L}
is closed and hence

\textnormal{Ran}\,\mathcal{L}=(\textnormal{Ran}\,\mathcal{L})^{\perp\perp}=(\ker\mathcal{L}^{*})^{\perp}=\mathbf{H}_{0}.

We conclude that
\mathcal{L}:D_{0}(\mathcal{L})\to\mathbf{H_{0}}
is bijective and we denote its inverse by
\mathcal{L}^{-1}
. The estimate (
9.9
) with
k=0
follows immediately by taking
f=\mathcal{L}^{-1}(F)
in (
9.2
).

We can obtain the
k>0
case by an inductive argument. Suppose we have established
\mathcal{L}^{-1}(F)\in\mathbf{H}_{K}
and (
9.9
) with
k=K
for some
K\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
. Then we can repeat the argument above where in the proof of Lemma
9.2
we replace
\mathcal{L}
by the operator
\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{k_{1}}X^{k_{2}}(\mathcal{L}-k_{2}(k_{2}+1))
, with
k_{1}+k_{2}=1
, applying (
9.5
) instead of (
9.1
) to conclude that in fact
\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{k_{1}}(rX)^{k_{2}}\mathcal{L}^{-1}(F)
, with
k_{1}+k_{2}=1
lies in
\mathbf{H}_{K}
and (
9.9
) holds for
\mathcal{L}^{-1}(F)
replaced by
\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{k_{1}}X^{k_{2}}\mathcal{L}^{-1}(F)
. Hence,
\mathcal{L}^{-1}(F)\in\mathbf{H}_{K+1}
and (
9.9
) holds for
k=K+1
.
∎

By Proposition
9.3
together with a standard Sobolev inequality, we immediately obtain:

Corollary 9.4
.

Let
F\in C^{\infty}_{\rm rad}(\Sigma_{0})
. Then
\mathcal{L}^{-1}(F)\in\bigcap_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}\mathbf{H}_{k}
and in particular
\mathcal{L}^{-1}(F)\in C^{\infty}(\Sigma_{0})
.

9.2
Decay towards
\mathcal{I}^{+}

In this section, we will establish additional
r
-decay estimates of
\mathcal{L}^{-1}(F)
(with suitably decaying
F
) towards
\mathcal{I}^{+}
. We will derive elliptic analogues of the
r^{p}
-weighted estimates from Section
5
.

We first define the following higher-order quantities:

\displaystyle f^{(n)}:=

\displaystyle\>((r^{2}+a^{2})X)^{n}f,

\displaystyle\check{f}^{(0)}:=

\displaystyle\>f,

\displaystyle\check{f}^{(1)}:=

\displaystyle\>[1+(\alpha+\alpha_{\Phi}\Phi)r^{-1}+(\beta+\beta_{\Phi^{2}}\Phi^{2})r^{-2}](r^{2}+a^{2})Xf,

\displaystyle\check{f}^{(2)}:=

\displaystyle\>[1+\gamma r^{-1}](r^{2}+a^{2})X\check{f}^{(1)},

where the coefficients
\alpha,\alpha_{\Phi},\beta,\beta_{\Phi},\gamma,\gamma_{\Phi}
are chosen as in Section
4
.

We moreover observe that

[\mathcal{L},\pi_{\ell}]=0,

so we can independently derive additional decay estimates for
\mathcal{L}^{-1}(F_{\ell})=(\mathcal{L}^{-1}(F))_{\ell}
.

We define the following auxiliary operators:

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathcal{L}}:=

\displaystyle\>(r^{2}+a^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathcal{L}(r^{2}+a^{2})^{-\frac{1}{2}},

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathcal{L}}^{(n)}:=

\displaystyle\>(r^{2}+a^{2})(X(r^{2}+a^{2}))^{n}(r^{2}+a^{2})^{-1}\widehat{\mathcal{L}},

\displaystyle\check{\mathcal{L}}^{(0)}:=

\displaystyle\>(r^{2}+a^{2})\mathcal{L}(r^{2}+a^{2})^{-\frac{1}{2}},

\displaystyle\check{\mathcal{L}}^{(1)}:=

\displaystyle\>(r^{2}+a^{2})^{\frac{3}{2}}X(r^{2}+a^{2})\left[1+(\alpha+\alpha_{\Phi}\Phi)r^{-1}+(\beta+\beta_{\Phi^{2}}\Phi^{2})r^{-2}\right](r^{2}+a^{2})^{-1}\widehat{\mathcal{L}},

\displaystyle\check{\mathcal{L}}^{(2)}:=

\displaystyle\>(r^{2}+a^{2})^{\frac{3}{2}}X(r^{2}+a^{2})[1+\gamma r^{-1}](r^{2}+a^{2})^{-1}\check{\mathcal{L}}^{(1)}.

Lemma 9.5
.

Let
n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, then

\begin{split}(r^{2}+a^{2})^{-1}\widehat{\mathcal{L}}^{(n)}f:=&\>X\left(\Delta(r^{2}+a^{2})^{-1}Xf^{(n)}\right)-2n[1+O_{\infty}(r^{-1})]Xf^{(n)}+2a(r^{2}+a^{2})^{-1}X\Phi f^{(n)}\\
&+(r^{2}+a^{2})^{-1}[\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}-n(n+1)]f^{(n)}+O_{\infty}(r^{-3})[f^{(n)}+\Phi f^{(n)}]\\
&+n\sum_{m=0}^{n-1}O_{\infty}(r^{-2})[f^{(m)}+\Phi f^{(m)}].\end{split}

(9.10)

Furthermore, for
n=0,1,2
:

\begin{split}(r^{2}+a^{2})^{-\frac{3}{2}}\check{\mathcal{L}}^{(n)}f_{n}:=&\>X\left(\Delta(r^{2}+a^{2})^{-1}Xf^{(n)}_{n}\right)-2n[1+O_{\infty}(r^{-1})]Xf^{(n)}_{n}+2a(r^{2}+a^{2})^{-1}X\Phi f^{(n)}_{n}\\
&+\sum_{m=0}^{n}O_{\infty}(r^{-3})[f^{(m)}_{n}+\Phi f^{(m)}_{n}].\end{split}

(9.11)

Proof.

We can write

\begin{split}\mathcal{L}((r^{2}+a^{2})^{-\frac{1}{2}}f)=&\>X\left(\Delta X((r^{2}+a^{2})^{-1/2}f)\right)+2aX(r^{2}+a^{2})^{-1/2}\Phi f)+(r^{2}+a^{2})^{-1/2}\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}f\\
=&\>X\left((r^{2}+a^{2})^{1/2}\Delta(r^{2}+a^{2})^{-1}Xf\right)-X\left(\Delta r(r^{2}+a^{2})^{-3/2}f\right)+2a(r^{2}+a^{2})^{-1/2}X\Phi f\\
&+(r^{2}+a^{2})^{-1/2}\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}f-2ar(r^{2}+a^{2})^{-3/2}\Phi f\\
=&\>(r^{2}+a^{2})^{1/2}X\left(\Delta(r^{2}+a^{2})^{-1}Xf\right)+2a(r^{2}+a^{2})^{-1/2}X\Phi f-2ar(r^{2}+a^{2})^{-3/2}\Phi f\\
&+(r^{2}+a^{2})^{-1/2}\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}f-\left(\Delta r(r^{2}+a^{2})^{-3/2}\right)^{\prime}f.\end{split}

(9.12)

Hence, (
9.10
) follows for
n=0
. The
n>0
case then follows by an induction argument that is analogous to the proof of Proposition
3.3
. Equation (
9.11
) follows by observing that cancellations occur when considering the modified quantities
\check{f}^{(n)}
as in the proof of Proposition
4.1
.
∎

The following proposition contains the elliptic analogues of
r^{p}
-weighted energy estimates:

Proposition 9.6
.

Let
n\in\{0,1,2\}
, then for
f\in C^{\infty}(\widehat{\Sigma}_{0})
and
\delta\in(0,1)
, there exists a constant
C_{\delta}(M,a)>0
, such that

\begin{split}\int_{\Sigma_{0}}&r^{1-\delta}(Xf^{(n)}_{\geq n+1})^{2}+r^{-1-\delta}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}f^{(n)}_{\geq n+1}|^{2}+r^{-1-\delta}(f^{(n)}_{\geq n+1})^{2}\,d\omega d\uprho\\
\leq&\>C_{\delta}\int_{\Sigma_{0}}r^{-1-\delta}\left(\widehat{\mathcal{L}}^{(n)}f_{\geq n+1}-n\sum_{m=0}^{n-1}O_{\infty}(r^{0})[f^{(m)}_{\geq n+1}+\Phi f^{(m)}_{\geq n+1}]\right)^{2}\,d\omega d\uprho\\
&+C_{\delta}\sum_{m=0}^{n}\int_{\Sigma_{0}}((rX)^{m}\mathcal{L}((r^{2}+a^{2})^{-\frac{1}{2}}f_{\geq n+1}))^{2}\,d\omega d\rho\end{split}

(9.13)

and

\begin{split}\int_{\Sigma_{0}}r^{3-\delta}(X\check{f}_{n}^{(n)})^{2}+r^{-1-\delta}(\check{f}_{n}^{(n)})^{2}\,d\omega d\uprho\leq&\>C\int_{\Sigma_{0}}r^{-1-\delta}\left(\check{\mathcal{L}}^{(n)}f-n\sum_{m=0}^{n-1}O_{\infty}(r^{0})[f_{n}^{(m)}+\Phi f_{n}^{(m)}]\right)^{2}\,d\omega\\
&+C\sum_{m=0}^{n}\int_{\Sigma_{0}}((rX)^{m}\mathcal{L}((r^{2}+a^{2})^{-\frac{1}{2}}f_{n}))^{2}\,d\omega d\rho.\end{split}

(9.14)

Proof.

Let
\chi
be a smooth cut-off function such that
\chi(r)=1
for
r\geq R
and
\chi(r)=0
for
r\leq R-M
, where
R>r_{+}+M
will be chosen suitably large. Consider the multiplier
-(r^{2}+a^{2})^{\frac{p}{2}}Xf
. Then by (
9.10
) we obtain

\begin{split}-(r^{2}+a^{2})^{\frac{p}{2}-1}Xf\cdot\widehat{\mathcal{L}}f=&-\frac{1}{2}\Delta^{-1}(r^{2}+a^{2})^{\frac{p+2}{2}}X\left(\Delta^{2}(r^{2}+a^{2})^{-2}(Xf)^{2}\right)-a(r^{2}+a^{2})^{\frac{p-4}{2}}\Phi((Xf)^{2})\\
&+\not{\rm div}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}(\ldots)-\frac{1}{2}(r^{2}+a^{2})^{\frac{p-2}{2}}X(|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}f|^{2})+O(r^{p-3})\chi\Phi fXf+O(r^{p-3})fXf\\
=&\>\left[\frac{p}{2}r^{p-1}+O(r^{p-2})\right](Xf)^{2}+\frac{1}{2}(p-2)[r^{p-3}+O(r^{p-4})]|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}f|^{2}\\
&+O(r^{p-3})\Phi fXf+O(r^{p-3})fXf+\frac{1}{2}(r_{+}^{2}+a^{2})^{\frac{p-2}{2}}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}f|^{2}|_{\mathcal{H}^{+}}\\
&-\frac{1}{2}X\left(\Delta(r^{2}+a^{2})^{\frac{p-2}{2}}(Xf)^{2}\right)-a(r^{2}+a^{2})^{\frac{p-2}{2}}\Phi((Xf)^{2})+\not{\rm div}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}(\ldots).\end{split}

We integrate over
\Sigma_{0}
, applying (
2.20
) and (
9.1
) to control terms in bounded
r
regions, to obtain: for
1\leq p\leq 2

\int_{\Sigma_{0}}r^{p-1}(Xf)^{2}+(2-p)r^{p-3}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}f|^{2}+r^{-2}f^{2}\,d\omega d\uprho\leq C\int_{\Sigma_{0}}r^{p-3}(\widehat{\mathcal{L}}f)^{2}+(\mathcal{L}((r^{2}+a^{2})^{-\frac{1}{2}}f)^{2}\,d\omega d\uprho.

(9.15)

Note that when replacing
f
with
f_{0}=\pi_{0}f
, we can in fact take
p=4-\delta
to obtain:

\int_{\Sigma_{0}}r^{3-\delta}(Xf_{0})^{2}+r^{-1-\delta}f_{0}^{2}\,d\omega d\uprho\leq C\int_{\Sigma_{0}}r^{-1-\delta}(\check{\mathcal{L}}f_{0})^{2}+(\mathcal{L}((r^{2}+a^{2})^{-\frac{1}{2}}f_{0})^{2}\,d\omega d\uprho.

(9.16)

For
n>0
we consider the multiplier
-(r^{2}+a^{2})^{\frac{p}{2}}Xf^{(n)}
together with
(r^{2}+a^{2})^{-1}\widehat{\mathcal{L}}^{(n)}f
, and we proceed analogously to above to obtain:

\begin{split}\int_{\Sigma_{0}}&r^{p-1}(Xf^{(n)})^{2}+(2-p)r^{p-3}[|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}f^{(n)}|^{2}-n(n+1)(f^{(n)})^{2}]\,d\omega d\uprho\\
\leq&\>C\int_{\Sigma_{0}}r^{p-3}\left(\widehat{\mathcal{L}}^{(n)}f-\sum_{m=0}^{n-1}O_{\infty}(r^{0})[f^{(m)}+\Phi f^{(m)}]\right)^{2}+\sum_{m=0}^{n}((rX)^{m}\mathcal{L}((r^{2}+a^{2})^{-\frac{1}{2}}f))^{2}\,d\omega d\uprho,\end{split}

(9.17)

for
0<p<2
.

Suppose
n=1
. Then for
f_{\geq 2}
, the left-hand side of (
9.17
) is non-negative definite by (
2.15
). Furthermore, if we consider (
9.11
) with the multiplier
-(r^{2}+a^{2})^{\frac{p}{2}}\chi X\check{f}_{1}^{(1)}
, we can take
p=4-\delta
to obtain:

\begin{split}\int_{\Sigma_{0}}r^{3-\delta}(X\check{f}_{1}^{(1)})^{2}+r^{-1-\delta}(\check{f}_{1}^{(1)})^{2}\,d\omega d\uprho\leq&\>C\int_{\Sigma_{0}}r^{-1-\delta}\left(\check{\mathcal{L}}^{(1)}f-O_{\infty}(r^{0})[f_{1}+\Phi f_{1}]\right)^{2}\,d\omega\\
&+C\sum_{m=0}^{1}\int_{\Sigma_{0}}((rX)^{m}\mathcal{L}((r^{2}+a^{2})^{-\frac{1}{2}}f_{1}))^{2}\,d\omega d\rho.\end{split}

(9.18)

Similarly, for
n=2
and
f_{\geq 3}
, (
9.17
) has a non-negative definite left-hand side. For
f_{2}
, we consider (
9.11
) with the multiplier
-(r^{2}+a^{2})^{\frac{p}{2}}\chi X\check{f}_{2}^{(2)}
with
p=4-\delta
to obtain:

\begin{split}\int_{\Sigma_{0}}r^{3-\delta}(X\check{f}_{2}^{(2)})^{2}+r^{-1-\delta}(\check{f}_{2}^{(2)})^{2}\,d\omega d\uprho\leq&\>C\int_{\Sigma_{0}}r^{-1-\delta}\left(\check{\mathcal{L}}^{(2)}f-O_{\infty}(r^{0})\sum_{m=0}^{1}[f_{2}^{(m)}+\Phi f_{2}^{(m)}]\right)^{2}\,d\omega\\
&+C\sum_{m=0}^{2}\int_{\Sigma_{0}}((rX)^{m}\mathcal{L}((r^{2}+a^{2})^{-\frac{1}{2}}f_{2}))^{2}\,d\omega d\rho.\end{split}

(9.19)

∎

We can moreover obtain
r^{p}
-weighted energy estimates for higher-order derivatives with respect to
\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}
and
rX
:

Corollary 9.7
.

Let
n\in\{0,1,2\}
and
k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, then for
f\in C^{\infty}(\widehat{\Sigma}_{0})
and
\delta\in(0,1)
, there exists a constant
C_{\delta}(M,a,k)>0
, such that

\begin{split}\sum_{k\leq k_{1}+k_{2}\leq k+1}&\int_{\Sigma_{0}}r^{-1-\delta}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{k_{1}}(rX)^{k_{2}}f^{(n)}_{\geq n+1}|^{2}\,d\omega d\uprho\\
\leq&\>C_{\delta}\sum_{k_{1}+k_{2}=k}\Bigg\{\int_{\Sigma_{0}}r^{-1-\delta}\left|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{k_{1}}(rX)^{k_{2}}\left[\widehat{\mathcal{L}}^{(n)}f_{\geq n+1}-\sum_{m=0}^{n-1}O_{\infty}(r^{0})(f^{(m)}_{\geq n+1}+\Phi f^{(m)}_{\geq n+1})\right]\right|^{2}\,d\omega\\
&+C_{\delta}\sum_{m=0}^{n}\int_{\Sigma_{0}}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{k_{2}}(rX)^{m+k_{2}}\mathcal{L}((r^{2}+a^{2})^{-\frac{1}{2}}f_{\geq n+1})|^{2}\,d\omega d\rho\Bigg\}.\end{split}

(9.20)

and

\begin{split}\int_{\Sigma_{0}}&r^{3-\delta}(X(rX)^{k}\check{f}_{n}^{(n)})^{2}+r^{-1-\delta}((rX)^{k}\check{f}_{n}^{(n)})^{2}\,d\omega d\uprho\\
\leq&\>C\int_{\Sigma_{0}}r^{-1-\delta}\left((rX)^{k}\check{\mathcal{L}}^{(n)}f-nO_{\infty}(r^{0})\sum_{m=0}^{n-1}[(rX)^{k}f_{n}^{(m)}+\Phi(rX)^{k}f_{n}^{(m)}]\right)^{2}\,d\omega\\
&+C\sum_{m=0}^{n}\int_{\Sigma_{0}}((rX)^{m+k}\mathcal{L}((r^{2}+a^{2})^{-\frac{1}{2}}f_{n}))^{2}\,d\omega d\rho.\end{split}

(9.21)

Proof.

We consider
(rX)^{k_{2}}\widehat{\mathcal{L}}^{(n)}f
with the multiplier
-(-1)^{k_{1}}(r^{2}+a^{2})^{\frac{p}{2}}X\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{k_{1}}(rX)^{k_{2}}f
, and
(rX)^{k_{2}}\check{\mathcal{L}}^{(n)}f_{n}
with the multiplier
-(r^{2}+a^{2})^{\frac{p}{2}}X(rX)^{k_{2}}f_{n}
and proceed to integrate by parts over
\mathbb{S}^{2}
, as in the proof of Proposition
9.1
, and apply the arguments of Proposition
9.6
.
∎

In order to establish stronger decay properties of
\mathcal{L}^{-1}(F)
, we introduce the following norms: let
0<\delta<1
, then

\displaystyle||f||_{k,0,0,\delta}^{2}:=

\displaystyle\>\sum_{k_{2}\leq k}\int_{\Sigma_{0}}r^{-1-\delta}((rX)^{k_{2}}f_{0})^{2}\,d\omega d\uprho,

\displaystyle||f||_{k,1,m,\delta}^{2}:=

\displaystyle\>\sum_{k_{2}\leq k}\sum_{n=0}^{m}\int_{\Sigma_{0}}r^{-1-\delta}((rX)^{k_{2}}f^{(n)}_{1})^{2}\,d\omega d\uprho\quad 0\leq m\leq 1,

\displaystyle||f||_{k,2,m,\delta}^{2}:=

\displaystyle\>\sum_{k_{2}\leq k}\sum_{n=0}^{m}\int_{\Sigma_{0}}r^{-1-\delta}((rX)^{k_{2}}f^{(n)}_{2})^{2}\,d\omega d\uprho\quad 0\leq m\leq 2,

\displaystyle||f||_{k,3,m,\delta}^{2}:=

\displaystyle\>\sum_{k_{1}+k_{2}\leq k}\sum_{n=0}^{m}\int_{\Sigma_{0}}r^{-1-\delta}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{k_{1}}(rX)^{k_{2}}f^{(n)}_{\geq 3}|^{2}\,d\omega d\uprho\quad 0\leq m\leq 3.

We denote with
\mathbf{H}_{k,n,m,\delta}
the completions of:
\pi_{n}C^{\infty}(\widehat{\Sigma}_{0})
for
n\in\{0,1,2\}
and
\pi_{\geq 3}C^{\infty}(\widehat{\Sigma})
for
n=3
under the norms
||\cdot||_{k,3,m,\delta}
defined above.

Let
D_{k,n,m,\delta}
, with
n\in\{0,1,2\}
, denote the completion of
\pi_{n}C^{\infty}(\widehat{\Sigma}_{0})
under the graph norm
||\cdot||_{k,n,m,\delta}
with respect to the operator
A
, where
A=\check{\mathcal{L}}^{(0)}
for
n=0
, and when
n\in\{1,2\}
:
A=\widehat{\mathcal{L}}^{(m)}
for
m\leq n-1
and
A=\check{\mathcal{L}}^{(n)}
, for
m=n
.

Similarly, let
D_{k,3,m,\delta}
, denote the completion of
\pi_{\geq 3}C^{\infty}(\widehat{\Sigma}_{0})
under the graph norm
||\cdot||_{k,3,m,\delta}
, with respect to the operator
A
, where
A=\widehat{\mathcal{L}}^{(m)}
.

Then

A:D_{k,n,m,\delta}\to\mathbf{H}_{k,n,m,\delta}

is a densely defined, closed, linear operator.

Proposition 9.8
.

Let
K\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
and let
(r^{2}+a^{2})^{-\frac{1}{2}}F\in\mathbf{H}_{K}
. Then there exists a unique
f\in(r^{2}+a^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{H}_{K}
, such that

\widehat{\mathcal{L}}f=F.

If moreover,

\displaystyle(r^{2}+a^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}\check{F}_{0}\in\mathbf{H}_{K,0,0,\delta},

\displaystyle F_{1}\in\mathbf{H}_{K,1,0,\delta}\quad\textnormal{and}\quad(r^{2}+a^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}\check{F}_{1}^{(1)}\in\mathbf{H}_{K,1,0,\delta},

\displaystyle F_{2}\in\mathbf{H}_{K,2,1,\delta}\quad\textnormal{and}\quad(r^{2}+a^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}\check{F}_{2}^{(2)}\in\mathbf{H}_{K,2,0,\delta},

\displaystyle F_{\geq 3}\in\mathbf{H}_{K,3,3,\delta},

then we can estimate

\displaystyle||f_{0}||_{K,0,0,\delta}+||r^{2}Xf_{0}||_{K,0,0,\delta}\leq

\displaystyle\>C||(r^{2}+a^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}\check{F}_{0}||_{K,0,0,\delta},

(9.22)

\displaystyle||f_{1}||_{K,1,1,\delta}+||r^{2}Xf_{1}||_{K,1,1,\delta}\leq

\displaystyle\>C\left[||F_{1}||_{K,1,0,\delta}+||(r^{2}+a^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}\check{F}_{1}||_{K,1,0,\delta}\right],

(9.23)

\displaystyle||f_{2}||_{K,2,2,\delta}+||r^{2}Xf_{2}||_{K,2,2,\delta}\leq

\displaystyle\>C\left[||F_{2}||_{K,2,1,\delta}+||(r^{2}+a^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}\check{F}_{2}||_{K,2,0,\delta}\right],

(9.24)

\displaystyle||f_{\geq 3}||_{K+1,3,3,\delta}\leq

\displaystyle\>C||F_{\geq 3}||_{K,3,3,\delta}.

(9.25)

Proof.

The existence and uniqueness of
f\in(r^{2}+a^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{H}_{K}
follows directly from Proposition
9.3
. The improved estimates (
9.22
)–(
9.25
) would follow from Corollary
9.7
, if we knew
a priori
that
f
and its appropriately weighted derivatives decayed sufficiently fast towards
\mathcal{I}^{+}
. Since this decay does not follow from the fact that
f\in(r^{2}+a^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{H}_{K}
, we proceed by deriving invertibility of the operators
A:D_{k,m,\delta}\to\mathbf{H}_{k,m,\delta}
, as defined above.

We consider first (
9.22
) with
K=0
. Consider the restricted inverse operator:

\widehat{\mathcal{L}}^{-1}:\mathbf{H}_{0,0,0,\delta}\to\mathcal{L}^{-1}(\mathbf{H}_{0,0,0,\delta})\subseteq(r^{2}+a^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{H}_{0},

Let
F_{0}\in\mathbf{H}_{0,0,0,\delta}
and write
f_{0}:=\widehat{\mathcal{L}}^{-1}(F_{0})
. Let
\{(f_{0})_{j}\}
be a sequence in
C^{\infty}(\widehat{\Sigma_{0}})
defined as follows:

(f_{0})_{j}=f_{0}*\eta_{\frac{1}{j}},

with
\eta_{\frac{1}{j}}
a standard mollifier defined on an extension of
\widehat{\Sigma}_{0}
(where we view
\widehat{\Sigma}_{0}
as a compact subset of
\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}
). Note that
\{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{-\frac{1}{2}}(f_{0})_{j}\}
converges to
(r^{2}+a^{2})^{-\frac{1}{2}}f_{0}
with respect to
||\cdot||_{0}
.

By linearity of
\mathcal{L}
, we also have that
(F_{0})_{j}:=\mathcal{L}(f_{0})_{j}=F_{0}*\eta_{\frac{1}{j}}
, so by
F_{0}\in\mathbf{H}_{0,0,0,\delta}
, it is straightforward to show that:

||F_{0}-(F_{0})_{j}||_{0,0,0,\delta}\to 0\>\textnormal{ as $j\to\infty$}.

Hence,
\{(F_{0})_{j}\}
is Cauchy with respect to
||\cdot||_{0,0,0,\delta}
and we can apply (
9.14
) to the difference
(f_{0})_{j}-(f_{0})_{i}
,
i,j\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, to obtain (in particular) the Cauchy property of
\{(f_{0})_{j}\}
with respect to
||\cdot||_{0,0,0,\delta}
. It follows that
f_{0}\in\mathbf{H}_{0,0,0,\delta}
and we can conclude that (
9.22
) holds with
K=0
.

In order to derive (
9.23
) with
K=0
, we first establish invertibility of
\widehat{\mathcal{L}}:D_{1,0,0,\delta}\to\mathbf{H}_{1,0,0,\delta}
as outlined above and then consider the operator
\check{\mathcal{L}}^{(1)}:D_{1,0,1,\delta}\to\mathbf{H}_{1,0,1,\delta}
. Similarly, we obtain (
9.24
) by first considering
\widehat{\mathcal{L}}:D_{2,0,0,\delta}\to\mathbf{H}_{2,0,0,\delta}
, then
\widehat{\mathcal{L}}^{(1)}:D_{2,0,1,\delta}\to\mathbf{H}_{2,0,1,\delta}
, and finally
\check{\mathcal{L}}^{(2)}:D_{2,0,2,\delta}\to\mathbf{H}_{2,0,2,\delta}
. In order to derive (
9.25
), we analogously establish successively invertibility of
\widehat{\mathcal{L}}^{(n)}
for
n=0,1,2,3
.

Finally, the
K>0
cases follow via an inductive argument as in the proof of Proposition
9.3
.
∎

9.3
Construction of time integral data

If
\psi
is a solution to (
3.1
), then it follows from (
3.3
) that the restriction
\psi|_{\Sigma_{0}}
satisfies the inhomogeneous equation

\widehat{\mathcal{L}}\psi|_{\Sigma_{0}}=F[T\psi|_{\Sigma_{0}}],

with

(r^{2}+a^{2})^{-\frac{1}{2}}F[f]:=2[h\Delta-(r^{2}+a^{2})]Xf+[(\Delta h)^{\prime}-2r]f+[2h(r^{2}+a^{2})-h^{2}\Delta-a^{2}\sin^{2}\theta]Tf+2a(h-1)\Phi f.

Proposition 9.9
.

Consider initial data
(\psi|_{\Sigma_{0}},T\psi_{\Sigma_{0}})
for (
3.1
), with
(\phi|_{\Sigma_{0}},T\phi_{\Sigma_{0}})\in(C^{\infty}(\widehat{\Sigma}))^{2}
.

(i)

Then there exists a unique solution to (
3.1
), denoted
T^{-1}\psi
, such that
T^{-1}\psi\in C^{\infty}(\mathcal{R})
,

T(T^{-1}\psi)=\psi

and
r^{-1}T^{-1}\psi|_{\Sigma_{0}}\in L^{2}(\Sigma_{0})
and
T^{-1}\psi|_{\Sigma_{0}}\in\dot{H}^{1}(\Sigma_{0})
.

(ii)

If we moreover assume that

(I_{0}[\psi],I_{1}[\psi],I_{2}[\psi])=(0,0,0),

(9.26)

then the energy norms appearing on the right-hand sides of the estimates of Section
8
are finite for all
K\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
and
\delta>0
if we replace
\psi
with
T^{-1}\psi
.

Proof.

We can rewrite
F_{0}[\psi|_{\Sigma_{0}}]
as follows in terms of
\phi|_{\Sigma_{0}}
:

\begin{split}F_{0}[\psi|_{\Sigma_{0}}]=&\>2[h\Delta-(r^{2}+a^{2})]X\phi_{0}|_{\Sigma_{0}}+[(\Delta h)^{\prime}-2(h\Delta)r(r^{2}+a^{2})^{-1}]\phi_{0}|_{\Sigma_{0}}+[2h(r^{2}+a^{2})-h^{2}\Delta]T\phi_{0}|_{\Sigma_{0}}\\
&-a^{2}\sin^{2}\theta\pi_{0}(T\phi|_{\Sigma_{0}})\\
=&\>2r^{2}P_{0}+O_{\infty}(r^{-1})[\phi^{(1)}_{0}|_{\Sigma_{0}}+T\phi_{0}|_{\Sigma_{0}}+\phi_{0}|_{\Sigma_{0}}].\end{split}

(9.27)

By combining the equations in Proposition
4.1
with the expressions in Lemma
9.5
, we obtain moreover:

\displaystyle\check{F}^{(1)}_{1}[\psi|_{\Sigma_{0}}]=

\displaystyle\>2[r^{2}+O_{\infty}(r)]P_{1}|_{\Sigma_{0}}+O_{\infty}(r^{-1})\left[\sum_{m=0}^{1}\sum_{j=0}^{1}\phi_{1}^{(2)}+T^{j}\phi^{(1)}_{2m+1}+T^{j}\phi_{2m+1}\right],

(9.28)

\displaystyle\check{F}^{(2)}_{2}[\psi|_{\Sigma_{0}}]=

\displaystyle\>2[r^{2}+O_{\infty}(r)]P_{2}|_{\Sigma_{0}}+O_{\infty}(r^{-1})\left[\sum_{m=0}^{2}\sum_{j=0}^{1}\phi_{2}^{(3)}+T^{j}\phi^{(2)}_{2m}+T^{j}\phi^{(1)}_{2m}+\phi_{2m}\right],

(9.29)

Denote:

T^{-1}\psi|_{\Sigma_{0}}:=\mathcal{L}^{-1}(F[\psi|_{\Sigma_{0}}]).

Then by the regularity established in Corollary
9.4
,
T^{-1}\psi|_{\Sigma_{0}}\in C^{\infty}(\Sigma_{0})
and we denote with
T^{-1}\psi
the solution to (
3.1
) with initial data
(T^{-1}\psi|_{\Sigma_{0}},\psi_{\Sigma_{0}})
. We then have that

T(T^{-1}\psi)=\psi.

Furthermore, by the injectivity properties of
\mathcal{L}
following from Proposition
9.3
,
T^{-1}\psi
must the unique solution to (
3.1
) satisfying the conditions:
T(T^{-1}\psi)=\psi
,
r^{-1}T^{-1}\psi|_{\Sigma_{0}}\in L^{2}(\Sigma_{0})
and
T^{-1}\psi|_{\Sigma_{0}}\in\dot{H}^{1}(\Sigma_{0})
.

Furthermore, by the above expressions
(r^{2}+a^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}F_{0},(r^{2}+a^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}\check{F}_{1},(r^{2}+a^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}\check{F}_{2}\in C^{\infty}(\widehat{\Sigma}_{0})
, if (
9.26
) holds, so we can apply Proposition
9.8
to conclude that for
T^{-1}\phi:=(r^{2}+a^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}T^{-1}\psi
:

\displaystyle(T^{-1}\phi)_{0}|_{\Sigma_{0}},\quad r^{2}X(T^{-1}\phi)_{0}|_{\Sigma_{0}}\in\mathbf{H}_{K,0,0,\delta},

\displaystyle(T^{-1}\phi)_{1}|_{\Sigma_{0}},\quad r^{2}X(T^{-1}\phi)_{1}|_{\Sigma_{0}}\in\mathbf{H}_{K,1,1,\delta},

\displaystyle(T^{-1}\phi)_{2}|_{\Sigma_{0}},\quad r^{2}X(T^{-1}\phi)_{2}|_{\Sigma_{0}}\in\mathbf{H}_{K,2,2,\delta},

\displaystyle(T^{-1}\phi)_{\geq 3}|_{\Sigma_{0}},\quad rX(T^{-1}\phi)_{\geq 3}|_{\Sigma_{0}}\in\mathbf{H}_{K,3,3,\delta}.

for all
K\geq 0
and
\delta>0
and hence all the energies appearing in the estimates of Section
8
are finite if we replace
\psi
with
T^{-1}\psi
.
∎

Corollary 9.10
.

Consider initial data
(\psi|_{\Sigma_{0}},T\psi_{\Sigma_{0}})
for (
3.1
), with
(\phi|_{\Sigma_{0}},T\phi_{\Sigma_{0}})\in(C^{\infty}(\widehat{\Sigma}))^{2}
, such that (
9.26
) holds.

Then the energy decay estimates in Section
6
and the pointwise decay estimates in Section
8
hold when
\psi
is replaced with
T^{-1}\psi
and we can moreover express:

T^{-1}\psi(\tau,\uprho,\theta,\varphi_{*})=-\int_{\tau}^{\infty}\psi(\tau^{\prime},\uprho,\theta,\varphi_{*})\,,d\tau^{\prime}.

.

9.4
Time-inverted Newman–Penrose charges

In this section, we will express the Newman–Penrose charges
I_{0}[T^{-1}\psi]
,
I_{1m}[T^{-1}\psi]
, with
|m|\leq 1
and
I_{2m}[T^{-1}\psi]
, with
|m|\leq 2
, which are defined in Section
4
, in terms of integrals over
F[\psi|_{\Sigma_{0}}]
, where

\begin{split}\mathcal{L}(T^{-1}\psi|_{\Sigma_{0}})=&\>(r^{2}+a^{2})^{-\frac{1}{2}}F[\psi|_{\Sigma_{0}}]\\
=&\>2[h\Delta-(r^{2}+a^{2})]X\psi|_{\Sigma_{0}}+[(\Delta h)^{\prime}-2r]\psi|_{\Sigma_{0}}+[2h(r^{2}+a^{2})-h^{2}\Delta-a^{2}\sin^{2}\theta]T\psi|_{\Sigma_{0}}\\
&+2a(h-1)\Phi\psi|_{\Sigma_{0}}.\end{split}

(9.30)

Definition 9.1
.

We define the time-inverted Newman–Penrose charges
I^{(1)}_{\ell}[\psi]
, with
\ell=0,1,2
as follows:

I^{(1)}_{\ell}[\psi]:=I_{\ell}[T^{-1}\psi].

Proposition 9.11
.

Consider initial data
(\psi|_{\Sigma_{0}},T\psi|_{\Sigma_{0}})
for (
3.1
), with
(\phi|_{\Sigma_{0}},T\phi_{\Sigma_{0}})\in(C^{\infty}(\widehat{\Sigma}))^{2}
, such that moreover
I_{0}[\psi]=0
. Then we can express:

\begin{split}I_{0}^{(1)}[\psi]=I_{0}[T^{-1}\psi]=&M\int_{r_{+}}^{\infty}(r^{2}+a^{2})^{-\frac{1}{2}}\pi_{0}F(\uprho^{\prime})\,d\uprho^{\prime}-\frac{1}{2}\lim_{\uprho^{\prime}\to\infty}\uprho^{\prime}\pi_{0}F(\uprho^{\prime})-\lim_{\uprho^{\prime}\to\infty}[\Delta h-2(r^{2}+a^{2})]\phi_{0}(\uprho^{\prime})\\
&-\frac{1}{2}a^{2}\lim_{\uprho^{\prime}\to\infty}\pi_{0}(\sin^{2}\theta\phi)(\uprho^{\prime}).\end{split}

In particular, if
(\phi|_{\Sigma_{0}},T\phi_{\Sigma_{0}})\in(C_{c}^{\infty}({\Sigma}))^{2}
, then

I_{0}^{(1)}[\psi]=M\int_{r_{+}}^{\infty}(r^{2}+a^{2})^{-\frac{1}{2}}F_{0}[\psi|_{\Sigma_{0}}](\uprho^{\prime})\,d\uprho.

Furthermore, if
(\phi|_{\Sigma_{0}},T\phi_{\Sigma_{0}})\in(C_{c}^{\infty}({\Sigma}))^{2}
, then

\displaystyle I_{1m}^{(1)}[\psi]=I_{1m}[T^{-1}\psi]=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}M\int_{r_{+}}^{\infty}e^{-2iam\int_{r}^{\infty}\Delta^{-1}dr^{\prime}}\Delta X((r^{2}+a^{2})^{-\frac{1}{2}}F_{1m}[\psi|_{\Sigma_{0}}])(\uprho^{\prime})\,d\uprho^{\prime},

\displaystyle I_{2m}^{(1)}[\psi]=I_{2m}[T^{-1}\psi]=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{5}M\int_{r_{+}}^{\infty}e^{-2iam\int_{r}^{\infty}\Delta^{-1}dr^{\prime}}\Delta^{2}X^{2}((r^{2}+a^{2})^{-\frac{1}{2}}F_{2m}[\psi|_{\Sigma_{0}}])(\uprho^{\prime})\,d\uprho^{\prime}.

Proof.

We will suppress in the notation below the restriction
|_{\Sigma_{0}}
. We first consider the projection onto
\ell=0
. The quantity
(T^{-1}\psi)_{0}
satisfies:

X(\Delta X(T^{-1}\psi)_{0})=(r^{2}+a^{2})^{-\frac{1}{2}}F_{0}

and hence, by integrating from
\uprho=r_{+}
, we obtain

X(T^{-1}\psi)_{0}(\uprho)=\Delta^{-1}\int_{r_{+}}^{\uprho}(r^{2}+a^{2})^{-\frac{1}{2}}F_{0}(\uprho^{\prime})\,d\uprho^{\prime}.

Integrating the above expression again, starting from
\uprho=\infty
and using that
\lim_{\uprho\to\infty}(T^{-1}\psi)_{0}(\uprho)=0
by the regularity properties of
(T^{-1}\psi)_{0}
following from Proposition
9.9
, we obtain

\displaystyle(T^{-1}\psi)_{0}(\uprho)=

\displaystyle\>-\int_{\uprho}^{\infty}\Delta^{-1}(\uprho_{1})\int_{r_{+}}^{\uprho_{1}}(r^{2}+a^{2})^{-\frac{1}{2}}F_{0}(\uprho_{2})\,d\uprho_{2}d\uprho_{1}.

It will be convenient to denote

\displaystyle Q_{0}=

\displaystyle\>\int_{r_{+}}^{\infty}(r^{2}+a^{2})^{-\frac{1}{2}}F_{0}(\uprho^{\prime})\,d\uprho^{\prime},

\displaystyle G_{0}(\uprho)=

\displaystyle\>-\int_{\uprho}^{\infty}(r^{2}+a^{2})^{-\frac{1}{2}}F_{0}(\uprho^{\prime})\,d\uprho.

It follows that

G_{0}(\uprho)=-\uprho^{-1}\lim_{\uprho^{\prime}\to\infty}\uprho^{\prime}F_{0}(\uprho^{\prime})+O_{\infty}(\uprho^{-2}),

with
\lim_{\uprho^{\prime}\to\infty}\uprho^{\prime}F_{0}(\uprho^{\prime})
well-defined by Proposition
9.9
.
Then we obtain for
\uprho\geq R
, with
R>r_{+}
:

\displaystyle(T^{-1}\phi)_{0}(\uprho)=

\displaystyle\>-(r^{2}+a^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}\int_{\uprho}^{\infty}[\uprho_{1}^{-2}+2M\uprho_{1}^{-3}+O_{\infty}(\uprho_{1}^{-4})][Q_{0}-\uprho_{1}^{-1}\lim_{\uprho^{\prime}\to\infty}\uprho^{\prime}F_{0}(\uprho^{\prime})+O_{\infty}(\uprho_{1}^{-2})]d\uprho_{1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\>-Q_{0}(1+M\uprho^{-1})+\frac{1}{2}\lim_{\uprho^{\prime}\to\infty}\uprho^{\prime}F_{0}(\uprho^{\prime})\uprho^{-1}+O_{\infty}(\uprho^{-2})

\displaystyle(r^{2}XT^{-1}\phi)_{0}(\uprho)=

\displaystyle\>MQ_{0}-\frac{1}{2}\lim_{\uprho^{\prime}\to\infty}\uprho^{\prime}F_{0}(\uprho^{\prime})+O_{\infty}(\uprho^{-1}).

In order to determine
I_{0}[T^{-1}\psi]
, we need the following relation between
X
,
L
and
T
:

X=2(r^{2}+a^{2})\Delta^{-1}L+(h-2(r^{2}+a^{2})\Delta^{-1})T-2a\Delta^{-1}\Phi.

We conclude that

I_{0}[T^{-1}\psi]=MQ_{0}-\frac{1}{2}\lim_{\uprho^{\prime}\to\infty}\uprho^{\prime}F_{0}(\uprho^{\prime})-\lim_{\uprho^{\prime}\to\infty}(\Delta h-2(r^{2}+a^{2}))\phi_{0}(\uprho^{\prime})-\frac{1}{2}a^{2}\lim_{\uprho^{\prime}\to\infty}\pi_{0}(\sin^{2}\theta\phi)(\uprho^{\prime}).

By rearranging the terms in (
7.11
), we obtain the following expression for general
\ell
:

X(e^{-2iam\int_{r}^{\infty}\Delta^{-1}dr^{\prime}}\Delta^{\ell+1}X^{\ell+1}(T^{-1}\psi)_{\ell m})=e^{-2iam\int_{r}^{\infty}\Delta^{-1}dr^{\prime}}\Delta^{\ell}X^{\ell}F_{\ell m}.

(9.31)

Now consider the projection onto
\ell=1
and
m\in\{-1,0,1\}
and integrate (
9.31
) to obtain:

X^{2}(T^{-1}\psi)_{1m}(\uprho)=\Delta^{-2}e^{2iam\int_{r}^{\infty}\Delta^{-1}dr^{\prime}}\int_{r_{+}}^{\uprho}e^{-2iam\int_{r}^{\infty}\Delta^{-1}dr^{\prime}}\Delta X((r^{2}+a^{2})^{-\frac{1}{2}}F_{1m})(\uprho^{\prime})\,d\uprho^{\prime}.

Integrating the above expression again, starting from
\uprho=\infty
and using that
\lim_{\uprho\to\infty}X^{k}T^{-1}\psi_{1}(\uprho)=0
by the regularity properties of
T^{-1}\psi_{1}
following from Proposition
9.9
, we obtain

\displaystyle X(T^{-1}\psi)_{1m}(\uprho)=

\displaystyle-\int_{\rho}^{\infty}\Delta^{-2}e^{2iam\int_{r}^{\infty}\Delta^{-1}dr^{\prime}}\int_{r_{+}}^{\uprho_{1}}e^{-2iam\int_{r}^{\infty}\Delta^{-1}dr^{\prime}}\Delta X((r^{2}+a^{2})^{-\frac{1}{2}}F_{1m})(\uprho^{\prime})\,d\uprho_{2}d\uprho_{1},

\displaystyle(T^{-1}\psi)_{1m}(\uprho)=

\displaystyle\int_{\rho}^{\infty}\int_{\rho_{1}}^{\infty}\Delta^{-2}e^{2iam\int_{r}^{\infty}\Delta^{-1}dr^{\prime}}\int_{r_{+}}^{\uprho_{2}}e^{-2iam\int_{r}^{\infty}\Delta^{-1}dr^{\prime}}\Delta X((r^{2}+a^{2})^{-\frac{1}{2}}F_{1m})(\uprho^{\prime})\,d\uprho_{3}d\uprho_{2}d\uprho_{1}

By assumption of compact support of
\phi|_{\Sigma_{0}}
, the following expressions are well-defined

\displaystyle Q_{1m}=

\displaystyle\>\int_{r_{+}}^{\infty}e^{-2iam\int_{r}^{\infty}\Delta^{-1}dr^{\prime}}\Delta X((r^{2}+a^{2})^{-\frac{1}{2}}F_{1m})(\uprho^{\prime})\,d\uprho^{\prime}<\infty,

\displaystyle G_{1m}(\uprho)=

\displaystyle\>-\int_{\uprho}^{\infty}e^{-2iam\int_{r}^{\infty}\Delta^{-1}dr^{\prime}}\Delta X((r^{2}+a^{2})^{-\frac{1}{2}}F_{1m})(\uprho^{\prime})\,d\uprho^{\prime}

and it follows that
G_{1m}
has compact support, so in particular:

G_{1m}(\uprho)=O_{\infty}(\uprho^{-2}).

Note moreover that

\left(1-\frac{r_{+}}{r}\right)e^{-2iam\int_{r}^{\infty}\Delta^{-1}dr^{\prime}}=1+O_{\infty}(\uprho^{-2}).

We can therefore express for
\uprho\geq R
:

(T^{-1}\psi)_{1m}(\uprho)=\int_{\rho}^{\infty}\int_{\rho_{1}}^{\infty}[\uprho_{2}^{-4}+4M\uprho_{2}^{-5}+O_{\infty}(\uprho_{2}^{-6})]\left[Q_{1m}+O_{\infty}(\uprho_{2}^{-2})\right]\,d\uprho_{2}d\uprho_{1},

and hence

\begin{split}(T^{-1}\phi)_{1m}(\uprho)=&(r^{2}+a^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}\int_{\rho}^{\infty}\int_{\rho_{1}}^{\infty}[Q_{1m}\uprho_{2}^{-4}+4MQ_{1m}\uprho_{2}^{-5}+O_{\infty}(\uprho_{2}^{-6})\,d\uprho_{2}d\uprho_{1}\\
=&\frac{1}{6}Q_{1m}\uprho^{-1}+\frac{1}{3}MQ_{1m}\uprho^{-2}+O_{\infty}(\uprho^{-3}).\end{split}

We then obtain:

\displaystyle r^{2}X(T^{-1}\phi)_{1m}(\uprho)=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{6}Q_{1m}-\frac{2}{3}MQ_{1m}\uprho^{-1}+O_{\infty}(\uprho^{-2}),

\displaystyle r^{2}X(r^{2}X(T^{-1}\phi)_{1m})(\uprho)=

\displaystyle\>\frac{2}{3}MQ_{1m}+O_{\infty}(\uprho^{-1}).

By using the compactness of the support of
(\phi,T\phi)
, together with (
9.31
) and
\lim_{r\to\infty}(T^{-1}\phi)_{1}=0
, we obtain:

\begin{split}I_{1m}[T^{-1}\psi]=&\>\lim_{r\to\infty}r^{2}X(T^{-1}\check{\phi}^{(1)})_{1m}+ima(T^{-1}\check{\phi}^{(1)})_{1m}\\
=&\>\lim_{r\to\infty}r^{2}X(r^{2}X(T^{-1}\phi)_{1m})-(\alpha+im\alpha_{\Phi})r^{2}X(T^{-1}\phi)_{1m}+imar^{2}X(T^{-1}\phi)_{1m}\\
=&\>\frac{1}{2}MQ_{1m}.\end{split}

Finally, we consider the projection onto
\ell=2
and
m\in\{-,2,\ldots,2\}
and integrate (
9.31
) to obtain:

X^{3}(T^{-1}\psi)_{2m}(\uprho)=\Delta^{-3}e^{2iam\int_{r}^{\infty}\Delta^{-1}dr^{\prime}}\int_{r_{+}}^{\uprho}e^{-2iam\int_{r}^{\infty}\Delta^{-2}dr^{\prime}}\Delta^{2}X^{2}((r^{2}+a^{2})^{-\frac{1}{2}}F_{2m})(\uprho^{\prime})\,d\uprho^{\prime}.

Integrating the above expression multiple times, starting from
\uprho=\infty
and using that
\lim_{\uprho\to\infty}X^{k}(T^{-1}\psi)_{2}(\uprho)=0
by the regularity properties of
(T^{-1}\psi)_{2}
following from Proposition
9.9
, we obtain

(T^{-1}\psi)_{2m}(\uprho)=-\int_{\rho}^{\infty}\int_{\rho_{1}}^{\infty}\int_{\rho_{2}}^{\infty}\Delta^{-3}e^{2iam\int_{r}^{\infty}\Delta^{-1}dr^{\prime}}\int_{r_{+}}^{\uprho_{3}}e^{-2iam\int_{r}^{\infty}\Delta^{-1}dr^{\prime}}\Delta^{2}X^{2}((r^{2}+a^{2})^{-\frac{1}{2}}F_{2m})(\uprho^{\prime})\,d\uprho_{4}\ldots d\uprho_{1}.

By assumption of compact support of
\phi|_{\Sigma_{0}}
, the following expressions are well-defined

\displaystyle Q_{2m}=

\displaystyle\>\int_{r_{+}}^{\infty}e^{-2iam\int_{r}^{\infty}\Delta^{-1}dr^{\prime}}\Delta^{2}X^{2}((r^{2}+a^{2})^{-\frac{1}{2}}F_{2m})(\uprho^{\prime})\,d\uprho^{\prime}<\infty,

\displaystyle G_{2m}(\uprho)=

\displaystyle\>-\int_{\uprho}^{\infty}e^{-2iam\int_{r}^{\infty}\Delta^{-1}dr^{\prime}}\Delta^{2}X^{2}((r^{2}+a^{2})^{-\frac{1}{2}}F_{2m})(\uprho^{\prime})\,d\uprho^{\prime}

and it follows that
G_{2m}
is compactly supported, so in particular:

G_{2m}(\uprho)=O_{\infty}(\uprho^{-2}).

We can therefore express

\begin{split}(T^{-1}\phi)_{2m}(\uprho)=&-(r^{2}+a^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}\int_{\rho}^{\infty}\int_{\rho_{1}}^{\infty}\int_{\rho_{2}}^{\infty}Q_{2m}\uprho_{3}^{-6}+6MQ_{2m}\uprho_{3}^{-7}+O_{\infty}(\uprho_{3}^{-8})\,d\uprho_{3}d\uprho_{2}d\uprho_{1}\\
=&-\frac{1}{60}Q_{2m}\uprho^{-2}-\frac{1}{20}MQ_{2m}\uprho^{-3}+O_{\infty}(\uprho^{-4}).\end{split}

We then obtain:

\displaystyle r^{2}X(T^{-1}\phi)_{2m}(\uprho)=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{30}Q_{2m}\uprho^{-1}+\frac{3}{20}MQ_{2m}\uprho^{-2}+O_{\infty}(\uprho^{-3}),

\displaystyle(r^{2}X)^{2}(T^{-1}\phi)_{2m}(\uprho)=

\displaystyle\>-\frac{1}{30}Q_{2m}-\frac{3}{10}MQ_{2m}\uprho^{-1}+O_{\infty}(\uprho^{-2}),

\displaystyle(r^{2}X)^{3}(T^{-1}\phi)_{2m}(\uprho)=

\displaystyle\>\frac{3}{10}MQ_{2m}+O_{\infty}(\uprho^{-1}).

We use that
\lim_{r\to\infty}(T^{-1}\phi)_{2}=\lim_{r\to\infty}r^{2}X(T^{-1}\phi)_{2}=0
together with compactness of the support of
(\phi,T\phi)
to express:

\begin{split}I_{2m}[T^{-1}\psi]=&\>\lim_{r\to\infty}r^{2}X(T^{-1}\check{\phi}^{(2)})_{2m}+ima(T^{-1}\check{\phi}^{(2)})_{2m}\\
=&\>\lim_{r\to\infty}(r^{2}X)^{3}T^{-1}\phi_{2m}-(\gamma+\alpha+im\alpha_{\Phi})(r^{2}X)^{2}T^{-1}\phi_{2m}+ima(r^{2}X)^{2}(T^{-1}\phi)_{2m}\\
=&\>\frac{1}{5}MQ_{2m}.\end{split}

∎

Remark 9.1
.

One can generalize the argument in the proof of Proposition
9.11
in order to define
I_{\ell m}[T^{-1}\psi]
for
\ell=1,2
when the initial data
(\psi|_{\Sigma_{0}},T\psi|_{\Sigma_{0}})
is not compactly supported, but satisfies
(\phi|_{\Sigma_{0}},T\phi_{\Sigma_{0}})\in(C^{N}(\widehat{\Sigma}))^{2}
, for some suitably large
N
, together with the conditions:
I_{\ell}[\psi]=0
, as in the
\ell=0
case. In this setting, the expressions for
I_{\ell m}[T^{-1}\psi]
will be considerably more complicated than in the case of compactly supported initial data, so we do not pursue this generalization here.

In the corollary below, we obtain several simplified expressions for the time-inverted Newman–Penrose charges
I_{\ell m}^{(1)}[\psi]
with
\ell=0,1,2
.

Corollary 9.12
.

Let
(\phi|_{\Sigma_{0}},T\phi_{\Sigma_{0}})\in(C_{c}^{\infty}({\Sigma}))^{2}
.

(i)

We can express:

\displaystyle I_{0}[T^{-1}\psi]=

\displaystyle\>=\frac{1}{4\pi}M(r_{+}^{2}+a^{2})\int_{\Sigma_{0}\cap\mathcal{H}^{+}}\psi\,d\omega+\frac{1}{4\pi}M\int_{\Sigma_{0}}\mathbf{n}_{0}(\psi)\,d\mu_{0}.

(ii)

We alternatively express
I_{i}[T^{-1}\psi]
, with
i=1,2,3
as integrals along
\mathcal{I}^{+}
:

\displaystyle I_{0}^{(1)}[\psi]=

\displaystyle\>M\int_{0}^{\infty}\phi_{0}|_{\mathcal{I}^{+}}(\tau)\,d\tau

\displaystyle I_{1}^{(1)}[\psi](\theta,\varphi_{*})=

\displaystyle\>3M\int_{0}^{\infty}r^{2}X\phi_{1}|_{\mathcal{I}^{+}}(\tau,\theta,\varphi_{*})\,d\tau,

\displaystyle I_{2}^{(1)}[\psi](\theta,\varphi_{*})=

\displaystyle\>6M\int_{0}^{\infty}(r^{2}X)^{2}\phi_{2}|_{\mathcal{I}^{+}}(\tau,\theta,\varphi_{*})\,d\tau.

Proof.

In order to obtain (i), we first observe that

\rho^{2}d\tau^{\sharp}=(r^{2}+a^{2}-h\Delta)X+[a^{2}\sin^{2}\theta+h^{2}\Delta-2h(r^{2}+a^{2})]T+a(1-h)\Phi.

We can therefore write

(r^{2}+a^{2})^{-\frac{1}{2}}F[\psi|_{\Sigma_{0}}]=-\rho^{2}d\tau^{\sharp}(\psi)|_{\Sigma_{0}}+X\left[(h\Delta-(r^{2}+a^{2}))\psi\right]-a(1-h)\Phi(\psi).

Recall from (
2.10
) that

-\rho^{2}\sin\theta d\tau^{\sharp}(\psi)|_{\Sigma_{0}}=\mathbf{n}_{0}(\psi)\sqrt{\det g|_{\Sigma_{0}}}.

The expression in (i) now immediately follows.

We consider now (ii). Note that the quantities
Q_{0}
and
Q_{im}
, with
i=1,2
, which are defined in the proof of Proposition
9.11
, can be expressed as limits of projected radiation fields of
T^{-1}\psi
:

\displaystyle(T^{-1}\phi)_{0}|_{\Sigma_{0}}(\infty)=

\displaystyle-Q_{0},

\displaystyle r^{2}X(T^{-1}\phi)_{1m}|_{\Sigma_{0}}(\infty)=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{6}Q_{1m},

\displaystyle(r^{2}X)^{2}(T^{-1}\phi)_{2m}|_{\Sigma_{0}}(\infty)=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{30}Q_{2m}.

Using that

(T^{-1}\phi)_{0}|_{\mathcal{I}^{+}},r^{2}X(T^{-1}\phi)_{1m}|_{\mathcal{I}^{+}},(r^{2}X)^{2}(T^{-1}\phi)_{2m}|_{\mathcal{I}^{+}}\to 0

as
\tau\to\infty
, by the decay estimates established in Propositions
8.1
–
8.4
applied to
T^{-1}\psi
, we can integrate
\phi_{0}|_{\mathcal{I}^{+}},r^{2}X\phi_{1m}|_{\mathcal{I}^{+}},(r^{2}X)^{2}\phi_{2m}|_{\mathcal{I}^{+}}
to obtain (ii).
∎

10
Late-time polynomial tails: the
\ell=0
projection

We derive in this section the precise leading-order behaviour in time of
\psi_{\geq\ell}
, with
\ell=0,1,2
, which will take the form of inverse polynomial tails.

We introduce the following additional initial data quantities: for
0<\beta\leq 1
and
R_{0}>r_{+}
arbitrarily large, we define

D_{0,\beta,K}[\psi]:=\max_{1\leq k\leq K}\left|\left|v^{2+\beta+k}L^{k}\left(P_{0}-2I_{0}[\psi]v^{-2}\right)\right|\right|_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma_{0}\cap\{r\geq R_{0}\})}.

We moreover introduce the following auxiliary norm on
\Sigma_{\tau}
: for
0<\delta<1
, we define

\begin{split}S_{0,\delta,K}[\psi]:=&\>\sup_{\tau\geq 0}\Bigg[(1+\tau)^{1-\delta}\sum_{k_{1}+k_{2}\leq K}(1+\tau)^{k_{2}}||(rL)^{k_{1}}T^{k_{2}}\phi_{0}||_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma_{\tau})}\\
&+a^{2}(1+\tau)^{2-\delta}\sum_{\ell\in\{0,2\}}\sum_{k_{1}+k_{2}\leq K+1,k_{2}\leq K}(1+\tau)^{k_{2}}||(rL)^{k_{1}}T^{k_{2}+1}\phi_{\ell}||_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma_{\tau})}\Bigg].\end{split}

The lemma below establishes boundedness of
S_{0,\delta,K}[\psi]
in terms of the weighted initial data energy norms defined in Section
6
.

Lemma 10.1
.

There exists a constant
C=C(M,a,\delta,K)>0
such that:

S_{0,\delta,K}[\psi]\leq C\sqrt{\sum_{k+j\leq K+2}E_{0,k,\frac{\delta}{2}}[N^{j}\psi]+E_{2,k,\frac{\delta}{2}}[N^{j}\psi]}.

Proof.

The estimate follows immediately by applying the estimates in Proposition
8.1
and
8.3
.
∎

We will consider in this section the timelike hypersurfaces
\gamma^{\alpha}=\{v-u=v^{\alpha}\}
, with
\alpha<1
and the following subsets of
\{r\geq R\}
, with
R>r_{+}
suitably large:

\mathcal{A}_{\gamma^{\alpha}}=\{(u,v,\theta,\varphi_{*})\in\mathcal{R}\cap\{r\geq R\}\,|\,u\leq v-v^{\alpha}\}.

Let us moreover introduce the notation
(u,v_{\gamma^{\alpha}}(u),\theta,\varphi_{*})
and
(u_{\gamma^{\alpha}}(v),v,\theta,\varphi_{*})
for points on the curve
\gamma^{\alpha}
.

Figure 3
:
A 2-dimensional representation of the spacetime region
\mathcal{A}_{\gamma^{\alpha}}
.

10.1
Asymptotics in
\mathcal{A}_{\gamma^{\alpha}}

The following relation between
r
and the variables
u
and
v
will be important:

Lemma 10.2
.

Let
R_{0}>r_{+}
. Then there exists constants
c=c(M,a,R_{0})
and
C=C(M,a,R_{0})
, such that in the region
r\geq R_{0}
:

cM\log\left(1+\frac{|v-u|}{M}\right)\leq\left|r-\frac{v-u}{2}\right|\leq CM\log\left(1+\frac{|v-u|}{M}\right).

(10.1)

Proof.

We recall that
v-u=2r_{*}
and

\frac{dr_{*}}{dr}=\frac{r^{2}+a^{2}}{\Delta}.

Solutions
r_{*}
have the following form:

r_{*}(r)=r+2M\log r+O_{\infty}(r^{0}).

The estimates in (
10.1
) then follows immediately.
∎

Proposition 10.3
.

Let
\alpha_{K}>\frac{3+K}{2+K}
. Then there exists
\delta(\alpha_{K})>0
suitably small, such that

\left|L^{K}P_{0}(u,v)-2I_{0}[\psi]L^{k}(v^{-2})\right|\leq CS_{0,\delta,K}[\psi]v^{-(3+K-2\delta)\alpha_{K}}+CD_{0,\beta,K}[\psi]v^{-2-K-\beta}.

(10.2)

Proof.

Let
K\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
. By acting with
L^{K}
on both sides of (
4.10
), we obtain:

4\underline{L}L^{K}P_{0}=\sum_{k=0}^{K}O_{\infty}(r^{-3-K})(rL)^{k}\phi_{0}+\sum_{k=0}^{K+1}O_{\infty}(r^{-3-K})[(rL)^{k}T\phi_{2,0}+(rL)^{k}T\phi_{0}].

(10.3)

We can estimate

||\underline{L}L^{K}P_{0}||_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma_{\tau)}}\leq C(1+\tau)^{-1+\delta}r^{-3-K}S_{0,\delta,K}[\psi].

(10.4)

Integrating from
\Sigma_{0}
in the
\underline{L}
direction, applying (
10.1
) together with (
10.4
), we obtain

\begin{split}\left|L^{K}P_{0}(u,v)-L^{K}P_{0}(u_{\Sigma_{0}}(v),v)\right|\leq&\>CS_{0,\delta,K}[\psi]\int_{u_{\Sigma_{0}}(v)}^{u}(1+\tau)^{-1+\delta}(u^{\prime},v)r^{-3-K}(u^{\prime},v)\,du^{\prime}\\
\leq&\>CS_{0,\delta,K}[\psi]\int_{u_{\Sigma_{0}}(v)}^{u}u^{\prime-1+\delta}(v-u^{\prime})^{-3-K}\,du^{\prime}\\
\leq&\>CS_{0,\delta,K}[\psi]v^{-(3+K-2\delta)\alpha_{K}}\int_{u_{\Sigma_{0}}(v)}^{u}u^{\prime-1-\delta}\,du^{\prime}\\
\leq&\>CS_{0,\delta,K}[\psi]v^{-(3+K-2\delta)\alpha_{K}}.\end{split}

Furthermore, by definition of
D_{0,\beta,K}[\psi]
:

\left|L^{K}P_{0}(u_{\Sigma_{0}}(v),v)-2I_{0}[\psi]L^{K}(v^{-2})\right|\leq Cv^{-2-K-\beta}D_{0,\beta,K}[\psi].

By combining the above, we obtain (
10.4
).
∎

Corollary 10.4
.

Let
\alpha_{K}>\frac{2+K}{3+K}
. Then there exists
\delta(\alpha_{K})>0
suitably small, such that

\begin{split}&\left|LT^{K}\phi_{0}(u,v)-2I_{0}[\psi]L^{K}(v^{-2})\right|\leq CS_{0,\delta,K}[\psi]\Bigg(v^{-(3+K-2\delta)\alpha_{K}}+u^{-K+\delta}(v-u)^{-3}+u^{-1+\delta}(v-u)^{-3-K}\\
&+u^{-2-K+\delta}(v-u)^{-2}\Bigg)+CD_{0,\beta,K}[\psi]v^{-2-K-\beta}.\end{split}

(10.5)

Proof.

It follows from a straightforward inductive argument that we can express

T^{K}P_{0}=L^{K}P_{0}+\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\underline{L}L^{k}T^{K-1-k}P_{0}.

Furthermore, we have that

\begin{split}\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}|\underline{L}L^{k}T^{K-1-k}P_{0}|(u,v)\leq&\>C\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}u^{-(K-k)+\delta}r^{-3-k}S_{0,\delta,K}[\psi]\\
\leq&\>S_{0,\delta,K}[\psi][u^{-K+\delta}r^{-3}+u^{-1+\delta}r^{-3-K}].\end{split}

Now, recall that

P_{0}=L\phi_{0}-\frac{1}{4}a^{2}\frac{\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\pi_{0}(\sin^{2}\theta T\phi),

so we can estimate

\begin{split}|LT^{K}\phi_{0}(u,v)-T^{K}P_{0}(u,v)|\leq&\>CS_{0,\delta,K}[\psi](v-u)^{-2}u^{-2-K+\delta}.\end{split}

The estimate (
10.5
) follows by combining the above and applying (
10.1
).
∎

Corollary 10.5
.

For
\alpha_{K}^{\prime}
suitably close to 1,
\delta>0
suitably small and
0<\beta\leq 1
suitably large, there exists
\nu>0
and a constant
C=C(M,a,\alpha_{K}^{\prime},\nu,\delta)>0
such that

\begin{split}\left|T^{K}\psi_{0}(u,v)-4I_{0}[\psi]T^{K}\left(\frac{1}{uv}\right)\right|\leq&\>C\left(\sqrt{E_{0,K,\delta}[\psi]}+S_{0,\delta,K}[\psi]+D_{0,\beta,K}[\psi]+|I_{0}[\psi]|\right)v^{-1}u^{-1-K-\nu},\end{split}

(10.6)

for all
(u,v,\theta,\varphi)\in\mathcal{A}_{\gamma^{\alpha_{K}^{\prime}}}
.

In particular,

\displaystyle\left|T^{K}\phi_{0}|_{\mathcal{I}^{+}}-2I_{0}[\psi]T^{K}\left(u^{-1}\right)\right|\leq

\displaystyle\>C\left(S_{0,\delta,K}[\psi]+D_{0,\beta,K}[\psi]+|I_{0}[\psi]|+\sqrt{E_{0,K,\delta}[\psi]}\right)u^{-1-K-\nu},

(10.7)

\displaystyle\left|T^{K}\psi_{0}|_{\gamma^{\alpha_{K}^{\prime}}}-4I_{0}[\psi]T^{K}((1+\tau)^{-2})\right|\leq

\displaystyle\>C\left(S_{0,\delta,K}[\psi]+D_{0,\beta,K}[\psi]+|I_{0}[\psi]|+\sqrt{E_{0,K,\delta}[\psi]}\right)(1+\tau)^{-1-K-\nu}.

(10.8)

Proof.

Observe first of all that for
K\geq 1
:

\int_{v_{\gamma^{\alpha_{K}}(u)}}^{v}L^{K}(v^{-2})\,dv^{\prime}=L^{K-1}(v^{-2})-L^{K-1}(v^{\prime-2})|_{v^{\prime}=v_{\gamma^{\alpha_{K}}(u)}}

with

|v_{\gamma^{\alpha_{K}}(u)}^{-1-K}-u^{-1-K}|\leq Cu^{-2-K+\alpha_{k}}

and

L^{K-1}(v^{-2})-L^{K-1}(v^{\prime-2})|_{v^{\prime}=u}=T^{K}\left(\frac{1}{u}-\frac{1}{v}\right)=(v-u)T^{K}\left(\frac{1}{uv}\right).

Using the above together with (
10.5
) and taking
\alpha_{K}
suitably close to 1, we can integrate
LT^{K}\phi_{0}
to obtain

\begin{split}&\left|T^{K}\phi_{0}(u,v)-T^{K}\phi_{0}(u,v_{\gamma^{\alpha_{K}}(u)})-2I_{0}[\psi](v-u)T^{K}\left(\frac{1}{uv}\right)\right|\\
\leq&\>C\left(S_{0,\delta,K}[\psi]+D_{0,\beta,K}[\psi]+|I_{0}[\psi]|\right)u^{-1-K-\nu},\end{split}

for some
\nu>0
.

By (
8.3
), the following holds: given
\delta^{\prime}>0
arbitrarily small, there exists a constant
C>0
such that

\begin{split}|T^{K}\phi_{0}|(u,v_{\gamma^{\alpha_{K}}(u)})\leq&\>Cr^{\frac{1}{2}}(u,v_{\gamma^{\alpha_{K}}(u)})u^{-\frac{3}{2}-K+2\delta^{\prime}}\sqrt{E_{0,K,\delta^{\prime}}[\psi]}\\
\leq&\>C\sqrt{E_{0,K}[\psi]}u^{-\frac{3}{2}+\frac{1}{2}\alpha_{k}-K+\delta^{\prime}},\end{split}

so that

\begin{split}\left|T^{K}\phi_{0}(u,v)-2I_{0}[\psi]T^{K}\left(\frac{v-u}{uv}\right)\right|\leq&\>C\left(S_{0,\delta,K}[\psi]+D_{0,\beta,K}[\psi]+|I_{0}[\psi]|+\sqrt{E_{0,K,\delta}[\psi]}\right)u^{-1-K-\nu},\end{split}

for
\delta>0
suitably small.

By dividing the above equation by
r
, we moreover obtain the following estimate in the
smaller
region
\mathcal{A}_{\gamma^{\alpha_{K}^{\prime}}}
, with
\alpha_{K}^{\prime}>\alpha_{K}
suitably close to 1, then there exists a constant
\nu>0
such that

\begin{split}\left|T^{K}\psi_{0}(u,v)-4I_{0}[\psi]T^{K}\left(\frac{1}{uv}\right)\right|\leq&\>C\left(S_{0,\delta,K}[\psi]+D_{0,\beta,K}[\psi]+|I_{0}[\psi]|+\sqrt{E_{0,K,\delta}[\psi]}\right)v^{-1}u^{-1-K-\nu}.\end{split}

∎

10.2
Asymptotics in
\mathcal{R}\setminus\mathcal{A}_{\gamma^{\alpha}}

In this section, we
propagate
the late-time asymptotics derived in Corollary
10.5
to the rest of the spacetime.

Proposition 10.6
.

Let
K\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
and let
0<\alpha<1
be sufficiently close to
1
. Then there exists a constant
C>0
such that

\begin{split}&\left|T^{K}\psi_{0}-4I_{0}[\psi]T^{K}((1+\tau)^{-2})\right|(\tau,\uprho)\\
\leq&\>C(1+\tau)^{-2-K-\nu}\left(S_{0,\delta,K}[\psi]+D_{0,\beta,K}[\psi]+|I_{0}[\psi]|+\sum_{\ell\in\{0,2\}}\sqrt{E_{\ell,K+2,\delta}[\psi]}+\sum_{j=0}^{1}\sqrt{E_{\ell,K+1,\delta}[N^{j}\psi]}\right)\end{split}

(10.9)

for all
(\tau,\uprho,\theta,\varphi_{*})\in\mathcal{R}\setminus\mathcal{A}_{\gamma^{\alpha}}
.

Proof.

We apply the fundamental theorem of calculus, integrating
XT^{K}\psi_{0}
between
\uprho=\uprho^{\prime}
and
\uprho=\uprho_{\gamma^{\alpha}(\tau)}
. We use (
10.8
) to estimate the boundary term on
\uprho=\uprho_{\gamma^{\alpha}(\tau)}
and (
8.22
) to estimate the contribution of the integral.
∎

Remark 10.1
.

One can insert the expression (
10.6
) when integrating both sides of (
10.3
) to refine the asymptotics of
\phi_{0}
along
\mathcal{I}^{+}
and obtain the next-to-leading order logarithmic asymptotics; see Remark
1.1
and also
[
AAG19
]
for an application of this argument in the case
a=0
. We do not pursue this refinement in the present paper.

10.3
Asymptotics with vanishing Newman–Penrose charges

The estimates in Section
10.1
and
10.2
provide the late-time asymptotics for
\psi
arising from initial data with
I_{0}[\psi]\neq 0
. Using the time-integral construction from Section
9
, we can moreover apply these estimates to the setting when
I_{0}[\psi]=0
, and in particular, to the setting where the initial data of
\psi
is smooth and compactly supported.

Proposition 10.7
.

Consider initial data
(\psi|_{\Sigma_{0}},T\psi|_{\Sigma_{0}})
for (
3.1
), with
(\phi|_{\Sigma_{0}},T\phi_{\Sigma_{0}})\in(C^{\infty}(\widehat{\Sigma}))^{2}
, such that moreover
I_{0}[\psi]=0
. Let
r_{0}>r_{+}
. Then there exists a
\nu>0
and a constant
C=C(M,a,\Sigma_{0},r_{0},\nu)>0
, such that

\begin{split}&\left|T^{K}\psi_{0}(u,v)-4I_{0}[T^{-1}\psi]T^{K+1}\left(\frac{1}{uv}\right)\right|\\
\leq&\>Cv^{-1}u^{-2-K-\nu}\left(\sqrt{E_{0,K+1,\delta}[T^{-1}\psi]}+S_{0,\delta,K+1}[T^{-1}\psi]+D_{0,\beta,K+1}[T^{-1}\psi]+|I_{0}[T^{-1}\psi]|\right)\end{split}

in
\{r\geq r_{0}\}
, whereas in
\{r\leq r_{0}\}
, we can express:

\begin{split}&\left|T^{K}\psi_{0}+8I_{0}[T^{-1}\psi]T^{K}((1+\tau)^{-3})\right|(\tau,\uprho)\\
\leq&\>C(1+\tau)^{-3-K-\nu}\Bigg(S_{0,\delta,K+1}[T^{-1}\psi]+D_{0,\beta,K+1}[T^{-1}\psi]+|I_{0}[T^{-1}\psi]|\\
&+\sum_{\ell\in\{0,2\}}\sqrt{E_{\ell,K+3,\delta}[T^{-1}\psi]}+\sum_{j=0}^{1}\sqrt{E_{\ell,K+2,\delta}[N^{j}T^{-1}\psi]}\Bigg).\end{split}

Proof.

We apply Propositions
9.9
and
9.11
to conclude that
T^{-1}\phi
has sufficiently high regularity to conclude that all the relevant initial data energy norms for
T^{-1}\psi
are finite and
I_{0}[T^{-1}\psi]
is well-defined. From this it follows that Corollary
10.5
and Proposition
10.6
immediately apply when
\psi
is replaced by
T^{-1}\psi
.
∎

11
Late-time polynomial tails: the
\ell=1
projection

We derive in this section the leading-order late-time asymptotics of the restriction
\psi_{1}
of
\psi
.

It will be useful for the estimates in this section to define the following quantities: let
\beta>0
and
0<\delta<1
, then

\displaystyle D_{1,\beta,K}[\psi]:=

\displaystyle\>\max_{0\leq k\leq K}||v^{4+\beta+k}L^{k}\left(r^{-2}P_{1}-8I_{1}[\psi]v^{-4}\right)||_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma_{0}\cap\{r\geq R_{0}\})},

\displaystyle S_{1,\delta,K}[\psi]:=

\displaystyle\>\sup_{\tau\geq 0}\>(1+\tau)^{1-\delta}\sum_{j=0}^{1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq k_{1}+k_{2}\leq K+1\\
k_{2}\leq K\end{subarray}}(1+\tau)^{k_{2}}||(rL)^{k_{1}}T^{k_{2}}\check{\phi}_{1}^{(j)}||_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma_{\tau})}

\displaystyle+\sup_{\tau\geq 0}(1+\tau)^{2-\delta}\sum_{\ell\in\{1,3\}}\sum_{j=0}^{1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq k_{1}+k_{2}\leq K+1\\
k_{2}\leq K\end{subarray}}(1+\tau)^{k_{2}}||(rL)^{k_{1}}T^{k_{2}+1}\check{\phi}_{\ell}^{(j)}||_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma_{\tau})}.

Lemma 11.1
.

There exists a constant
C=C(M,a,\delta,K)>0
such that:

S_{1,\delta,K}[\psi]\leq C\sqrt{\sum_{k+j\leq K+2}E_{1,k,\frac{\delta}{2}}[N^{j}\psi]+E_{\geq 3,k,\frac{\delta}{2}}[N^{j}\psi]}.

Proof.

The estimate follows immediately by applying the estimates in Proposition
8.2
and
8.4
.
∎

11.1
Asymptotics in
\mathcal{A}_{\gamma^{\alpha}}

Proposition 11.2
.

Let
\alpha_{K}>\frac{4+K}{5+K}
. Then there exists
\delta(\alpha_{K})>0
suitably small, such that

\left|L^{K}(r^{-2}P_{1})(u,v,\theta,\varphi_{*})-8I_{1}[\psi](\theta,\varphi_{*})L^{K}(v^{-4})\right|\leq CS_{1,\delta,K}[\psi]v^{-(5+K-2\delta)\alpha_{K}}+C(D_{1,\beta,K}[\psi]+I_{1}[\psi])v^{-(4+K+\beta)}.

(11.1)

Proof.

Let
K\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
. By acting with
r^{-2}
and then with
L^{K}
on both sides of (
4.11
), we obtain:

4\underline{L}L^{K}(r^{-2}P_{1})=\sum_{k=0}^{K}O_{\infty}(r^{-4-K})(rL)^{k}P_{1}+\sum_{j=0}^{1}\sum_{k=0}^{K}O_{\infty}(r^{-5-K})(rL)^{k}\phi^{(j)}_{1}+\sum_{j=0}^{1}\sum_{k=0}^{K+1}O_{\infty}(r^{-5-K})(rL)^{k}T\pi_{1}(\sin^{2}\theta\phi^{(j)}).

(11.2)

We can further estimate for all
k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
:

|(rL)^{k}P_{1}-r^{-1}(rL)^{k+1}\check{\phi}^{(1)}_{1}|(u,v,\theta,\varphi_{*})\leq Cr^{-2}(u,v)\sum_{j=0}^{1}a^{2}||(rL)^{k}T\check{\phi}_{3}^{(j)}||_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma_{\tau})}+a||(rL)^{k}\check{\phi}^{(j)}_{1}||_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma_{\tau})}

(11.3)

and hence obtain:

|\underline{L}L^{K}(r^{-2}P_{1})|(u,v,\theta,\varphi_{*})\leq Cu^{-1+\delta}r^{-5-K}S_{1,\delta,K}[\psi].

(11.4)

Integrating from
\Sigma_{0}
in the
\underline{L}
direction, applying (
10.1
) together with (
10.4
), we obtain

\begin{split}\left|L^{K}(r^{-2}P_{1})(u,v,\theta,\varphi_{*})-L^{K}(r^{-2}P_{1})(u_{\Sigma_{0}}(v),v,\theta,\varphi_{*})\right|\leq&\>CS_{1,\delta,K}[\psi]\int_{u_{\Sigma_{0}}(v)}^{u}(1+\tau)^{-1+\delta}(u^{\prime},v)r^{-5-K}(u^{\prime},v)\,du^{\prime}\\
\leq&\>CS_{1,\delta,K}[\psi]\int_{u_{\Sigma_{0}}(v)}^{u}u^{\prime-1+\delta}(v-u^{\prime})^{-5-K}\,du^{\prime}\\
\leq&\>CS_{1,\delta,K}[\psi]v^{-(5+K-2\delta)\alpha_{K}}\int_{u_{\Sigma_{0}}(v)}^{u}u^{\prime-1-\delta}\,du^{\prime}\\
\leq&\>CS_{1,\delta,K}[\psi]v^{-(5+K-2\delta)\alpha_{K}}.\end{split}

Furthermore, we have that

\left|L^{K}(r^{-2}P_{1})(u_{\Sigma_{0}}(v),v,\theta,\varphi_{*})-8I_{1}[\psi]L^{K}(v^{-4})\right|\leq Cv^{-4-K-\beta}D_{1,\beta,K}[\psi].

so we can conclude that

\left|L^{K}(r^{-2}P_{1})(u,v,\theta,\varphi_{*})-8I_{1}[\psi]L^{K}(v^{-4})\right|\leq CS_{1,\delta,K}[\psi]v^{-(5+K-2\delta)\alpha_{K}}+CD_{1,\beta,K}[\psi]v^{-4-K-\beta}.

∎

Corollary 11.3
.

Let
\alpha_{K}>\frac{4+K}{5+K}
. Then there exists
\delta(\alpha_{K})>0
suitably small, such that

\begin{split}&\left|LT^{K}\check{\phi}_{1}^{(1)}(u,v,\theta,\varphi_{*})-2I_{1}[\psi](v-u)^{2}L^{K}(v^{-4})\right|\\
\leq&\>CS_{1,\delta,K}[\psi][a(v-u)^{-2}u^{-1-K+\delta}+(v-u)^{2}v^{-(5+K-2\delta)\alpha_{K}}+u^{-K+\delta}(v-u)^{-3}+u^{-1+\delta}(v-u)^{-3-K}]\\
&+CD_{1,\beta,K}[\psi](v-u)^{2}v^{-4-K-\beta}.\end{split}

(11.5)

Proof.

By induction, it follows that we can express

r^{-2}T^{K}P_{1}=L^{K}(r^{-2}P_{1})+\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\underline{L}L^{k}(r^{-2}T^{K-1-k}P_{1}).

Furthermore, by applying (
11.2
) with
P_{1}
replaced by
T^{K-1-k}P_{1}
, we obtain:

\begin{split}\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}|\underline{L}L^{k}T^{K-1-k}P_{1}|(u,v)\leq&\>C\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}u^{-(K-k)+\delta}r^{-5-k}S_{1,\delta,K}[\psi]\\
\leq&\>S_{1,\delta,K}[\psi][u^{-K+\delta}r^{-5}+u^{-1+\delta}r^{-5-K}].\end{split}

Hence, it follows from (
11.1
) that

\begin{split}\left|T^{K}P_{1}(u,v,\theta,\varphi_{*})-8I_{1}[\psi]r^{2}L^{K}(v^{-4})\right|\leq&\>CS_{1,\delta,K}[\psi][r^{2}v^{-(5+K-2\delta)\alpha_{K}}+u^{-K+\delta}r^{-3}+u^{-1+\delta}r^{-3-K}]\\
&+CD_{1,\beta,K}[\psi]r^{2}v^{-4-K-\beta}.\end{split}

By (
11.3
) with
k=0
and
\phi
replaced by
T^{K}\phi
, we obtain:

\begin{split}\left|LT^{K}\check{\phi}_{1}^{(1)}(u,v,\theta,\varphi_{*})-T^{K}P_{1}\right|\leq&\>CaS_{1,\delta,K}[\psi]r^{-2}u^{-1-K+\delta}.\end{split}

By combining the above and applying (
10.1
), we conclude that (
11.5
) holds.
∎

The lemma below provides some integral identities that will be useful for integrating (multiple times) in the
L
-direction.

Lemma 11.4
.

Let
K\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
and
m\in\mathbb{R}
, with
m>1
. Then:

\int_{v_{1}}^{v_{2}}(v-u)^{m-2}L^{K}(v^{-m})\,dv=(m-1)^{-1}\left[(v_{2}-u)^{m-1}T^{K}\left(\frac{1}{uv^{m-1}}\right)|_{v=v_{2}}-(v_{1}-u)^{m-1}T^{K}\left(\frac{1}{uv^{m-1}}\right)|_{v=v_{1}}\right].

(11.6)

Proof.

Note that

\begin{split}\int_{v_{1}}^{v_{2}}(v-u)^{m-2}L^{K}(v^{-m})\,dv=&\>\int_{v_{1}}^{v_{2}}T^{K}((v-u)^{m-2}v^{-m})\,dv\\
=&\>\int_{v_{1}}^{v_{2}}LT^{K}\left((m-1)^{-1}\frac{(v-u)^{m-1}}{uv^{m-1}}\right)\,dv\\
=&(m-1)^{-1}\left[(v_{2}-u)^{m-1}T^{K}\left(\frac{1}{uv^{m-1}}\right)|_{v=v_{2}}-(v_{1}-u)^{m-1}T^{K}\left(\frac{1}{uv^{m-1}}\right)|_{v=v_{1}}\right].\end{split}

∎

Proposition 11.5
.

For
\alpha_{K}^{\prime\prime}
suitably close to 1,
\delta>0
suitably small and
0<\beta\leq 1
suitably large, there exists
\nu>0
and a constant
C=C(M,a,\alpha_{K}^{\prime\prime},\nu,\delta)>0
such that

\displaystyle\Big|T^{K}\check{\phi}^{(1)}_{1}(u,v,\theta,\varphi_{*})

\displaystyle-\frac{2}{3}I_{1}[\psi](\theta,\varphi_{*})(v-u)^{3}T^{K}(u^{-1}v^{-3})\Big|

(11.7)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\>C\left(\sqrt{E_{1,K,\delta}[\psi]}+S_{1,\delta,K}[\psi]+D_{1,\beta,K}[\psi]+\sum_{m=-1}^{1}|I_{1m}[\psi]|\right)u^{-1-K-\nu},

(11.8)

\displaystyle\Big|r^{-1}T^{K}\psi_{1}(u,v,\theta,\varphi_{*})

\displaystyle-\frac{8}{3}I_{1}[\psi](\theta,\varphi_{*})T^{K}(u^{-2}v^{-2})\Big|

(11.9)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\>C\left(\sqrt{E_{1,K,\delta}[\psi]}+S_{1,\delta,K}[\psi]+D_{1,\beta,K}[\psi]+\sum_{m=-1}^{1}|I_{1m}[\psi]|\right)v^{-2}u^{-2-K-\nu},

(11.10)

\displaystyle\Big|LT^{K}\psi_{1}(u,v,\theta,\varphi_{*})

\displaystyle-\frac{4}{3}I_{1}[\psi](\theta,\varphi_{*})T^{K}(u^{-2}v^{-2})\Big|

(11.11)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\>C\left(\sqrt{E_{1,K,\delta}[\psi]}+S_{1,\delta,K}[\psi]+D_{1,\beta,K}[\psi]+\sum_{m=-1}^{1}|I_{1m}[\psi]|\right)v^{-2}u^{-2-K-\nu}

(11.12)

in
\mathcal{A}_{\gamma^{\alpha_{K}^{\prime\prime}}}
.

In particular, we have that

\displaystyle\left|T^{K}{\check{\phi}^{(1)}_{1}}|_{\mathcal{I}^{+}}-\frac{2}{3}I_{1}[\psi]T^{K}(u^{-1})\right|\leq

\displaystyle\>C\left(\sqrt{E_{1,K,\delta}[\psi]}+S_{1,\delta,K}[\psi]+D_{1,\beta,K}[\psi]+\sum_{m=-1}^{1}|I_{1m}[\psi]|\right)u^{-1-K-\nu},

(11.13)

\displaystyle\left|T^{K}\phi_{1}|_{\mathcal{I}^{+}}-\frac{2}{3}I_{1}[\psi]T^{K}(u^{-2})\right|\leq

\displaystyle\>C\left(\sqrt{E_{1,K,\delta}[\psi]}+S_{1,\delta,K}[\psi]+D_{1,\beta,K}[\psi]+\sum_{m=-1}^{1}|I_{1m}[\psi]|\right)u^{-2-K-\nu}

(11.14)

and

\displaystyle\left|r^{-1}T^{K}\psi_{1}|_{\gamma^{\alpha_{K}^{\prime}}}-\frac{8}{3}I_{1}[\psi]T^{K}(\tau^{-4})\right|\leq

\displaystyle\>C\left(\sqrt{E_{1,K,\delta}[\psi]}+S_{1,\delta,K}[\psi]+D_{1,\beta,K}[\psi]+\sum_{m=-1}^{1}|I_{1m}[\psi]|\right)\tau^{-4-K-\nu},

(11.15)

\displaystyle\left|XT^{K}\psi_{1}|_{\gamma^{\alpha_{K}^{\prime}}}-\frac{8}{3}I_{1}[\psi]T^{K}(\tau^{-4})\right|\leq

\displaystyle\>C\left(\sqrt{E_{1,K,\delta}[\psi]}+S_{1,\delta,K}[\psi]+D_{1,\beta,K}[\psi]+\sum_{m=-1}^{1}|I_{1m}[\psi]|\right)\tau^{-4-K-\nu}.

(11.16)

Proof.

Let
(u,v,\theta,\varphi_{*})
be a point in
\mathcal{A}_{\gamma^{\alpha_{K}}}
. Let us denote with
\gamma_{L}
the integral curve tangent to
L
connecting the point
(u,v,\theta,\varphi_{*})
with the curve
\gamma^{\alpha_{K}}
, where
\dot{\gamma}_{L}=L
. Then
(u_{\gamma_{L}}(v^{\prime}),\theta_{\gamma_{L}}(v^{\prime}))=(u,\theta)
for all
v_{\gamma^{\alpha_{K}}(u)}\leq v^{\prime}\leq v
and
L(\varphi_{*})=\frac{a}{r^{2}+a^{2}}
with
(\varphi_{*})_{\gamma_{L}}(v)=\varphi_{*}
.

We can therefore obtain for all
v_{\gamma^{\alpha_{K}}(u)}\leq v^{\prime}\leq v
:

|\varphi_{*}-(\varphi_{*})_{\gamma_{L}}(v^{\prime})|\leq\int_{v_{\gamma^{\alpha_{K}}}}^{v}|a|(r^{2}+a^{2})^{-1}\,dv^{\prime}\leq Cu^{-\alpha_{K}}.

(11.17)

Hence, for all
v_{\gamma^{\alpha_{K}}(u)}\leq v^{\prime}\leq v
:

|I_{1}(\theta,(\varphi_{*})_{\gamma_{L}}(v^{\prime}))-I_{1}(\theta,\varphi_{*})|\leq C\sum_{m=-1}^{1}|I_{1m}|u^{-\alpha_{K}}.

(11.18)

By combining the above estimate with Lemma
11.4
for
m=4
, we have that:

\left|\int_{v_{\gamma^{\alpha_{K}}(u)}}^{v}2I_{1}[\psi]|_{\gamma_{L}}(v^{\prime}-u)^{2}L^{K}(v^{\prime-4})\,dv^{\prime}-\frac{2}{3}I_{1}[\psi](\theta,\varphi_{*})(v-u)^{3}T^{K}(u^{-1}v^{-3})\right|\leq C\sum_{m=-1}^{1}|I_{1m}|u^{-4-K+3\alpha_{K}}.

By integrating (
11.5
), and applying (
8.8
), we obtain

\begin{split}|T^{K}\check{\phi}_{1}^{(1)}|(u,v_{\gamma^{\alpha_{K}}(u)},\theta,\varphi_{*})\leq&\>Cr^{\frac{1}{2}(1+\delta)}(u,v_{\gamma^{\alpha_{K}}(u)})u^{-\frac{3}{2}-K+2\delta}\sqrt{E_{1,K,\delta}[\psi]}\\
\leq&\>C\sqrt{E_{1,K,\delta}[\psi]}u^{-\frac{3}{2}+\frac{1}{2}\alpha_{K}-K+2\delta},\end{split}

we then obtain (
11.7
) and (
11.13
) for some
\nu>0
suitably small.

Furthermore, since
T^{K}\check{\phi}_{1}^{(1)}=2(r^{2}+O(r))L\phi_{1}+aKO(r^{-1})T^{K-1}\phi_{1}
, we have that:

\begin{split}&\left|LT^{K}\phi_{1}(u,v,\theta,\varphi_{*})-\frac{4}{3}I_{1}[\psi](\theta,\varphi_{*})(v-u)T^{K}(u^{-1}v^{-3})\right|\\
\leq&\>C\left(\sqrt{E_{1,K,\delta}[\psi]}+S_{1,\delta,K}[\psi]+D_{1,\beta,K}[\psi]+\sum_{m=-1}^{1}|I_{1m}|\right)(v-u)^{-2}u^{-1-K-\nu}.\end{split}

(11.19)

Let
\alpha_{K}^{\prime}>\alpha_{K}
, then we can estimate in
\mathcal{A}_{\gamma^{\alpha_{K}^{\prime}}}
using (
8.7
):

\begin{split}|T^{K}\phi_{1}|(u,v_{\gamma^{\alpha_{K}^{\prime}}(u)},\theta,\varphi)\leq&\>C\sqrt{E_{1,K,\delta}[\psi]}u^{-\frac{5}{2}+\frac{1}{2}\alpha^{\prime}_{K}-K+\delta},\end{split}

so by integrating (
11.19
) and applying (
11.18
) and Lemma
11.4
again, taking
\alpha_{K}^{\prime}>\alpha_{K}
suitably large, we obtain the following estimate in
\mathcal{A}_{\gamma^{\alpha_{K}^{\prime}}}
:

\begin{split}&\left|T^{K}\phi_{1}(u,v,\theta,\varphi_{*})-\frac{2}{3}I_{1}[\psi](\theta,\varphi_{*})(v-u)^{2}T^{K}(u^{-2}v^{-2})\right|\\
\leq&\>C\left(\sqrt{E_{1,K,\delta}[\psi]}+S_{1,\delta,K}[\psi]+D_{1,\beta,K}[\psi]+\sum_{m=-1}^{1}|I_{1m}|\right)u^{-2-K-\nu}.\end{split}

(11.20)

Hence, (
11.14
) follows.

We conclude that (
11.9
) must hold in the smaller region
\mathcal{A}_{\gamma^{\alpha_{K}^{\prime\prime}}}
, with
\alpha_{K}^{\prime\prime}>\alpha_{K}
suitably large, by multiplying both sides of (
11.20
) with
r^{-2}
.

Finally, we combine (
11.20
) with (
11.19
) and use that

L\psi_{1}=-\Delta\frac{r^{3}}{2(r^{2}+a^{2})^{\frac{5}{2}}}r^{-1}\psi_{1}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{r^{2}+a^{2}}}L\phi_{1}

to obtain (
11.11
). The estimates (
11.15
) and (
11.15
) then follows immediately.
∎

11.2
Asymptotics in
\mathcal{R}\setminus\mathcal{A}_{\gamma^{\alpha}}

In this section, we will extend the late-time asymptotics in Proposition
11.5
to the rest of the spacetime. In particular, we will demonstrate the presence of oscillations in the late-time behaviour along the null generators of the event horizon
\mathcal{H}^{+}
.

Proposition 11.6
.

Let
K\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
and let
\alpha>0
be arbitrarily large. Then there exists a constant
C>0
such that

\displaystyle\left|T^{K}\psi_{1}(\tau,\uprho,\theta,\varphi_{*})-\frac{8}{3}I_{1}[\psi](\theta,\varphi_{*})\uprho T^{K}((1+\tau)^{-4})\right|(\tau,\uprho,\theta,\varphi_{*})

(11.21)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\>C(1+\tau)^{-4-K-\nu}\Bigg(S_{1,\delta,K}[\psi]+D_{1,\beta,K}[\psi]+\sum_{m=-1}^{1}|I_{1m}[\psi]|+\sqrt{E_{1,K+5,\delta}[\psi]}+\sum_{j=0}^{2}\sqrt{E_{1,K+3,\delta}[N^{j}\psi]}

\displaystyle+\sqrt{E_{\geq 3,K+2,\delta}[\psi]}+\sum_{j=0}^{1}\sqrt{E_{\geq 3,K+2,\delta}[N^{j}\psi]}\Bigg),

\displaystyle\left|XT^{K}\psi_{1}(\tau,\uprho,\theta,\varphi_{*})-\frac{8}{3}I_{1}[\psi](\theta,\varphi_{*})T^{K}((1+\tau)^{-4})\right|(\tau,\uprho,\theta,\varphi_{*})

(11.22)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\>C(1+\tau)^{-4-K-\nu}\Bigg(S_{1,\delta,K}[\psi]+D_{1,\beta,K}[\psi]+\sum_{m=-1}^{1}|I_{1m}|+\sqrt{E_{1,K+5,\delta}[\psi]}+\sum_{j=0}^{2}\sqrt{E_{1,K+3,\delta}[N^{j}\psi]}

\displaystyle+\sqrt{E_{\geq 3,K+2,\delta}[\psi]}+\sum_{j=0}^{1}\sqrt{E_{\geq 3,K+2,\delta}[N^{j}\psi]}\Bigg),

for all
(\tau,\uprho,\theta,\varphi_{*})\in\mathcal{R}\setminus\mathcal{A}_{\gamma^{\alpha}}
.

In particular, the following oscillatory asymptotics hold along the null generators of
\mathcal{H}^{+}
:

\begin{split}&\left|T^{K}\psi_{1}|_{\mathcal{H}^{+}}(v,\theta,\varphi_{\mathcal{H}^{+}})-\frac{8}{3}r_{+}\sum_{m=-1}^{1}I_{1m}[\psi]Y_{1,m}(\theta,\varphi_{\mathcal{H}^{+}})e^{im\upomega_{+}v}T^{K}((1+\tau)^{-4})\right|\\
\leq&\>C(1+\tau)^{-4-K-\nu}\Bigg(S_{1,\delta,K}[\psi]+D_{1,\beta,K}[\psi]+\sum_{m=-1}^{1}|I_{1m}|+\sqrt{E_{1,K+5,\delta}[\psi]}+\sum_{j=0}^{2}\sqrt{E_{1,K+3,\delta}[N^{j}\psi]}\\
&+\sqrt{E_{\geq 3,K+2,\delta}[\psi]}+\sum_{j=0}^{1}\sqrt{E_{\geq 3,K+2,\delta}[N^{j}\psi]}\Bigg),\end{split}

(11.23)

where we use
Y_{\ell,m}(\theta,\varphi_{\mathcal{H}^{+}})
denote spherical harmonics with respect to the polar angle
\theta
and the azimuthal angle
\varphi_{\mathcal{H}^{+}}
.

Proof.

In order to obtain (
11.22
), we apply the fundamental theorem of calculus, integrating
X^{2}T^{K}\psi_{1}
between
\uprho=\uprho^{\prime}
and
\uprho=\uprho_{\gamma^{\alpha}(\tau)}
. We use (
11.16
) to estimate the boundary term on
\uprho=\uprho_{\gamma^{\alpha}(\tau)}
and (
8.23
) to estimate the contribution of the integral.

The estimate (
11.21
) then follows by applying the fundamental theorem of calculus again, integrating now
XT^{K}\psi_{1}
between
\uprho=\uprho^{\prime}
and
\uprho=\uprho_{\gamma^{\alpha}(\tau)}
and using the estimates (
11.22
) and (
11.15
).

Finally, (
11.23
) follows from (
11.21
) by using the relation (
2.3
) between
\varphi_{\mathcal{H}^{+}}
and
\varphi_{*}
to conclude that

\displaystyle e^{im\varphi_{*}}=

\displaystyle\>e^{im\varphi_{\mathcal{H}^{+}}}e^{im\upomega_{+}v},\quad\textnormal{and hence}

\displaystyle Y_{\ell,m}(\theta,\varphi_{*})=

\displaystyle\>Y_{\ell,m}(\theta,\varphi_{\mathcal{H}^{+}})e^{im\upomega_{+}v}.

∎

11.3
Asymptotics with vanishing Newman–Penrose charges

We can now apply the time-integral construction from Section
9
to obtain late-time asymptotics for
\psi_{1}
arising from initial data that are smooth and compactly supported.

Proposition 11.7
.

Consider initial data
(\psi|_{\Sigma_{0}},T\psi|_{\Sigma_{0}})
for (
3.1
), with
(\phi|_{\Sigma_{0}},T\phi_{\Sigma_{0}})\in(C_{c}^{\infty}(\Sigma_{0}))^{2}
. Let
r_{0}>r_{+}
. Then there exists a
\nu>0
and a constant
C=C(M,a,\Sigma_{0},r_{0},\nu)>0
, such that

\begin{split}\Big|r^{-1}T^{K}\psi_{1}(u,v,\theta,\varphi_{*})&-\frac{8}{3}I_{1}[T^{-1}\psi](\theta,\varphi_{*})T^{K+1}(u^{-2}v^{-2})\Big|\\
\leq&\>Cv^{-2}u^{-3-K-\nu}\Bigg(S_{1,\delta,K+1}[T^{-1}\psi]+D_{1,\beta,K+1}[\psi]+\sum_{m=-1}^{1}|I_{1m}[T^{-1}\psi]|\\
&+\sqrt{E_{1,K+6,\delta}[T^{-1}\psi]}+\sum_{j=0}^{2}\sqrt{E_{1,K+4,\delta}[N^{j}T^{-1}\psi]}+\sqrt{E_{\geq 3,K+3,\delta}[T^{-1}\psi]}\\
&+\sum_{j=0}^{1}\sqrt{E_{\geq 3,K+3,\delta}[N^{j}T^{-1}\psi]}\Bigg)\end{split}

in
\{r\geq r_{0}\}
, whereas in
\{r\leq r_{0}\}
, we can express:

\begin{split}&\left|T^{K}\psi_{1}(\tau,\uprho,\theta,\varphi_{*})+\frac{32}{3}I_{1}[T^{-1}\psi](\theta,\varphi_{*})\uprho T^{K}((1+\tau)^{-5})\right|(\tau,\uprho,\theta,\varphi_{*})\\
\leq&\>C(1+\tau)^{-5-K-\nu}\Bigg(S_{1,\delta,K+1}[T^{-1}\psi]+D_{1,\beta,K+1}[T^{-1}\psi]+\sum_{m=-1}^{1}|I_{1m}[T^{-1}\psi]|\\
&+\sqrt{E_{1,K+6,\delta}[T^{-1}\psi]}+\sum_{j=0}^{2}\sqrt{E_{1,K+4,\delta}[N^{j}T^{-1}\psi]}+\sqrt{E_{\geq 3,K+3,\delta}[T^{-1}\psi]}\\
&+\sum_{j=0}^{1}\sqrt{E_{\geq 3,K+3,\delta}[N^{j}T^{-1}\psi]}\Bigg).\end{split}

In particular, the following oscillatory asymptotics hold along the null generators of
\mathcal{H}^{+}
:

\begin{split}&\left|T^{K}\psi_{1}|_{\mathcal{H}^{+}}(v,\theta,\varphi_{\mathcal{H}^{+}})+\frac{32}{3}r_{+}\sum_{m=-1}^{1}I_{1m}[T^{-1}\psi]Y_{1,m}(\theta,\varphi_{\mathcal{H}^{+}})e^{im\upomega_{+}v}T^{K}((1+\tau)^{-5})\right|\\
\leq&\>C(1+\tau)^{-5-K-\nu}\Bigg(S_{1,\delta,K+1}[T^{-1}\psi]+D_{1,\beta,K+1}[T^{-1}\psi]+\sum_{m=-1}^{1}|I_{1m}[T^{-1}\psi]|\\
&+\sqrt{E_{1,K+6,\delta}[T^{-1}\psi]}+\sum_{j=0}^{2}\sqrt{E_{1,K+4,\delta}[N^{j}T^{-1}\psi]}+\sqrt{E_{\geq 3,K+3,\delta}[T^{-1}\psi]}\\
&+\sum_{j=0}^{1}\sqrt{E_{\geq 3,K+3,\delta}[N^{j}T^{-1}\psi]}\Bigg).\end{split}

Proof.

We apply Propositions
9.9
and
9.11
to conclude that
T^{-1}\phi
has sufficiently high regularity to conclude that all the relevant energies for
T^{-1}\psi
are finite and
I_{1}[T^{-1}\psi]
is well-defined. Then the estimates follows immediately by applying the estimates in Propositions
11.5
and
11.6
to
T^{-1}\psi
instead of
\psi
.
∎

12
Late-time polynomial tails: the
\ell=2
projection

In this section we derive the late-time asymptotics for the projection
\psi_{2}
. In contrast with the
\ell=0,1
cases,
the leading-order late-time behaviour of
\psi_{2}
is coupled with the leading-order late-time behaviour of
\psi_{0}
and will therefore not just depend on the Newman–Penrose charge
I_{2}[\psi]
, but also on
I_{0}[\psi]
. As will be shown below, this coupling mechanism will limit the decay rate of
\psi_{2}
in regions of bounded
r
.

It will be useful for the estimates in this section to define the following quantities: let
\beta>0
and
0<\delta<1
, then

\displaystyle D_{2,\beta,K}[\psi]:=

\displaystyle\>\max_{0\leq k\leq K}||v^{6+\beta+k}L^{k}\left(r^{-4}P_{2}-32I_{2}[\psi]v^{-6}\right)||_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma_{0}\cap\{r\geq R_{0}\})},

\displaystyle S_{2,\delta,K}[\psi]:=

\displaystyle\>\sup_{\tau\geq 0}\>(1+\tau)^{1-\delta}\sum_{j=0}^{2}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq k_{1}+k_{2}\leq K+1\\
k_{2}\leq K\end{subarray}}(1+\tau)^{k_{2}}||(rL)^{k_{1}}T^{k_{2}}\check{\phi}_{2}^{(j)}||_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma_{\tau})}

\displaystyle+\sup_{\tau\geq 0}(1+\tau)^{1-\delta}\sum_{\ \ell\in\{2,4\}}\sum_{j=0}^{2}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq k_{1}+k_{2}\leq K+1\\
k_{2}\leq K\end{subarray}}(1+\tau)^{k_{2}}||(rL)^{k_{1}}T^{k_{2}+1}\check{\phi}_{\ell}^{(j)}||_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma_{\tau})}

\displaystyle+\sup_{\tau\geq 0}(1+\tau)^{1-\delta}\sum_{j=0}^{1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq k_{1}+k_{2}\leq K+1\\
k_{2}\leq K\end{subarray}}(1+\tau)^{k_{2}}||(rL)^{k_{1}}T^{k_{2}+1}\check{\phi}_{0}^{(j)}||_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma_{\tau})}

\displaystyle+S_{0,\delta,3+K}[\psi]+|I_{0}[\psi]|+D_{0,1-\delta,3+K}[\psi].

Lemma 12.1
.

There exists a constant
C=C(M,a,\delta,K)>0
such that:

S_{2,\delta,K}[\psi]\leq C\sum_{k+j\leq K+2}\sqrt{E_{2,k,\frac{\delta}{2}}[N^{j}\psi]}+\sqrt{E_{\geq 3,k,\frac{\delta}{2}}[N^{j}\psi]}+\sqrt{E_{0,k+3,\frac{\delta}{2}}[N^{j}\psi]}+C|I_{0}[\psi]|+CD_{0,1-\delta,3+K}[\psi].

Proof.

The estimate follows immediately by applying the estimates in Proposition
8.3
and
8.3
, together with Lemma
10.1
and also (
12.6
) below to estimate the terms involving
(rL)^{k_{1}}T^{k_{2}+1}\check{\phi}_{0}^{(1)}
.
∎

12.1
Asymptotics in
\mathcal{A}_{\gamma^{\alpha}}

We recall here (
4.12
), but with the dependence on
\pi_{2}(\sin^{2}\theta\check{\phi}^{(2)}_{0})
stated more precisely:

\begin{split}4\underline{L}P_{2}=&\>[-8r^{-1}+O_{\infty}(r^{-2})]P_{2}-[2r^{-1}+O_{\infty}(r^{-2})]\frac{a^{2}\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})}\pi_{2}(\sin^{2}\theta T\check{\phi}^{(2)}_{0})+L(\frac{a^{2}\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})}\pi_{2}(\sin^{2}\theta T\check{\phi}^{(2)}_{0}))\\
&+O_{\infty}(r^{-3})[\check{\phi}^{(2)}_{2}+\check{\phi}^{(1)}_{2}+\phi_{2}]+O_{\infty}(r^{-3})[T\pi_{2}(\sin^{2}\theta\check{\phi}_{4}^{(2)})+T\pi_{2}(\sin^{2}\theta\check{\phi}^{(1)})+T\pi_{2}(\sin^{2}\theta\phi)]\\
&+O_{\infty}(r^{-2})[LT\pi_{2}(\sin^{2}\theta\check{\phi}_{4}^{(2)})+LT\pi_{2}(\sin^{2}\theta\check{\phi}^{(1)})+LT\pi_{2}(\sin^{2}\theta\phi)]\end{split}

Due to the slower decay properties of
\check{\phi}^{(2)}_{0}
, it will be convenient to put some of the terms involving
\check{\phi}^{(2)}_{0}
on the left-hand side of the above equation by introducing the modified quantity:

\widetilde{P}_{2}=P_{2}-\frac{1}{4}r^{4}L\left(r^{-4}\frac{a^{2}\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})}\pi_{2}(\sin^{2}\theta T\check{\phi}^{(2)}_{0})\right).

Then:

\begin{split}4\underline{L}(r^{-4}\widetilde{P}_{2})=&\>L^{2}\left(r^{-4}\frac{a^{2}\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})}\pi_{2}(\sin^{2}\theta\check{\phi}^{(2)}_{0})\right)+O_{\infty}(r^{-6})P_{2}\\
&+O_{\infty}(r^{-7})[\check{\phi}^{(2)}_{2}+\check{\phi}^{(1)}_{2}+\phi_{2}]+O_{\infty}(r^{-7})[T\pi_{2}(\sin^{2}\theta\check{\phi}_{4}^{(2)})+T\pi_{2}(\sin^{2}\theta\check{\phi}^{(1)})+T\pi_{2}(\sin^{2}\theta\phi)]\\
&+O_{\infty}(r^{-7})[rLT\pi_{2}(\sin^{2}\theta\check{\phi}_{4}^{(2)})+rLT\pi_{2}(\sin^{2}\theta\check{\phi}^{(1)})+rLT\pi_{2}(\sin^{2}\theta\phi)].\end{split}

(12.1)

In order to determine the late-time asymptotics of
\psi_{2}
, it will be necessary to appeal to the late-time asymptotics of
\psi_{0}
obtained in Section
10
. We derive in the lemma below the necessary estimates for
\psi_{0}
.

Lemma 12.2
.

Let
K\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
and
\alpha_{K}>\frac{6+K}{7+K}
. Then there exists
\delta(\alpha_{K})>0
suitably small, such that for all
j,m\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
with
j+m=K
, we have that in
\mathcal{A}_{\gamma^{\alpha_{K}}}
:

\displaystyle\Bigg|\frac{1}{4}L^{j+1}

\displaystyle\left[r^{-4}\frac{a^{2}\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}T^{m}\check{\phi}^{(2)}_{0}\right]-2a^{2}I_{0}[\psi]L^{K}T^{m}\Bigg(24(v-u)^{-2}v^{-4}-24(v-u)^{-4}v^{-2}\Bigg|

(12.2)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\>C(r^{-6-j}u^{-2-m+\delta}+r^{-6-K}u^{-1+\delta}+v^{-(7-K+2\delta)\alpha_{K-1}})S_{2,\delta,K-1}[\psi],

\displaystyle\Bigg|\frac{1}{4}L^{j+2}

\displaystyle\left[r^{-4}\frac{a^{2}\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}T^{m}\check{\phi}^{(2)}_{0}\right]-2a^{2}I_{0}[\psi]\underline{L}L^{K}T^{m}\Bigg(-96(v-u)^{-1}v^{-5}-24(v-u)^{-2}v^{-4}

\displaystyle+16(v-u)^{-3}v^{-3}+24(v-u)^{-4}v^{-2}\Bigg)\Bigg|

(12.3)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\>C(r^{-7-j}u^{-2-m+\delta}+r^{-7-K}u^{-1+\delta}+v^{-(8-K+2\delta)\alpha_{K}})S_{2,\delta,K}[\psi],

\displaystyle\Bigg|\frac{1}{4}\frac{a^{2}\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}

\displaystyle T^{K+1}\check{\phi}^{(2)}_{0}-2a^{2}I_{0}[\psi]L^{K}\left(\frac{3}{2}(v-u)^{2}v^{-4}-(v-u)v^{-3}\right)\Bigg|

(12.4)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\>C(r^{-3-K}u^{-1+\delta}+r^{-2}u^{-2-K+\delta}+v^{-(4-K+2\delta)\alpha_{K-1}})S_{2,\delta,K-1}[\psi].

Furthermore, for all
k\leq K
, we can estimate

\begin{split}r^{-6}|(rL)^{k}(r^{-2}T\check{\phi}^{(2)}_{0})|\leq CS_{2,\delta,K}[\psi][(r^{-5}v^{-3}+r^{-7}u^{-2+\delta}+r^{-8}u^{-1+\delta})\end{split}

(12.5)

Proof.

We can write

\begin{split}\frac{1}{4}L\left(r^{-4}\frac{a^{2}\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}T^{m}\check{\phi}^{(2)}_{0}\right)=&\>\frac{1}{2}L\left(a^{2}\left[r^{-4}+O_{\infty}(r^{-5})\right]LT^{m}\check{\phi}^{(1)}_{0}\right)\\
=&\>L\left(a^{2}\left[r^{-2}+O_{\infty}(r^{-3})\right]L^{2}T^{m}{\phi}_{0}\right)+L\left(a^{2}\left[r^{-3}+O_{\infty}(r^{-4})\right]LT^{m}{\phi}_{0}\right)\\
=&\>a^{2}(r^{-2}+O_{\infty}(r^{-3}))L^{3}T^{m}{\phi}_{0}-\frac{3}{2}a^{2}(r^{-4}+O_{\infty}(r^{-5}))\sin^{2}\theta LT^{m}{\phi}_{0}.\end{split}

And hence,

\begin{split}\frac{1}{4}&L^{K}\left[L\left(r^{-4}\frac{a^{2}\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\check{\phi}^{(2)}_{0}\right)\right]=a^{2}\sum_{k=0}^{K}\frac{K!}{k!(K-k)!}L^{k}(r^{-2}+O_{\infty}(r^{-3}))L^{3+K-k}T^{m}{\phi}_{0}\\
&-\frac{3}{2}a^{2}\sum_{k=0}^{K}\frac{K!}{k!(K-k)!}L^{k}(r^{-4}+O_{\infty}(r^{-5}))L^{1+K-k}T^{m}{\phi}_{0}\end{split}

By (
10.2
) together with the arguments in the proof of Corollary
10.4
, we moreover have that for all
j\geq 1
and
\beta\geq 0
:

\begin{split}\left|L^{j}T^{m}\phi_{0}(u,v)+2I_{0}[\psi]L^{j+m}(v^{-1})\right|\leq&\>CS_{0,\delta,j+m-1}[\psi]\left[r^{-1-j}u^{-2-m+\delta}+u^{-1+\delta}r^{-1-j-m}+v^{-(2+j+m-2\delta)\alpha_{j+m-1}}\right]\\
&+CD_{0,\beta,K}[\psi]v^{-1-j-m-\beta}\end{split}

(12.6)

in
\mathcal{A}_{\gamma^{\alpha_{j+m-1}}}
. By combining the above, we obtain

\begin{split}\Bigg|\frac{1}{4}&L^{j+1}\left[r^{-4}\frac{a^{2}\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}T^{m}\check{\phi}^{(2)}_{0}\right]-2a^{2}I_{0}[\psi]T^{m}L^{j}\Bigg(24(v-u)^{-2}v^{-4}-24(v-u)^{-4}v^{-2}\Bigg|\\
\leq&\>C(r^{-6-j}u^{-2-m+\delta}+r^{-6-K}u^{-1+\delta}+v^{-(7-K+2\delta)\alpha_{K-1}})S_{2,\delta,K-1}[\psi].\end{split}

and hence

\begin{split}\Bigg|\frac{1}{4}&L^{j+2}\left[r^{-4}\frac{a^{2}\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}T^{m}\check{\phi}^{(2)}_{0}\right]-2a^{2}I_{0}[\psi]L^{j}T^{m}\Bigg(24(v-u)^{-2}v^{-4}-24(v-u)^{-4}v^{-2}\Bigg|\\
\leq&\>C(r^{-7-j}u^{-2-m+\delta}+r^{-7-K}u^{-1+\delta}+v^{-(8-K+2\delta)\alpha_{K}})S_{2,\delta,K}[\psi].\end{split}

We obtain (
12.4
) and (
12.5
) in an analogous manner, appealing again to the arguments in the proof of Corollary
10.4
.
∎

Let
f
be a suitably regular function on
\mathbb{S}^{2}
. Then recall that by (
2.13
), we can write:

\pi_{2}(\sin^{2}\theta\pi_{0}(f))=-\frac{4}{3}\sqrt{\frac{\pi}{5}}\pi_{0}(f)Y_{2,0}(\theta).

Proposition 12.3
.

Let
\alpha_{K}>\frac{6+K}{7+K}
. Then there exists
\delta(\alpha_{K})>0
suitably small, such that in
\mathcal{A}_{\gamma^{\alpha_{K}}}
:

\begin{split}&\Bigg|L^{K}(r^{-4}\widetilde{P}_{2})-32I_{2}[\psi]L^{K}(v^{-6})+\frac{8}{3}\sqrt{\frac{\pi}{5}}a^{2}I_{0}[\psi]Y_{2,0}L^{K}(v^{-6})\\
\ &+\frac{8}{3}\sqrt{\frac{\pi}{5}}a^{2}I_{0}[\psi]Y_{2,0}L^{K}\Bigg(-96(v-u)^{-1}v^{-5}-24(v-u)^{-2}v^{-4}+16(v-u)^{-3}v^{-3}+24(v-u)^{-4}v^{-2}\Bigg)\Bigg|\\
\leq&\>Cv^{-(8-K+2\delta)\alpha_{K}+1}S_{2,\delta,K}[\psi]+CD_{2,\beta,K}[\psi]v^{-6-K-\beta}.\end{split}

(12.7)

Proof.

Let
K\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
. By multiplying with
r^{-4}
and then acting with
L^{K}
on both sides of (
4.12
), we obtain:

\begin{split}4\underline{L}L^{K}(r^{-4}\widetilde{P}_{2})=&\>L^{2+K}\left(r^{-4}\frac{a^{2}\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})}\pi_{2}(\sin^{2}\theta\check{\phi}^{(2)}_{0})\right)\\
&+\sum_{k=0}^{K}O_{\infty}(r^{-6-K})(rL)^{k}P_{2}+\sum_{j=0}^{2}\sum_{k=0}^{K}O_{\infty}(r^{-7-K})(rL)^{k}\check{\phi}^{(j)}_{2}+\sum_{j=0}^{2}\sum_{k=0}^{K+1}O_{\infty}(r^{-7-K})(rL)^{k}T\pi_{2}(\sin^{2}\theta\phi^{(j)}_{4})\\
&+\sum_{j=0}^{1}\sum_{k=0}^{K+1}O_{\infty}(r^{-7-K})(rL)^{k}T\pi_{2}(\sin^{2}\theta\check{\phi}^{(j)}_{0}).\end{split}

(12.8)

We can further estimate for all
k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
:

\begin{split}|(rL)^{k}P_{2}-r^{-1}(rL)^{k+1}\check{\phi}^{(2)}_{2}|(u,v,\theta,\varphi_{*})\leq&\>Ca^{2}|(rL)^{k}(r^{-2}T\check{\phi}^{(2)}_{0})|+Car^{-2}(u,v)\sum_{j=0}^{1}(a||(rL)^{k}T\check{\phi}_{0}^{(j)}||_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma_{\tau)}}\\
&+||(rL)^{k}\check{\phi}^{(j)}_{2}||_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma_{\tau)}}+a||(rL)^{k}T\check{\phi}^{(j)}_{4}||_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma_{\tau)}}+a||(rL)^{k}T\check{\phi}^{(2)}_{4}||_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma_{\tau)}}).\end{split}

(12.9)

and hence obtain after applying (
12.2
) and (
12.5
):

\begin{split}&\Bigg|\underline{L}\Bigg[L^{K}(r^{-4}\widetilde{P}_{2})+\frac{8}{3}\sqrt{\frac{\pi}{5}}a^{2}I_{0}[\psi]Y_{2,0}L^{K}\Bigg(-96(v-u)^{-1}v^{-5}-24(v-u)^{-2}v^{-4}+16(v-u)^{-3}v^{-3}+24(v-u)^{-4}v^{-2}\Bigg)\Bigg]\Bigg|\\
\leq&\>C(r^{-7-K}u^{-1+\delta}+v^{-(8-K+2\delta)\alpha_{K}})S_{2,\delta,K}[\psi].\end{split}

(12.10)

Integrating from
\Sigma_{0}
in the
\underline{L}
direction, applying (
10.1
) together with (
10.4
), we obtain

\begin{split}\int_{u_{\Sigma_{0}}(v)}^{u}u^{\prime-1+\delta}r^{-7-K}(u^{\prime},v)\,du^{\prime}\leq&\>C\int_{u_{\Sigma_{0}}(v)}^{u}u^{\prime-1+\delta}(v-u^{\prime})^{-7-K}\,du^{\prime}\\
\leq&\>Cv^{-(7+K-2\delta)\alpha_{K}}\int_{u_{\Sigma_{0}}(v)}^{u}u^{\prime-1-\delta}\,du^{\prime}\\
\leq&\>Cv^{-(7+K-2\delta)\alpha_{K}}.\end{split}

Hence, (
12.7
) follows after integrating the above inequalities.
∎

Corollary 12.4
.

Let
K\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
and
\alpha_{K}>\frac{6+K}{7+K}
. Then there exists
\delta(\alpha_{K})>0
suitably small, such that in
\mathcal{A}_{\gamma^{\alpha_{K}}}
:

\begin{split}\Bigg|LT^{K}\check{\phi}^{(2)}_{2}&-2I_{2}[\psi](v-u)^{4}L^{K}(v^{-6})+\frac{40}{3}\sqrt{\frac{\pi}{5}}a^{2}I_{0}[\psi]Y_{2,0}(v-u)^{4}L^{K}(v^{-6})\\
&+\frac{8}{3}\sqrt{\frac{\pi}{5}}a^{2}Y_{2,0}I_{0}[\psi]T^{K}\left(-6(v-u)^{3}v^{-5}+\frac{3}{2}(v-u)^{2}v^{-4}\right)\Bigg|\\
\leq&\>C(u^{-1+\delta}r^{-2-K}+u^{-1-K+\delta}r^{-2}+r^{4}v^{-(8K+2\delta)\alpha_{K}+1})S_{2,\delta,K}[\psi]+CD_{2,\beta,K}[\psi]r^{4}v^{-6-K-\beta}.\end{split}

(12.11)

Proof.

In light of the estimate (
12.7
), it is convenient to introduce a second modification of
P_{2}
:

\widetilde{\widetilde{P}_{2}}:=\widetilde{P}_{2}+\frac{8}{3}\sqrt{\frac{\pi}{5}}a^{2}I_{0}[\psi]Y_{2,0}\Bigg(-96(v-u)^{-1}v^{-5}-24(v-u)^{-2}v^{-4}+16(v-u)^{-3}v^{-3}+24(v-u)^{-4}v^{-2}\Bigg)

By (
12.10
), we have that

|\underline{L}(r^{-4}L^{K}\widetilde{\widetilde{P}_{2}})|\leq C(r^{-7-K}u^{-1+\delta}+v^{-(8-K+2\delta)\alpha_{K}})S_{2,\delta,K}[\psi]

and by induction, it follows that we can express

r^{-4}T^{K}\widetilde{\widetilde{P}_{2}}=L^{K}(r^{-4}\widetilde{\widetilde{P}_{2}})+\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\underline{L}L^{k}(r^{-4}T^{K-1-k}\widetilde{\widetilde{P}_{2}}).

Furthermore, by repeating the arguments leading to (
12.10
) for
P_{2}
replaced by
T^{K-1-k}P_{2}
and applying (
12.2
) and (
12.5
), we obtain
