\begin{split}\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}|\underline{L}L^{k}(r^{-4}T^{K-1-k}\widetilde{P_{2}})|(u,v,\theta,\varphi_{*})\leq&\>C(u^{-1+\delta}r^{-6-K}+u^{-1-K+\delta}r^{-6}+v^{-(7-K+2\delta)\alpha_{K}})S_{2,\delta,K}[\psi].\end{split}

Furthermore,

\begin{split}LT^{K}\check{\phi}^{(2)}_{2}=&\>T^{K}\widetilde{\widetilde{P}_{2}}+\frac{8}{3}\sqrt{\frac{\pi}{5}}a^{2}I_{0}[\psi]Y_{2,0}T^{K}\Bigg(-6(v-u)^{3}v^{-5}-\frac{3}{2}(v-u)^{2}v^{-4}+(v-u)^{1}v^{-3}+\frac{3}{2}v^{-2}\Bigg)\\
&+\frac{1}{4}r^{4}T^{K}L\left(r^{-4}\frac{a^{2}\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})}\pi_{2}(\sin^{2}\theta\check{\phi}^{(2)}_{0})\right)+\frac{1}{4}\frac{a^{2}\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\pi_{2}(\sin^{2}\theta T^{K+1}\check{\phi}^{(2)}_{0}),\end{split}

so after applying Lemma
12.2
we conclude that

\begin{split}\Bigg|LT^{K}\check{\phi}^{(2)}_{2}&-2I_{2}[\psi](v-u)^{4}L^{K}(v^{-6})+\frac{40}{3}\sqrt{\frac{\pi}{5}}a^{2}I_{0}[\psi]Y_{2,0}(v-u)^{4}L^{K}(v^{-6})\\
&+\frac{8}{3}\sqrt{\frac{\pi}{5}}a^{2}Y_{2,0}I_{0}[\psi]T^{K}\left(-6(v-u)^{3}v^{-5}+\frac{3}{2}(v-u)^{2}v^{-4}\right)\Bigg|\\
\leq&\>C(u^{-1+\delta}r^{-2-K}+u^{-1-K+\delta}r^{-2}+r^{4}v^{-(8K+2\delta)\alpha_{K}+1})S_{2,\delta,K}[\psi]+CD_{2,\beta,K}[\psi]r^{4}v^{-6-K-\beta}.\end{split}

∎

Proposition 12.5
.

For
\alpha_{K}^{\prime\prime\prime}
suitably close to 1,
\delta>0
suitably small and
0<\beta\leq 1
suitably large, there exists
\nu>0
and a constant
C=C(M,a,\alpha_{K}^{\prime\prime\prime},\nu,\delta)>0
such that

\begin{split}&\Bigg|T^{K}\psi_{2}(u,v,\theta,\varphi_{*})-\frac{4}{15}I_{2}[\psi](\theta,\varphi_{*})(v-u)^{2}T^{K}(u^{-3}v^{-3})\\
&+\frac{64}{3}\sqrt{\frac{\pi}{5}}a^{2}I_{0}[\psi]Y_{2,0}(\theta)T^{K}\Bigg[\frac{1}{12}u^{-3}v^{-3}(v-u)^{2}-\frac{1}{4}u^{-3}v^{-2}(v-u)+\frac{1}{4}u^{-3}v^{-1}\Bigg]\Bigg|\\
&\leq Cu^{-3-K-\nu}v^{-1}(S_{2,\delta,K}[\psi]+D_{2,\beta,K}[\psi]+\sum_{m=-2}^{2}|I_{2m}[\psi]|)+C\sqrt{E_{2,K,\delta}[\psi]}u^{-4-K-\nu}\end{split}

(12.12)

in
\mathcal{A}_{\gamma^{\alpha_{K}^{\prime\prime\prime}}}
and along
\mathcal{I}^{+}
:

\displaystyle\Bigg|T^{K}\phi_{2}|_{\mathcal{I}^{+}}

\displaystyle-\frac{2}{15}I_{2}[\psi]T^{K}(u^{-3})+\frac{8}{9}\sqrt{\frac{\pi}{5}}a^{2}I_{0}[\psi]Y_{2,0}(\theta)T^{K}(u^{-3})\Bigg|

(12.13)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\>Cu^{-3-K-\nu}\Big(S_{2,\delta,K}[\psi]+D_{2,\beta,K}[\psi]+\sum_{m=-2}^{2}|I_{2m}[\psi]|+\sqrt{E_{2,K,\delta}[\psi]}\Big),

(12.14)

\displaystyle\Bigg|T^{K}\check{\phi}^{(1)}_{2}|_{\mathcal{I}^{+}}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{5}I_{2}[\psi]T^{K}(u^{-2})\Bigg|

(12.15)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\>Cu^{-2-K-\nu}\Bigg(S_{2,\delta,K}[\psi]+D_{2,\beta,K}[\psi]+\sum_{m=-2}^{2}|I_{2m}[\psi]|+\sqrt{E_{2,K,\delta}[\psi]}\Bigg),

(12.16)

\displaystyle\Bigg|T^{K}\check{\phi}^{(2)}_{2}|_{\mathcal{I}^{+}}

\displaystyle-\frac{2}{5}I_{2}[\psi]T^{K}(u^{-1})\Bigg|

(12.17)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\>Cu^{-1-K-\nu}\Bigg(S_{2,\delta,K}[\psi]+D_{2,\beta,K}[\psi]+\sum_{m=-2}^{2}|I_{2m}[\psi]|+\sqrt{E_{2,K,\delta}[\psi]}\Bigg).

(12.18)

In particular,

\begin{split}\Bigg|T^{K}\psi_{2}|_{\gamma^{\alpha_{K}^{\prime\prime}}}&+\frac{16}{3}\sqrt{\frac{\pi}{5}}a^{2}I_{0}[\psi]Y_{2,0}(\theta)T^{K}((1+\tau)^{-4})\Bigg|\\
\leq&C(1+\tau)^{-4-K-\nu}\left(S_{2,\delta,K}[\psi]+D_{2,\beta,K}[\psi]+\sum_{m=-2}^{2}|I_{2m}[\psi]|+\sqrt{E_{2,K,\delta}[\psi]}\right).\end{split}

(12.19)

Proof.

Note first of all that (
11.18
) holds also with
I_{1}[\psi]
replaced by
I_{2}[\psi]
.

We then integrate (
12.11
) in the
L
direction, starting from
\gamma^{\alpha_{K}}
and use (
8.15
) to obtain:

\begin{split}|T^{K}\check{\phi}^{(2)}_{2}|(u,v_{\gamma^{\alpha_{K}}(u)},\theta,\varphi_{*})\leq&\>Cr^{\frac{1}{2}(1+\delta^{\prime})}(u,v_{\gamma^{\alpha_{K}}(u)})u^{-\frac{3}{2}-K+2\delta^{\prime}}\sqrt{E_{2,K,\delta^{\prime}}[\psi]}\\
\leq&\>C\sqrt{E_{2,K,\delta^{\prime}}[\psi]}u^{-\frac{3}{2}+\frac{1}{2}\alpha_{K}-K+2\delta^{\prime}},\end{split}

for
\delta^{\prime}>0
arbitrarily small, together with (
11.6
), (
11.17
) and
0<\alpha_{K}<1
suitably large, to obtain:

\begin{split}&\Bigg|T^{K}\check{\phi}^{(2)}_{2}(u,v,\theta,\varphi_{*})-\frac{2}{5}I_{2}[\psi](\theta,\varphi_{*})(v-u)^{5}T^{K}(u^{-1}v^{-5})\\
&+\frac{8}{3}\sqrt{\frac{\pi}{5}}a^{2}I_{0}[\psi]Y_{2,0}(\theta)T^{K}\Bigg[u^{-1}v^{-5}(v-u)^{5}-\frac{3}{2}u^{-1}v^{-4}(v-u)^{4}+\frac{1}{2}u^{-1}v^{-3}(v-u)^{3}\Bigg]\Bigg|\\
\leq&\>Cu^{-1-K-\nu}(v-u)^{3}v^{-3}(S_{2,\delta,K}[\psi]+D_{2,\beta,K}[\psi]+\sum_{m=-2}^{2}|I_{2m}[\psi]|)+C\sqrt{E_{2,K,\delta}[\psi]}u^{-1-K-\nu},\end{split}

for some
\nu>0
, with
\delta>0
suitably small. In particular, (
12.17
) follows.

Since
T^{K}\check{\phi}_{2}^{(2)}=2(r^{2}+O(r))LT^{K}\check{\phi}_{2}^{(1)}+KO(r^{-1})T^{K-1}\check{\phi}_{2}^{(1)}
, we can integrate the above equation once more, starting from
\gamma^{\alpha_{K}^{\prime}}
, with
\alpha_{K}^{\prime}>\alpha_{K}
suitably large (depending on
\nu
above), using (
8.15
) again to estimate:

\begin{split}|T^{K}\check{\phi}^{(1)}_{2}|(u,v_{\gamma^{\alpha_{K}}(u)},\theta,\varphi)\leq&\>C\sqrt{E_{2,K,\delta^{\prime}}[\psi]}u^{-\frac{5}{2}+\frac{1}{2}\alpha_{K}-K+2\delta^{\prime}},\end{split}

for
\delta^{\prime}>0
and obtain:

\begin{split}&\Bigg|T^{K}\check{\phi}^{(1)}_{2}-\frac{1}{5}I_{2}[\psi](v-u)^{4}T^{K}(u^{-2}v^{-4})\\
&+\frac{16}{3}\sqrt{\frac{\pi}{5}}a^{2}I_{0}[\psi]Y_{2,0}T^{K}\Bigg[\frac{1}{4}u^{-2}v^{-4}(v-u)^{4}-\frac{1}{2}u^{-2}v^{-3}(v-u)^{3}+\frac{1}{4}u^{-2}v^{-2}(v-u)^{2}\Bigg]\Bigg|\\
&\>\leq Cu^{-2-K-\nu}(v-u)^{2}v^{-2}(S_{2,\delta,K}[\psi]+D_{2,\beta,K}[\psi]+\sum_{m=-2}^{2}|I_{2m}[\psi]|)+C\sqrt{E_{2,K,\delta}[\psi]}u^{-2-K-\nu}\end{split}

in
\mathcal{A}_{\gamma^{\alpha_{K}^{\prime}}}
with
\delta>0
suitably small. In particular, (
12.15
) follows.

Using that
T^{K}\check{\phi}_{2}^{(1)}=2(r^{2}+O(r))L{\phi}_{2}+KO(r^{-1})T^{K-1}\phi_{2}
and for
\alpha_{K}^{\prime}<\alpha_{K}^{\prime\prime}<1
(
8.15
) implies that

\begin{split}|T^{K}\phi_{2}|(u,v_{\gamma^{\alpha_{K}^{\prime\prime}}(u)},\theta,\varphi)\leq&\>C\sqrt{E_{2,K,\delta^{\prime}}[\psi]}u^{-\frac{7}{2}+\frac{1}{2}\alpha_{K}^{\prime\prime}-K+2\delta^{\prime}},\end{split}

we integrate a final time starting from
\gamma^{\alpha_{K}^{\prime\prime}}
, with
\alpha_{K}^{\prime\prime}>\alpha_{K}^{\prime\prime}
suitably large and
\delta^{\prime}>0
correspondingly small, to obtain:

\begin{split}&\Bigg|T^{K}\phi_{2}-\frac{2}{15}I_{2}[\psi](v-u)^{3}T^{K}(u^{-3}v^{-3})+\frac{32}{3}\sqrt{\frac{\pi}{5}}a^{2}I_{0}[\psi]Y_{2,0}T^{K}\Bigg[\frac{1}{12}u^{-3}v^{-3}(v-u)^{3}-\frac{1}{4}u^{-3}v^{-2}(v-u)^{2}\\
&+\frac{1}{4}u^{-3}v^{-1}(v-u)\Bigg]\Bigg|\\
&\leq Cu^{-3-K-\nu}(v-u)v^{-1}(S_{2,\delta,K}[\psi]+D_{2,\beta,K}[\psi]+\sum_{m=-2}^{2}|I_{2m}[\psi]|)+C\sqrt{E_{2,K,\delta}[\psi]}u^{-3-K-\nu}\end{split}

in
\mathcal{A}_{\gamma^{\alpha_{K}^{\prime\prime}}}
. We obtain in particular (
12.13
).

The estimate (
12.12
) then follows by restricting to a smaller region
\mathcal{A}_{\gamma^{\alpha_{K}^{\prime\prime\prime}}}
, with
\alpha_{K}^{\prime\prime\prime}>\alpha_{K}^{\prime\prime}
suitably large depending on
\nu
and dividing the equation above by
r
. The estimate
(
12.19
) follows directly.
∎

12.2
Asymptotics in
\mathcal{R}\setminus\mathcal{A}_{\gamma^{\alpha}}

We extend now the late-time asymptotics derived in the region
\mathcal{A}_{\gamma^{\alpha}}
in Proposition
12.5
to the rest of the spacetime.

Proposition 12.6
.

Let
K\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
and let
\alpha>0
be arbitrarily large. Then there exists a constant
C=C(M,a,K,\delta)>0
such that

\begin{split}\Bigg|T^{K}\psi_{2}&-\frac{4}{3}\sqrt{\frac{\pi}{5}}a^{2}I_{0}[\psi]Y_{2,0}(\theta)T^{K}((1+\tau)^{-4})\Bigg|(\tau,\uprho,\theta,\varphi_{*})\\
\leq&\>C(1+\tau)^{-4-K-\nu}\left(S_{2,\delta,K}[\psi]+D_{2,\beta,K}[\psi]+\sum_{m=-2}^{2}|I_{2m}[\psi]|\right)\\
&+C(1+\tau)^{-4-K-\nu}\Bigg[\sqrt{E_{2,K+7,\delta}[\psi]}+\sum_{j=0}^{3}\sqrt{E_{2,K+4,\delta}[N^{j}\psi]}+\sqrt{E_{0,K+7,\delta}[\psi]}\\
&+\sum_{j=0}^{3}\sqrt{E_{0,K+4,\delta}[N^{j}\psi]}+\sqrt{E_{\geq 3,K+2,\delta}[\psi]}+\sum_{j=0}^{1}\sqrt{E_{\geq 3,K+2,\delta}[N^{j}\psi]}\Bigg].\end{split}

(12.20)

for all
(\tau,\uprho,\theta,\varphi_{*})\in\mathcal{R}\setminus\mathcal{A}_{\gamma^{\alpha}}
and
\delta>0
suitably small.

Proof.

First of all, by (
8.16
), we have that:

\begin{split}|XT^{K}\psi_{2}|_{\gamma^{\alpha}(\tau)}|(\tau,\theta,\varphi_{*})\leq&\>C(1+\tau)^{-4-\alpha-K+2\delta}\Bigg(\sqrt{E_{2,K+5,\delta}[\psi]+\sum_{j=0}^{2}E_{2,K+3,\delta}[N^{j}\psi]}\\
&+\sqrt{E_{0,K+5,\delta}[\psi]+\sum_{j=0}^{2}E_{0,K+3,\delta}[N^{j}\psi]\Bigg)}.\end{split}

By (
8.24
), we moreover have that

\begin{split}|rX^{2}T^{K}\psi_{2}|(\tau,\uprho,\theta,\varphi_{*})\leq&\>C(1+\tau)^{-5-K+2\delta}\Bigg[\sqrt{E_{2,K+7,\delta}[\psi]}+\sum_{j=0}^{3}\sqrt{E_{2,K+4,\delta}[N^{j}\psi]}+\sqrt{E_{0,K+7,\delta}[\psi]}\\
&+\sum_{j=0}^{3}\sqrt{E_{0,K+4,\delta}[N^{j}\psi]}+\sqrt{E_{\geq 3,K+2,\delta}[\psi]}+\sum_{j=0}^{1}\sqrt{E_{\geq 3,K+2,\delta}[N^{j}\psi]}\Bigg].\end{split}

We apply the fundamental theorem of calculus, integrating
X^{2}T^{K}\psi_{2}
between
\uprho=\uprho^{\prime}
and
\uprho=\uprho_{\gamma^{\alpha}(\tau)}
, together with the estimates above to conclude that there exists a
\nu>0
such that

\begin{split}|T^{K}X\psi_{2}|(\tau,\uprho,\theta,\varphi_{*})\leq&\>C(1+\tau)^{-4-K-\nu}\Bigg[\sqrt{E_{2,K+7,\delta}[\psi]}+\sum_{j=0}^{3}\sqrt{E_{2,K+4,\delta}[N^{j}\psi]}+\sqrt{E_{0,K+7,\delta}[\psi]}\\
&+\sum_{j=0}^{3}\sqrt{E_{0,K+4,\delta}[N^{j}\psi]}+\sqrt{E_{\geq 3,K+2,\delta}[\psi]}+\sum_{j=0}^{1}\sqrt{E_{\geq 3,K+2,\delta}[N^{j}\psi]}\Bigg].\end{split}

(12.21)

We conclude the proof by applying the fundamental theorem of calculus again, integrating now
XT^{K}\psi_{2}
between
\uprho=\uprho^{\prime}
and
\uprho=\uprho_{\gamma^{\alpha}(\tau)}
. The corresponding boundary term at
\uprho=\uprho_{\gamma^{\alpha}(\tau)}
can be estimated by (
12.19
) and we estimate the integral term with (
12.21
). Taking
\alpha
suitably large, we arrive at (
12.20
).
∎

12.3
Asymptotics with vanishing Newman–Penrose charges

We apply here the time-integral construction from Section
9
to obtain the late-time asymptotics for
\psi_{2}
arising from initial data that is smooth and compactly supported.

Proposition 12.7
.

Consider initial data
(\psi|_{\Sigma_{0}},T\psi|_{\Sigma_{0}})
for (
3.1
), with
(\phi|_{\Sigma_{0}},T\phi_{\Sigma_{0}})\in(C_{c}^{\infty}(\Sigma))^{2}
. Let
r_{0}>r_{+}
. Then there exists a
\nu>0
and a constant
C=C(M,a,\Sigma_{0},r_{0},\nu)>0
, such that

\begin{split}&\Bigg|T^{K}\psi_{2}(u,v,\theta,\varphi_{*})-\frac{4}{15}I_{2}[T^{-1}\psi](\theta,\varphi_{*})(v-u)^{2}T^{K+1}(u^{-3}v^{-3})\\
&+\frac{64}{3}\sqrt{\frac{\pi}{5}}a^{2}I_{0}[T^{-1}\psi]Y_{2,0}(\theta)T^{K+1}\Bigg[\frac{1}{12}u^{-3}v^{-3}(v-u)^{2}-\frac{1}{4}u^{-3}v^{-2}(v-u)+\frac{1}{4}u^{-3}v^{-1}\Bigg]\Bigg|\\
\leq&\>Cu^{-4-K-\nu}v^{-1}(S_{2,\delta,K+1}[T^{-1}\psi]+D_{2,\beta,K+1}[T^{-1}\psi]+\sum_{m=-2}^{2}|I_{2m}[T^{-1}\psi]|)\\
&+Cu^{-4-K-\nu}v^{-1}\Bigg[\sqrt{E_{2,K+8,\delta}[T^{-1}\psi]}+\sum_{j=0}^{3}\sqrt{E_{2,K+5,\delta}[N^{j}T^{-1}\psi]}+\sqrt{E_{0,K+8,\delta}[T^{-1}\psi]}\\
&+\sum_{j=0}^{3}\sqrt{E_{0,K+5,\delta}[N^{j}\psi]}+\sqrt{E_{\geq 3,K+3,\delta}[T^{-1}\psi]}+\sum_{j=0}^{1}\sqrt{E_{\geq 3,K+3,\delta}[N^{j}T^{-1}\psi]}\Bigg]\end{split}

in
\{r\geq r_{0}\}
.

In particular,

\begin{split}\Bigg|T^{K}\phi_{2}|_{\mathcal{I}^{+}}(u,\theta,\varphi_{*})&+\frac{2}{5}I_{2}[T^{-1}\psi](\theta,\varphi_{*})T^{K}(u^{-4})-\frac{8}{3}\sqrt{\frac{\pi}{5}}a^{2}I_{0}[T^{-1}\psi]Y_{2,0}(\theta)T^{K}(u^{-4})\Bigg|\\
\leq&\>Cu^{-4-K-\nu}\Big(S_{2,\delta,K+1}[T^{-1}\psi]+D_{2,\beta,K+1}[T^{-1}\psi]+\sum_{m=-2}^{2}|I_{2m}[T^{-1}\psi]|+\sqrt{E_{2,K+1,\delta}[T^{-1}\psi]}\Big).\end{split}

In
\{r\leq r_{0}\}
, we can express:

\begin{split}&\Bigg|T^{K}\psi_{2}+\frac{16}{3}\sqrt{\frac{\pi}{5}}a^{2}I_{0}[T^{-1}\psi]Y_{2,0}(\theta)T^{K}((1+\tau)^{-5})\Bigg|(\tau,\uprho,\theta,\varphi_{*})\\
\leq&\>C(1+\tau)^{-5-K-\nu}(S_{2,\delta,K+1}[T^{-1}\psi]+D_{2,\beta,K+1}[T^{-1}\psi]+\sum_{m=-2}^{2}|I_{2m}[T^{-1}\psi]|)\\
&+C(1+\tau)^{-5-K-\nu}\Bigg[\sqrt{E_{2,K+8,\delta}[T^{-1}\psi]}+\sum_{j=0}^{3}\sqrt{E_{2,K+5,\delta}[N^{j}T^{-1}\psi]}+\sqrt{E_{0,K+8,\delta}[T^{-1}\psi]}\\
&+\sum_{j=0}^{3}\sqrt{E_{0,K+5,\delta}[N^{j}\psi]}+\sqrt{E_{\geq 3,K+3,\delta}[T^{-1}\psi]}+\sum_{j=0}^{1}\sqrt{E_{\geq 3,K+3,\delta}[N^{j}T^{-1}\psi]}\Bigg].\end{split}

Proof.

We apply Propositions
9.9
and
9.11
to conclude that
T^{-1}\phi
has sufficiently high regularity to conclude that all the relevant energies for
T^{-1}\psi
are finite and
I_{2}[T^{-1}\psi]
is well-defined. Then the estimates follows immediately by applying the estimates in Propositions
12.5
and
12.6
to
T^{-1}\psi
instead of
\psi
.
∎

Appendix A
Weighted pointwise estimates

We derive in this section a lemma which is convenient for turning weighted energy estimates into pointwise estimates.

Lemma A.1
.

Let
h,f:\mathcal{R}\to\mathbb{R}
be
C^{1}
functions, such that
\lim_{\uprho\to\infty}h(\tau,\uprho,\theta,\varphi_{*})=0
. Let
k\in\mathbb{R}_{>0}
and
\delta>0
arbitrarily small. Let
k\geq 0
, then there exists
C=C(M,a,R,k,\delta)>0
, such that

\displaystyle\int_{S^{2}_{\tau^{\prime},\uprho^{\prime}}}f^{2}\,d\omega\leq

\displaystyle\>C\int_{N_{\tau^{\prime}}}r^{1+\delta}(Lf)^{2}+r^{-3+\delta}[(Tf)^{2}+(\Phi f)^{2}]\,d\omega d\uprho+\int_{\Sigma_{\tau^{\prime}}\cap\{R-M\leq r\leq R\}}f^{2}\,d\omega d\uprho\>\>\textnormal{if $\uprho^{\prime}>R$},

(A.1)

\displaystyle\int_{S^{2}_{\tau^{\prime},\uprho^{\prime}}}\uprho^{\prime-1-\delta}f^{2}\,d\omega\leq

\displaystyle\>C\int_{N_{\tau^{\prime}}}r^{-\delta}(Lf)^{2}+r^{-4-\delta}[(Tf)^{2}+(\Phi f)^{2}]\,d\omega d\uprho+\int_{\Sigma_{\tau^{\prime}}\cap\{R-M\leq r\leq R\}}f^{2}\,d\omega d\uprho\>\>\textnormal{if $\uprho^{\prime}>R$},

(A.2)

\displaystyle\int_{S^{2}_{\tau^{\prime},\uprho^{\prime}}}\uprho^{\prime}h^{2}\,d\omega\leq

\displaystyle\>C\int_{\Sigma_{\tau^{\prime}}}J^{N}[h]\cdot\mathbf{n}_{\tau}\,r^{2}d\omega d\uprho,

(A.3)

\displaystyle\int_{S^{2}_{\tau^{\prime},\uprho^{\prime}}}h^{2}\,d\omega\leq

\displaystyle\>C\sqrt{\int_{\Sigma_{\tau^{\prime}}}r^{-2}J^{N}[h]\cdot\mathbf{n}_{\tau}\,r^{2}d\omega d\uprho}\cdot\sqrt{\int_{\Sigma_{\tau^{\prime}}}J^{N}[h]\cdot\mathbf{n}_{\tau}\,r^{2}d\omega d\uprho},

(A.4)

\displaystyle\int_{S^{2}_{\tau^{\prime},\uprho^{\prime}}}{\uprho^{\prime}}^{-2k}h^{2}\,d\omega\leq

\displaystyle\>C\int_{\Sigma_{\tau^{\prime}}}\left(r^{-2k-1}J^{N}[h]\cdot\mathbf{n}_{\tau}+r^{-2k-3}h^{2}\right)\,r^{2}d\omega d\uprho.

(A.5)

Proof.

Let
R_{0}>r_{+}+M
and let
\chi:[r_{+},\infty)\to\mathbb{R}
be a smooth cut-off function, such that
\chi(r)=1
for all
r\geq R_{0}
and
\chi=0
for
r\leq R_{0}-M
.

By applying the fundamental theorem of calculus, integrating from
\uprho=R_{0}-M
, together with Cauchy–Schwarz and (
2.20
), we obtain for
\uprho^{\prime}\geq R_{0}
:

\begin{split}f^{2}(\uprho^{\prime},\theta,\varphi_{*},\tau^{\prime})=&\>\int_{R_{0}-M}^{\uprho^{\prime}}2\chi fX(\chi f)\,d\uprho\Big|_{\tau=\tau^{\prime}}\\
\leq&\>\sqrt{\int_{R_{0}-M}^{\uprho^{\prime}}r^{-1-\delta}(\chi f)^{2}\,d\uprho}\cdot\sqrt{\int_{R_{0}-M}^{\uprho^{\prime}}r^{1+\delta}(X(\chi f))^{2}\,d\uprho}\Big|_{\tau=\tau^{\prime}}\\
\leq&\>C\sqrt{\int_{R_{0}-M}^{\uprho^{\prime}}r^{1+\delta}(Xf)^{2}\,d\uprho}\cdot\sqrt{\int_{R_{0}-M}^{\uprho^{\prime}}r^{1-\delta}(Xf)^{2}\,d\uprho}\Big|_{\tau=\tau^{\prime}}\\
\leq&\>C\int_{R_{0}-M}^{\uprho^{\prime}}r^{1+\delta}(Lf)^{2}+r^{-3+\delta}[(Tf)^{2}+(\Phi f)^{2}]\,d\uprho+C\int_{R_{0}-M}^{R_{0}}f^{2}\,d\uprho\Big|_{\tau=\tau^{\prime}}.\end{split}

The estimate (
A.1
) then follows by integrating over
\mathbb{S}^{2}
, choosing
R_{0}
appropriately.

We similarly obtain

\begin{split}\uprho^{\prime-1-\delta}f^{2}(\uprho^{\prime},\theta,\varphi_{*},\tau^{\prime})=&\>\int_{R_{0}-M}^{\uprho^{\prime}}-(1+\delta)r^{-2-\delta}(\chi f)^{2}+2r^{-1-\delta}\chi fX(\chi f)\,d\uprho\Big|_{\tau=\tau^{\prime}}\\
\leq&\>\int_{R_{0}-M}^{\uprho^{\prime}}-\left(\frac{1}{2}+\delta\right)r^{-2}(\chi f)^{2}+2r^{-\delta}(X(\chi f))^{2}\,d\uprho\Big|_{\tau=\tau^{\prime}}\\
\leq&\>C\int_{R_{0}-M}^{\uprho^{\prime}}r^{-\delta}(Lf)^{2}+r^{-4-\delta}[(Tf)^{2}+(\Phi f)^{2}]\,d\uprho+C\int_{R_{0}-M}^{R_{0}}f^{2}\,d\uprho\Big|_{\tau=\tau^{\prime}}.\end{split}

We apply the fundamental theorem of calculus again to obtain:

\begin{split}h^{2}(\uprho^{\prime},\theta,\varphi_{*},\tau^{\prime})=&\left(0-\int_{\uprho^{\prime}}^{\infty}X(h)\,d\uprho\right)^{2}\Big|_{\tau=\tau^{\prime}}\leq\rho^{\prime-1}\int_{\rho^{\prime}}^{\infty}r^{2}(Xh)^{2}\,d\uprho\Big|_{\tau=\tau^{\prime}}.\end{split}

The estimate (
A.3
) then follows by integrating over
\mathbb{S}^{2}
and applying Cauchy–Schwarz again on
\mathbb{S}^{2}
.

By applying the fundamental theorem of calculus, integrating from
\uprho=\infty
, in combination with Cauchy–Schwarz and (
2.20
), we obtain

\begin{split}h^{2}(\uprho^{\prime},\theta,\varphi_{*},\tau^{\prime})=&\>0-\int_{\uprho^{\prime}}^{\infty}2hXh\,d\uprho^{\prime}\\
\leq&\>C\sqrt{\int_{\uprho^{\prime}}^{\infty}h^{2}\,d\uprho}\sqrt{\int_{\uprho^{\prime}}^{\infty}(Xh)^{2}\,d\uprho}\\
\leq&\>C\sqrt{\int_{\uprho^{\prime}}^{\infty}r^{2}(Xh)^{2}\,d\uprho}\sqrt{\int_{R_{0}-M}^{\uprho^{\prime}}(Xh)^{2}\,d\uprho}.\end{split}

We obtain (
A.4
) by integrating over
\mathbb{S}^{2}
.

Finally, we repeat the above application of the fundamental theorem of calculus, integrating from
\uprho=\infty
to obtain

\begin{split}r^{-2k}h_{\geq 1}^{2}(\uprho,\theta,\varphi_{*},\tau^{\prime})=&\>0-\int_{\uprho^{\prime}}^{\infty}X(r^{-2k}h_{\geq 1}^{2})\,d\uprho\\
\leq&\>C\int_{\uprho^{\prime}}^{\infty}r^{-2k-1}h_{\geq 1}^{2}+r^{-2k+1}(Xh_{\geq 1})^{2}\,d\uprho\end{split}

We then integrate over
\mathbb{S}^{2}
and apply (
2.15
) to obtain (
A.4
).
∎

Appendix B
A basic interpolation inequality

The lemma below is useful for interpolating between
r
- and
\tau
-decay.

Lemma B.1
.

Let
f:\mathcal{R}\to\mathbb{R}
be a continuous function, such that for
q_{1},q_{2}\in\mathbb{R}
with
0\leq q_{1}\leq q_{2}
and
r_{+}<r_{1}<r_{2}\leq\infty
:

\displaystyle\int_{r_{1}}^{r_{2}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}r^{q_{1}}f^{2}(\tau,\uprho,\theta,\varphi_{*})\,d\omega d\uprho\leq D_{1}(1+\tau)^{-p},

(B.1)

\displaystyle\int_{r_{1}}^{r_{2}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}r^{q_{2}}f^{2}(\tau,\uprho,\theta,\varphi_{*})\,d\omega d\uprho\leq D_{2}(1+\tau)^{-p+(q_{2}-q_{1})}.

(B.2)

Then for all
q_{1}\leq q\leq q_{2}
:

\int_{r_{1}}^{r_{2}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}r^{q}f^{2}(\tau,\uprho,\theta,\varphi_{*})\,d\omega d\uprho\leq(D_{1}+D_{2})(1+\tau)^{-p+(q-q_{1})}.

(B.3)

Proof.

We split
[r_{1},r_{2}]=J_{\leq}+J_{>}
, with
J_{\leq}=[r_{1},r_{2}]\times\mathbb{S}^{2}\cap\{r\leq(1+\tau)\}
and
J_{>}=[r_{1},r_{2}]\times\mathbb{S}^{2}\cap\{r>(1+\tau)\}
. Then by applying (
B.1
) and (
B.2
), we obtain

\begin{split}\int_{r_{1}}^{r_{2}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}r^{q}f^{2}\,d\omega d\uprho=&\>\int_{J_{\leq}}r^{q}f^{2}\,d\omega d\uprho+\int_{J_{>}}r^{q}f^{2}\,d\omega d\uprho\\
\leq&\>(1+\tau)^{q-q_{1}}\int_{J_{\leq}}r^{q_{1}}f^{2}\,d\omega d\uprho+(1+\tau)^{-(q_{2}-q)}\int_{J_{>}}r^{q_{2}}f^{2}\,d\omega d\uprho\\
\leq&\>D_{1}(1+\tau)^{-p+(q-q_{1})}+D_{2}(1+\tau)^{-(q_{2}-q)-p+(q_{2}-q_{1})},\end{split}

from which (
B.3
) immediately follows.
∎
